# LITTLEBIG: decisions

One line per deviation from BRIEF.md or contract change, with the reason. Newest at the bottom of
each section. Tag lines with the task id.

## Foundation (F0)

- [F0] World axes: planet at the origin, +Y = north pole, (lat 0, lon 0) = +Z; heading = compass radians clockwise from north. One convention for lat/lon, headings and charts (world/sphere.ts).
- [F0] City plan space: +x = east, +z = south at the centre, so (x, up, z) is right-handed and "right of (dx, dz)" is (−dz, dx). Plan angles increase clockwise seen from above; every outline uses positive winding (Σ x_i·z_{i+1} − x_{i+1}·z_i > 0). Documented in world/city/types.ts.
- [F0] The city chart's exponential map measures arc length on the plateau surface (R + 2 = 162 m), not sea level, so plan metres are surface metres. Circumferential distances shrink by sin θ/θ (−5 % at the plateau edge); collision and validation use plan space consistently, so nothing clashes.
- [F0] City at lat 20°, lon 10°. Off the poles (no frame flips) and off the equator.
- [F0] The planet does not spin; the sun circles it westward (world/sun.ts). Same look, simpler shadows/caches, and every lit position stays fixed.
- [F0] Tiny-planet consequence: local sun elevation changes ~1° per 2.8 m, so the city spans ~30° of "time of day" edge to edge. Shots that need a sun angle (dusk) compute the time at their own viewpoint (`eveningTimeAt`), not at the city centre.
- [F0] Plateau blend 0.2 rad (≈32 m) plus a land guard ring 0.3 rad beyond it: the plateau always sits on land, coast 30-60 m past the blend, slope across the edge < 0.7 (spec'd). The first draft put the sea right at the plateau edge (a 10 m drop).
- [F0] Coastline is continuous (height 0 at the waterline from both sides); the first draft had a 0.5 m step there.
- [F0] Camera state stores the heading as a tangent vector (`fwd`), not an angle: no gimbal flip over the poles. `heading` is derived. Drag-look yaw turns `fwd` directly, so the view direction survives lifting off from street level; `lookYaw` stays in the state (contract) but core no longer accumulates it.
- [F0] `ViewSpec` (setView) takes degrees; internal math is radians. `pitch` in a ViewSpec is an override that holds until the user interacts, then blends back to the altitude curve.
- [F0] `setView(v, { glide })` (additive): scripted sequences (the dive, C1's clip) let the ground/roof reference spring between frames instead of snapping, so crossing a building does not jolt the camera.
- [F0] (Superseded in the fix round, see below: roofs are no longer part of the altitude reference.)
- [F0] Pitch curve: −90° above 120 m, −8° at 4 m, −4° at eye height (BRIEF said ~−8° at 4 m; at 1.7 m −4° keeps the horizon just below centre). FOV 40° → 70° in log-altitude. Spec checks the horizon is in frame below ~25 m. (Made C1 in the fix round.)
- [F0] Shared fill light lives in the toon shader (planet-aware hemisphere: up = normalised world position), not a three HemisphereLight (whose up is a fixed world axis, wrong on a sphere). Fill values are ×π-scaled because three divides indirect diffuse by π.
- [F0] Tone mapping: NeutralToneMapping (Khronos PBR Neutral). Keeps the saturated palette's hues; ACES desaturated and darkened it. B4 may change it.
- [F0] Toon ramp is an R8 64-px linear texture with soft band edges (RAMP_STOPS in render/toon.ts); the night side gets no direct light, only the fill.
- [F0] Reveal = per-instance `aReveal` delay (or a per-material delay) against one shared clock uniform; the spring is in the vertex shader (geometry grows from its local origin), and the shadow depth twin gets the same code. Reduced motion dither-fades instead (no transparency, no sorting).
- [F0] Shader warm-up: `renderer.compileAsync` (KHR_parallel_shader_compile) kicked as soon as the sky exists, with a warm mesh for the shared toon variant and the shadow-pass depth program, so compiles overlap terrain generation. Cold first-visit compile was 1.3 s blocking; now ~0.25-0.5 s in parallel. (Fix round: the depth warm-up now matches the shadow pass's program key; see below.)
- [F0] Terrain and ocean share one pass of heightAt over the icosphere (world/ico-heights.ts, cached per planet + detail).
- [F0] Terrain detail 6 on both tiers (2.6 m facets: crisp at orbit, chunky at street level). Detail 7 quadruples boot cost (~250 ms); A1 decides.
- [F0] Sky dome, stars and the space gradient: the sky is measured as "angle above the planet's limb", so it works from orbit (where every background ray is just above the limb) to the street. (The altitude-only space blend went grey/lavender mid-dive; replaced by the per-ray air model in the fix round.) Stars draw at the far plane with depth test so the planet hides them. The rim glow is a back-faced additive shell (R + 46 m), faded in only above ~110 m where it reads against dark space.
- [F0] The site's CRT scanline overlay (body::after) is hidden while a LITTLEBIG canvas is on the page (app/globals.css), as for GOLDENLINE: it banded the sky gradients.
- [F0] `perf()` is a serial measurement (update + render + 1-px readPixels to sync the GPU). In headless Chromium the harness itself costs ~5-6 ms per frame, so the result includes `baseline` (an empty scene measured the same way) and `net` = median − baseline. Compare `net` against budgets, or measure in a real browser with the rAF stats.
- [F0] `?cold` (shot tool `--cold`) salts every shader's output so the macOS Metal shader cache misses: it reproduces a first visit's compile cost. Without it, repeat runs on the same machine report warm-cache boots.
- [F0] The stub city (world/city/stub.ts) is built with a reusable road-graph builder (world/city/graph.ts: trimmed centrelines, lanes, connectors with conflicts, rounded intersection outlines, sidewalk/corner/crossing walk graph). A2 may keep using the builder or replace it; validate.ts is the invariant checker for whatever plan ships.
- [F0] Quality tiers fixed per session (`low`: DPR ≤ 1.5, 1024 shadow map, no MSAA, density 0.45, no expensive post). Switching tiers at runtime would recompile programs mid-flow.

## F0 fix round (after the foundation review)

- [F0] `ctx.compile()` (additive): compiles the colour AND shadow-depth programs of what a stage-2 system just added, with those roots hidden until done; the order is build → add → compile → `reveal.slot()`. Core compiles after `init` if a system forgets. Shadow programs are warmed through proxies drawn with a render target bound and no scene fog, which is how three's shadow pass keys them (warming against the canvas, or with fog, built the wrong program and the real one compiled inside the first frame: the old 90-150 ms "shadow first draw").
- [F0] Reveal duration is a uniform (`lbRevealDur`), not a define, and fade ranges too: durations and ranges never fork programs. Program key = reveal mode + reduced motion + fade mode + patch key.
- [F0] Dev guards: a program compiled inside the first frame, or by any frame after a stage-2 system is initialised, logs a console warning naming it.
- [F0] Dispose during a stage-2 build: `ctx.yield()` and `ctx.compile()` never settle once disposed, the stage-2 loop checks `disposed` after every await, and a system whose init finishes late gets `dispose()`. Shot tool `--leak N --close-at 50,150,400,700,ready`: no timer growth, no listener growth, every renderer garbage-collected.
- [F0] Stage-1 init order: the sky first (it makes the lights and fog every lit program's key depends on), so the warm-up starts ~20 ms in; the city plan is built while it compiles. Update order is unchanged (camera first). Boot marks split `planet` / `city plan`.
- [F0] City plan budget for A2: plan + index ≤ 30 ms on the M3 (first-frame path). The stub takes 33-49 ms in the browser, mostly all-pairs placement tests; A2's builder must use the grid.
- [F0] Time-slice budget follows the display: max(2, min(8, rAF interval − last frame CPU − 2)) ms, instead of a fixed 7 ms that dropped frames at 120 Hz.
- [F0] `System.resize?(ctx, w, h, dpr)` (additive), called for initialised systems before the redraw.
- [F0] `step(dt, n)` reports `time.dt` = n·dt and `realDt` = n·dt to the single update it runs; visuals should derive from `time.render` where possible.
- [F0] Context loss: rendering stops; on `webglcontextrestored` the canvas remounts (fresh context) and the engine reboots.
- [F0] Render contract for ink (B4): outlines from depth plus normals reconstructed from depth, so no MRT outputs anywhere. Not inked = no depth write or `LAYER_NO_INK` (1, enabled on the camera by core; `LAYER_WORLD` = 0). Custom ShaderMaterials merge `ctx.uniforms` and end with the tone-mapping and colour-space chunks (the stars and the rim shell now do).
- [F0] Lighting hooks for A3 in the shared toon kit: `lbDuskTint` multiplies direct light in the terminator band (`lbDuskAt`, sun −7°…+20° over the fragment), and `lbCloudShadow` / `lbCloudShadowOn` / `lbCloudShadowRot` (an equirectangular coverage map, off by default). The v0 sky sets a pink tint.
- [F0] Toon `fade` option: a shared dither fade by camera altitude (`altTerrain`) or distance, applied to the shadow depth twin too; `ToonPatch.depthFragment` (additive) for custom fragment-side effects on shadows. The reduced-motion reveal dither now reaches shadows as well.
- [F0] GLSL: no `smoothstep` with descending edges (undefined in GLSL ES 3.0 / MSL: Safari, iPhone); `lbNightAt` and the stars use `1 − smoothstep(lo, hi, x)`.
- [F0] Toon ramp band edges widened from 0.05 to ~0.08 (≈ 25 m of ground per edge) so a band edge across the flat plateau reads as a soft gradient, not a seam. B4 may retune.
- [F0] Camera altitude reference = terrain / water, or inside the city the walking surface (`cityIndex.groundH`: road, curb, lawn). Roofs never enter it; they only lift the eye through a separate spring (`lift`: rise ω 12, settle ω 3) to keep min(2.5, alt) above the highest roof within a radius that grows with altitude (a point query below 3 m, so walking past a wall never lifts you onto its roof), faded out from 40 to 80 m. Hard floors: 0.8 m above terrain and above the roof directly underneath. No zoom pumping when a tower passes under the camera.
- [F0] `ViewState.altTerrain` (additive): eye height above terrain/water, roofs ignored; `lbCamAlt` now mirrors it. LOD and fades use it. `ViewState.alt` is the zoom altitude that drives pitch and FOV.
- [F0] Grab-spin picks against a sphere at the terrain height under the camera, fixed for the whole drag (it used the roof-inclusive reference, so the grabbed point drifted over towers).
- [F0] Pitch curve is one monotone C1 curve in log-altitude: two cubic Hermite segments with the slope matched at 4 m (spec'd), flat where it meets top-down, with a zero-end-slope bump that keeps the horizon in frame below ~25 m. The old curve stalled at −8° at 4 m and kicked again below.
- [F0] Keyboard: the page variant ignores keys while focus is in an input, textarea, select or contentEditable, and clears held keys when focus moves into one (the palette works on /planet).
- [F0] `CameraService.lastInputAt()` (additive) replaces the untyped `ctx.cameraInput` side channel for the hint row.
- [F0] Sky: the sky amount is per ray, from the air along it (density by the height of the ray's closest approach, a grazing ray seeing more path), not from camera altitude alone. Thin air keeps its hue: pale horizon → sky blue → deep saturated blue → space. Cartoon cheat: the air's scale height shrinks with altitude (60 m seen from the street, 7 m from orbit), one value per frame, so there is no seam inside a frame. The dive sky now goes (0,4,22) → (9,67,143) → (106,171,239) → (168,204,237) with no grey or lavender step; the rim shell is a thinner edge on top (only above 180 m).
- [F0] Sky night per ray (where the ray's air is), and dip-aware near the ground: the sky's night/dusk use the sun's elevation above the VISIBLE horizon (dip capped at 9°, faded out toward orbit). On this planet the horizon dips ~8° at eye height, so the sun still shows after it has set underfoot.
- [F0] Sun disc drawn in screen space at a constant pixel radius (1.9° at frame centre): no egg at the frame edge at 70° FOV. The halo stays angular.
- [F0] Golden light without moving the terminator: kept START_HOUR_ANGLE 64° (sun ~28° over the city; raising it to 78° would put the eastern half of the plateau in night at t = 0, since elevation changes 1° per 2.8 m). Instead a wide `warm` window (elevation sin 0.1–0.85) turns the sun golden, and the narrow `dusk` band drives the sunset sky. Sunset colour on surfaces is per fragment (`lbDuskTint`).
- [F0] Shots: `dusk` is timed at −(dip − 1°) ≈ −7.3° at the viewpoint so the disc touches the visible horizon; `orbit` moved 12° east so the terminator is in frame.
- [F0] Connector conflicts also cover near passes: two connectors at a node closer than `VEHICLE_CLEARANCE` = 2.9 m (bus 2.4 m + margin) conflict even if they never cross (the stub's opposing left turns passed 2.10 m apart). `validatePlan` checks it.
- [F0] Zebra crossings sit `CROSSING_SETBACK` = 2.0 m out along each junction arm (strip 0.8-3.2 m from the patch edge), never on the patch or over a connector; sidewalks split at the crossing points. Additive fields: `Lane.stopS` (front-bumper stop line, ≥ 1 m before the strip, else the lane end), `Lane.crossingAtEnd` / `crossingAtStart`, `WalkEdge.laneS`. A crossing's half-width is `width / 2` (no separate field).
- [F0] `ctx.services.crossings` (additive, `CrossingState`): `busy` (people → traffic) and `blocked` (traffic → people), per walk-edge id, written in fixedUpdate; traffic registers before people.
- [F0] `planBasis()` (additive, frame.ts): the exponential map's unnormalised Jacobian. Footprinted instances use it, so the rendered box matches its mapped footprint (unit tangents made a 16 m house up to 5 % wider: neighbours 0.5 m apart overlapped on screen). Residual second-order error ≈ 7 cm at the corners of a 14 × 10 m box (spec'd).
- [F0] Ground heights (config.ts): `ROAD_H` 0.05, `CURB_H` 0.15 (sidewalk top 0.2), `AREA_H` 0.03 above the plateau, so nothing paved is coplanar with the terrain; `CityIndex.groundH(x, z)` (additive) returns the surface anywhere (terrain outside the plan).
- [F0] `CityIndex.obstacles` (additive): features with a collision disc (`Feature.r`, or a default by kind: streetlight 0.2, tree 0.4, flag 0.15, bench 0.5, bus stop 0.6, fountain 1.6) are in `collide()`, so FPV no longer walks through lamps and trunks. `Feature.size` / `seed` (additive) for trees.
- [F0] `validatePlan` also checks buildings against every walk edge, features against buildings and carriageways, connectors inside their patch, crossings clear of connectors, stop lines before crossings, and connector clearance.
- [F0] The CityPlan is read-only and deep-frozen in development (it is shared by every engine instance).
- [F0] `render/palette.ts`: the BRIEF §3 palette as named Colors, owned by B4; terrain, ocean and sky import from it.
- [F0] `dirToChart` maps the city's exact antipode to (π·R, 0), not the city centre.
- [F0] `QualitySettings.antialias` is the canvas MSAA flag (a context attribute, fixed at creation); B4 sets it false on tiers whose post chain renders into its own targets.

## Camera and controls (A4)

- [A4] (Superseded in the refine round, see below.) The dive (`camera/dive.ts`, pure, spec'd) is a fixed 10 s shot (`DIVE_SECONDS`; render `DIVE_SECONDS × fps` frames with glide 1/fps). Altitude follows a pacing table (seconds per log-unit by altitude: brisk orbit, slower clouds, slowest rooftops → street) through a C1 trapezoid ease (from rest, into rest), then a 5 % hold on the street. Distance-to-go is a function of altitude (flared glide slope below 50 m), so position and height are locked and every frame is a pure function of u.
- [A4] (Superseded in the refine round, see below.) Dive ground track: the last ~60 m follow the street the `street` viewpoint stands on, traced backwards through the road graph (straightest continuation, ≤ 72° turns), corners rounded (Chaikin), easing onto the sidewalk only in the last 22 m and pushed clear of lamp posts / trunks / walls (`CityIndex.collide`, r 0.9) in the last 30 m. Before it, a Bézier swings in from the orbit start (swung 34° toward the city centre's side). Heading looks ahead along the track (5–40 m by altitude) and settles onto the viewpoint heading in the last 14 m.
- [A4] (Superseded in the refine round, see below.) Dive framing: above ~90 m the camera looks AT the city (a target halfway from the landing to the centre) and faces it, so the orbit frame has the city in the middle and the planet's limb across the top — the limb becomes the horizon, in frame for the whole descent (the dive opens at ≈ −77° pitch, not straight down; BRIEF's top-down globe stays the interactive orbit view). Below, it blends into the interactive curve + a bump (≤ 17°, 10–130 m). A running max keeps pitch monotone (the view only tips up), box-filtered. The interactive curve is unchanged (horizon in frame below ~33 m).
- [A4] (Superseded in the refine round, see below.) Dive touchdown = the `street` viewpoint slid forward along its heading (≤ 4 m) when a lamp post or trunk stands in the last 4.5 m of the approach corridor: the current viewpoint has a lamp 2 m behind it, and passing it at < 1 m read as a clip. The `street` shot itself is unchanged.
- [A4] Named shot `approach` (the dive at u = 0.6, ~12 m over the street) for reviewing the street from low flight.
- [A4] (Superseded in the refine round, see below.) Roof lift: the target is max over nearby buildings of (roof + min(2.5, alt) − eye) × liftRamp(distance to the footprint) (1 within a margin ≤ 1 m, easing to 0 over 0.3 + 0.3·(alt − 7) m), evaluated at the focus and 0.45 s ahead along its plan velocity; spring rise ω 9, settle ω 3. Below 7 m only the roof directly underneath counts (walking collisions apply there). Narrow enough to fly down a street canyon between towers; a tower swells the camera up instead of jolting it.
- [A4] (Superseded in the refine round, see below.) A zoom/fly-to never lands on a roof: below 25 m with the zoom headed for the street, a focus over a footprint glides to the sidewalk (near the curb) of the nearest road on its side — not the nearest face, which can be a back yard — and the view turns to look along that street; obstacles just nudge it (`collide`). Fly-to targets below 4 m resolve the same way first. Over open water the zoom floor is 6 m (no standing on the sea); walking blocks land → sea only.
- [A4] Movement (keys, stick) eases: heading-relative velocity approaches the wanted one (rate 9/s walking, 6/s panning; stop 11/s / 5/s). Pan speed 0.7 m/s per metre of altitude (cap 160, ×2 with Shift). Wall collisions only at walking height (w > 0.95).
- [A4] ← → turn (1.7 rad/s, eased) at street level instead of strafing (A / D strafe), so keyboard-only players can look around; above street level the arrows still pan. Jump is edge-triggered (a tap shorter than a frame still jumps).
- [A4] Grab-spin that starts off the planet's disc grabs the limb (the ray's closest approach to the centre), so dragging in space still spins. A zoom with the cursor on the sky (or keys) zooms straight: no stale anchor.
- [A4] Pointer lock: mouse clicks only (never touch), and released automatically once you lift off above street level. The wheel takes keyboard focus (WASD after a scroll-only descent in the window), never from a text field.
- [A4] (Superseded in the refine round, see below.) Touch: a left-thumb virtual stick (lower-left 45 % × 70 % zone, floating under the thumb, 46 px travel, dead zone + soft curve, full deflection runs), offered only once touch is seen or the pointer is coarse, only at street level; the other thumb looks around. When a pinch ends with one finger down, that finger carries on as a drag (no jump). Pinch zoom gain 1.3 (was 1.6: a big spread went 2.7 log-units). Stick drawn square (the site's `border-radius: 0`).
- [A4] `CameraService.stick?()` (additive): the touch UI's live state for the canvas overlay (visible / active / origin / deflection / touch seen).
- [A4] Hint row: shows 2 s after the first frame or the last input; a line already shown returns only after 9 s idle (a new line, e.g. on reaching the street, after 2 s). Lines: orbit `drag to spin · scroll to dive · double-click to fly`; street `wasd walk · drag to look · space jump · scroll out to fly` (page: `click to look`); touch `drag to spin · pinch to dive · double-tap to fly` / `left thumb walks · drag to look · pinch out to fly`.

### A4 refine round (after the critic's 5.5/10)

- [A4] Landings are chosen, not inherited (`camera/landing.ts`, pure): a pavement spot (sidewalk / corner / plaza walk edges, never a carriageway) plus a heading, scored on the first street-level frame: no camera solid within 4 m inside ±30–35° of the view (hard for the dive), clear view length (analytic ray vs building boxes, centre and ±18°), room to the side, the spot and the street ahead in sun (a ray toward the sun vs roof heights), poles off the view axis, and for the dive building mass in view + the clock tower visible + 7–12° off the street axis. Two-stage search (cheap score for every spot, full score for the best few): ~4 ms interactive, ~10 ms for the whole-city dive search.
- [A4] Camera solids: vertical cylinders built from plan features (lamp pole r 0.2 to 5.3 m + its head r 0.75 at 4.6–5.5 m on the arm; tree trunk + crown r 0.42·size from 0.28·size to size; shelters, flags, fountains). `CityIndex.obstacles` is 2D and knows nothing of lamp heads or crowns at eye height.
- [A4] The dive's landing is its own best landing (A2's `street` viewpoint competes on the same score; the `street` shot itself still stands on A2's viewpoint). Candidates are tried best first until the whole path passes `clearPath`: a HARD constraint, not a nudge — below 14 m the eye keeps 1.5 m (3D) from every solid and facade (0.6 m in the last 3 m), and in the last 25 m nothing at eye level sits within 4 m inside ±30° of the view (spec'd, independently re-checked). So the dive stays safe on whatever plan A2 ships. New shot `landing` (the dive's last frame, the /play loop point/poster); `approach` moved to u = 0.7.
- [A4] Dive opening: starts on the interactive start framing (top-down over the city centre, pitch −90°) and glides out onto the track over the first 2.4 s (smootherstep) while tipping into the look-at-city framing; heading comes from the un-blended track, so no flip passing over the framing target.
- [A4] Dive pitch: raw wish (look-at high up, curve + bump low) sampled at sparse altitude anchors, made monotone by isotonic regression (pool adjacent violators, a pooled run collapses to one knot), then a Fritsch–Carlson monotone cubic. No running-max plateau: every 0.3 s window between u 0.08 and 0.86 tips up ≥ 0.4° (spec'd).
- [A4] Dive pacing: orbit > 120 m 2.5 s, 120–50 m 1.6 s, the cloud layer 48–36 m 0.43 s (a short white-out), 36–15 m 2.0 s (the swoop), 15–5 m 1.7 s, 5–1.76 m 1.1 s, hold ~0.35 s + the ease. Trapezoid ease 0.07 / 0.91 (moving within 0.2 s). Below the clouds the heading sweeps 15° toward the side the landing heading turns to and settles back (parallax on near buildings); sidewalk ease over the last 24 m and heading ease over 26 m (the old 12 m jog swung the view 30° near the ground).
- [A4] Lens: vertical FOV 56° at street level, 45° at 8 m, 43° at 60 m, 40° in orbit (was 70° → 40°: towers at the frame edges leaned 15–35°). `lensFov` caps the horizontal FOV at 84° on wide canvases and widens the vertical FOV by 1/√aspect on portrait ones (≤ 80°). `lensShift`: a vertical lens shift of up to 16 % of the frame height between ~5 and ~80 m (none at street level, none in orbit, none on near-level views) — the camera axis pitches up by δ = atan(2k·tan(fov/2)) and `camera.view.offsetY = k` shifts the frustum down, so the frame's centre row still looks along the pose pitch while verticals converge less. `ctx.view.forward` / `pitch` stay the centre-row direction; `ctx.view.fov` is the fitted vertical FOV. **For B4:** the projection is off-axis whenever `camera.view` is enabled — reconstruct rays/normals from `camera.projectionMatrixInverse`, never from fov/aspect alone.
- [A4] Touchdown resolver (interactive): once a zoom whose target is below 10 m passes 28 m inside the city, it picks the best landing within 24 m of the focus (whatever is under it: roof, lot, carriageway, junction), marking down spots whose glide in would pass a building taller than the descending eye, and glides focus and heading onto it with progress tied to the log-altitude lost (done by ~3.5 m). Keys cancel it, a drag hands the heading back; zooming out past 12 m resets it. Fly-to below 4.5 m flies to the best landing within 14 m of the clicked point, turning on the way.
- [A4] Roof lift narrowed: only a roof under the eye or within ~0.6 + 1.2 m lifts it, and the look-ahead applies only to panning (keys, stick, thrown spin), not to zoom-anchor or landing motion. `keepOffWalls`: in low flight (not walking, not landing) a facade whose roof is above the eye pushes the focus out to 2.5–4.5 m (by altitude) toward the street — a lift-off from against a wall drifts out to ~3 m by 7 m up; a zoom aimed into a tall footprint has its anchor moved 3.5 m clear of it. The critic's elevator ride (20–28 m beside a 32 m tower, then a 20 m drop) is gone: max lift 0 m in the same trial.
- [A4] Touch: a touch in the stick zone is not the stick until it is held 100 ms or moves 8 px; a second finger landing first turns both into a pinch, lifting it first is a tap. Zone shrunk to the bottom-left 35 % × 40 % (always covering the resting stick plus a thumb's margin). Hint row never wraps on phones (smaller tracking under 460 px).

### A4 G1 fix round

- [A4] Zoom anchor (map-style, supersedes "re-picked every notch"): the world point under the cursor is locked once per gesture (re-picked only if the cursor / pinch centre moves > 8 px; a smaller move pans with it) and held under the cursor pixel every frame by a rigid rotation of the state after the altitude/pitch/lens update (the camera is re-posed, the cursor ray re-cast, grab-spin's trick, so the solve is exact). It holds fully above 24 m and lets go smoothly by 13 m (and on grazing rays < ~4–14°); while partly held the anchor follows the cursor's ground point, so no error piles up and the focus speed ramps instead of snapping, both ways (lift-off ramps in the same way). Lateral speed capped at max(15, 2.5·alt) m/s. Drift on the G1 wheel probe: 0–1 px down to 22 m (was 28 px at 94 m, 508 px at 46 m). `clearAnchor` (aim at a tower's foot) is gone: the anchor is no longer converged onto, so it is not needed.
- [A4] Aim: at the lock, the cursor ray is marched (0.75 m steps, bisected) for the first building it enters; that point (else the ground anchor) is what the landing faces. LandingQuery gets `aimX/aimZ` (additive): candidates whose heading is > 25° off the aim's bearing lose 5/rad, spots > 35 m from it lose a little, and stage 2 adds a variant turned toward the aim (≤ 45° off the pavement line). The G1 tower probe now lands 39 m from the helipad tower with it 17° off the view axis (was: 26 m past it, tower behind).
- [A4] Touchdown: the landing waits until the anchor has let go (≤ 13 m with an anchor, 28 m without), so it starts with the focus nearly at rest; the glide is a Hermite curve that still starts from the focus's current velocity (any residual pull), progress is monotone (zooming back up holds the glide instead of retracing it), and its lateral speed is capped at 3 + 1.5·alt m/s, 0.6 + 3.5·(distance left) m/s and ±90 m/s² — the descent is held back to that pace (the altitude spring continues from the held-back state). Per-frame change of the lateral step ≤ 0.13 m on every desktop/touch probe below 40 m (was 0.75→0 m/frame stops); spec'd in camera/flow.spec.ts (the real camera system on jsdom, wheel events, 30 fps).
- [A4] Glide clearance (the dive's rule, now interactive): every landing candidate (10, best first) is tried with three glide shapes (lateral motion done at 75 / 50 / 32 % of the log-altitude: a long diagonal, or cross over high and settle straight down under the canopy) and its planned eye path (Hermite + altitude + a roof-lift model that rises over roofs and settles at the spring's ω 3) must keep 1.5 m from every camera solid and facade (`glideMargin`, landing.ts), 0.6 m at walking height and in the last 3 m, and near the start only the room it already has. The first clear one wins, else the roomiest. Fly-to below 4.5 m runs the same test on its own path (eased track, hop and the altitude spring's lag simulated), and below 3 m the flight slides round poles like a walk. Search and checks are split over two frames (~3 ms + ≤ 2.6 ms worst case on the M3) to stay off the frame budget.
- [A4] Facades in low flight and landings: the push is weighed in as a roof rises from 0.5 m below the eye to 1.5 m above it (no jolt when a descent sinks below a roof line), ramps in over 2.2–4.7 m altitude (lift-off beside a wall drifts, no 0.24 m first step), is 7/s instead of 3.5/s while the zoom anchor pulls toward the wall, and is never blocked by a lower roof (it used to freeze over a low shop next to the church). During a landing the same push starts at keepOffWalls' berth (seamless hand-over), eases to 0.6 m and relaxes as the glide ends.
- [A4] Countryside: FPV walking collides with A1's `services.nature` trunks and boulders (body radius 0.55 m; the city's is 0.35); a low camera outside the city eases out of the discs with a wider berth while still at crown height (0.55 + 1.2 m above ~6 m). 0 of 21 random 5 s countryside walks enter a disc (was 3 of 10).
- [A4] Drag release: the release frame coasts (inertia applies the same frame), no one-frame freeze between the drag and the throw.
- [A4] Shot `clouds`: pitch −35° (was the altitude curve's −69°), so the shot shows the layer across to downtown instead of pale blobs at the frame edges. A3's anchors read only its position and heading, so they are unchanged but may want re-placing for the new framing.
- [A4] Core (bundle, small and contained): the engine imports `core/debug.ts` dynamically, only in dev or with `?shot` (debug is its own chunk). shots.ts and dive.ts still ship until clouds/index.ts stops importing `diveAt` / `SHOTS` statically (A3).

### A4 G2 fix round

- [A4] Dive landing = the poster: per spot (heading-free) the eye keeps ≥ 0.95 m from the kerb (KERB_MIN) and stands on the building half of the pavement (55 % / 100 % of the way from its centre line; the walk centre is no longer tried), ≥ 5 m from both ends of every zebra; a lamp / streetlight / flag pole within ±8° of the view axis and 22 m is a hard reject; looking out over junction asphalt (4 m / 8 m ahead) is rejected. Scored: kerb distance (+1.2 at 2.15 m), the kerb lane's queue zone (−3 within 22 m upstream of its stop line, easing out to 34 m: no truck parked at the lens), the road on the left (+0.6: the near lane's cars drive away; oncoming walkers keep right, out along the kerb), a street canyon (building mass on BOTH sides of the frame, houses counting half, downtown 1.2×) and −0.3 per lawn / verge sample in the lower frame (the old winner was the edge of town, half meadow). The clock tower is tried as a heading when within 28° of the pavement line. Deviation: the critic asked ≥ 2.5 m from the carriageway; sidewalks are 1.6–2.2 m wide, so that exists only at corners and plazas — the winner is a downtown corner at 2.9 m.
- [A4] The clip's start time: `DIVE_T0 = 1` (core/shots.ts). Render the /play clip with `--dive 301 --t 1`: its loop point (sim t = 11) has nobody within ~8 m of the lens, no walker coming at it, no vehicle within ~13 m, and ~20 people + 4 cars in view; the last 1.2 s of the descent stays clean too. Picked by running the whole 30 fps dive from each T0 ∈ [0, 6] (0.25 s apart) and scoring the frames from u = 0.88 plus a second after the end (docs/littlebig/shots/A4-G2fix/scan2.mjs, gitignored; worth moving to scripts/). Neighbouring T0 values ±0.25 s give quite different frames, and the sun moves 0.75°/s, so T0 stays small and **must be re-scanned whenever traffic or people change**. The `landing` shot runs at DIVE_T0 + DIVE_SECONDS (same light; a time jump places people afresh and clears the lens, so it's clean at every t from 9 to 13), `approach` at DIVE_T0 + 7. Wiring `diveT0` into the debug hook and the shot tool's `--dive` default is core's (debug.ts / scripts).
- [A4] `towerVisible` hid the tower behind its own box (the sight line stopped at half its width, not its half diagonal) and behind any thin pole: it was false for every view, the `street` viewpoint included. Fixed: stop short of the tower's corners; only crowns (r ≥ 0.5) block.
- [A4] Shot `street`: A2's viewpoint stepped 0.9 m toward the buildings and turned 6° toward them (kept only if that spot is still clear sidewalk): on the viewpoint a lamp pole 8 m ahead split the frame against the clock tower.
- [A4] FPV vs pedestrians: contract (additive) `Services.people?: PeopleService` with `pushOut(x, z, r, out)` (plan coords, zero-alloc). After the walls, the walking body (r 0.35) is pushed out of walker / idler discs, back by at most the step + 0.05 m, re-checked against the walls: it slides round people instead of passing through a dithered ghost. Spec'd with a faked service (camera/flow.spec.ts). **B2 must install the service** (people/: iterate its grid, body radius ~0.3); until then this is a no-op.
- [A4] Dive landing search: per-spot rules hoisted out of the heading loop and poles gathered per spot: ~2.8 k heading scores instead of ~9.9 k. buildDivePath ≈ 25 ms in vitest at load 12 (it runs at clouds init through `diveAt`).

## Sky, clouds, light (A3)

- [A3] Sky = one air model from street to orbit (sky/shaders.ts): a = (ray angle above the planet's limb) / θ, θ = `domeAngle(altSea)` (sky/rig.ts, a monotone C1 table in log-log: 190° at 20 m → 96° at 50 m → 19° at 110 m → 2.5° at 420 m). Colour along a: pale horizon → sky blue → deep blue → space; seen from orbit the profile tightens (crisp pale inner edge, long soft fade). Replaces F0's altitude-dependent scale height, which ballooned into a bright cyan halo mid-dive. F0's back-faced additive rim shell is gone.
- [A3] Sky time of day per ray: inside the air (street … ~60 m) at the eye over the visible, dipped horizon (one time of day for the whole sky; colour varies by direction to the sun: orange at it, pink beside, purple opposite); from orbit at the ray's closest approach (limb lit on one side, dark on the other, pink/purple at the terminator). Blend `smooth(60, 300, altSea)`. Per-ray-only made the low sky toward a setting sun read as midday (its air point sits ~15° sunward).
- [A3] Golden hour in the sky is a narrow gold band right on the horizon (strongest under the sun) with the pale band bridging to the blue: gold never meets blue directly, so they never mix to grey (the first wide glow around the sun read as a beige smear).
- [A3] Limb haze: a front-faced additive shell at R + 30 (above every peak) that only lights pixels whose ray meets the sea-level sphere: a glow growing toward the planet's silhouette, plus a narrow twilight belt across the disc (orchid on the sunward side → purple toward night, sun ≈ −10°…+4°). Orbit only (fades in 150 → 360 m). Reason: the toon kit's lbDuskTint can only multiply direct light, and pink × green grass = olive/brown; the belt is what makes the terminator read pink → purple → night from orbit. See the B4 note below.
- [A3] Start of day unchanged (START_HOUR_ANGLE 64°, sun ~28° over the city): measured, the twilight belt already reaches ~64 m east of the centre at t = 0, so any later start pushes part of the plateau into dusk. Golden comes from colour instead: key light lerps to #ffcb80 (golden, spec'd never orange: linear g/r > 0.55, b/r > 0.18), peach-tinted sky fill, golden rim light, the sky's horizon band.
- [A3] Night: moonlight fill #6670c8 × 1.25 (the night side reads as a blue-violet toy, dark enough for the city lights); dusk adds a pink-lilac sky fill. A cartoon moon (world/sun.ts `moonDirection`: trails the sun by 158°, dec −8°, so it rides high over the city at night) drawn in the dome as a constant-size disc, toon-lit by the sun (its phase), with craters. Stars fade per star with the air (hidden by daylit air, fade in over 1–14° above the night horizon), a few tinted.
- [A3] Clouds: 30 scattered cumulus + anchors, each a flat-based cluster (crown, overlapping ring, fillers) of instanced spheres; layout pure + spec'd (clouds/layout.ts). No scattered cloud within 0.3 rad (≈ 49 m) of the city centre, only small sparse ones out to 0.62 rad; spec: shadow coverage over the plateau < 15 % even with the dive anchor. Drift = a slow rotation (0.25°/s, param `clouds.drift`) about the CITY's axis, so coverage over the city never changes with time.
- [A3] Cloud anchors: one cluster where `diveAt` crosses the middle of the layer (the scripted dive goes straight through its crown: the white-out), and two framing the `clouds` shot (left of the camera footprint and ahead-right, low). Both follow A4's dive / shot automatically (computed from `diveAt` / `SHOTS.clouds` at init); at other times drift moves them on.
- [A3] Puff shading in a custom ShaderMaterial (merges ctx.uniforms, fog chunks, tone-mapping/colour-space chunks): two crisp toon bands, lavender shade deepening toward the flat base, cel-style pale-blue bellies, backlit translucency, golden rim, dusk blush (lbDuskAt), moonlit navy at night (lbNightAt, sampled 6 m higher: clouds keep the sun a moment longer). Cotton lumps and breathing in the vertex shader. Clouds cast no shadow-map shadows (they would be as dark as a building's).
- [A3] Cloud LOD (clouds/puffs.ts): a far InstancedMesh (UV sphere 22×13) and a near one (48×28, ≤ 64 puffs within 70/82 m of the eye, hysteresis), one shared material; instance buffers rewritten only when the partition changes. 826k → ~240k cloud triangles, smooth silhouettes when flying through.
- [A3] Cloud shadows through the toon kit's `lbCloudShadow` hook: a 512×256 equirectangular coverage map splatted from the layout and blurred, looked up through drift⁻¹ · (rotation toward the sun by the cloud's offset at the focus, ≤ 34 m; 12 m from orbit). Strength 0.4 (param `clouds.shadow`), fading out as the sun at the focus drops from 20° to 7°; always on from orbit.
- [A3] White-out: each frame the eye is tested against the puffs (cloud frame, flat bases honoured); veil = max over puffs of smooth(5.5 → 1.2 m to the surface) × smooth(2.6 → 0.3 m below the base). A full-screen veil (last, no depth, LAYER_NO_INK) with wisps that scroll with altitude; puffs inside 1.5 × near dither away under it, so the near plane never visibly slices one. The scripted dive spends ~0.5 s in it.
- [A3] Golden hour follows the CITY's clock near the city: the golden window sees min(local sun, city-centre sun) within ~0.6–1.1 rad of the city (sky/index.ts → computeRig `elevGold`). The plateau spans ~30° of sun angle; the `street` viewpoint (where the dive lands) has the sun ~42° at t = 0, so keyed to the focus alone the street read as midday. A colour cheat: shadows there stay medium-length.
- [A3] From orbit the key light carries half the golden tint (the whole lit hemisphere, noon included, is in view); it warms continuously on the way down.
- [A3] Clouds seen from inside the air (street … ~60 m) take the eye's time of day, like the sky (`uEyeNight` / `uEyeDusk`, blended out by `airSpace`): a cloud past the terminator, visible over the tiny planet's horizon, otherwise hung as a navy blob in a blue sky.
- [A3] Shadow fit: minimum half-extent 36 m (was 24) so towers peeking over the ~23 m street horizon keep their shadows; the rest of F0's fit (quantised extent, texel-snapped centre) kept.
- [A3] `SkyService.veil?: number` (additive): the white-out amount (0..1), for B4 to fade ink / tilt-shift while inside a cloud (ink composited over the veil would show the city through the cloud).
- [A3] Note for B4 (render/toon.ts is not A3's): `lbDuskTint` multiplies direct light, so on green grass any pink/purple tint becomes olive/brown. Proposed: tint by luminance in the dusk band, e.g. `vec3 d = reflectedLight.directDiffuse; reflectedLight.directDiffuse = mix(d, dot(d, vec3(0.3, 0.59, 0.11)) * lbDuskTint * 1.6, lbDuskAt(vLbWorld) * 0.6);` — then the surfaces themselves blush and the limb-haze twilight belt can get weaker.

### A3 refine round (after the critic, 6.4/10)

- [A3] Fill is lavender-blue #a8b6f2 × 2.6 from the sky and warm bounce #e0b892 × 1.25 from below (params `sky.fill`, `sky.bounce`; key `sky.key` 3.3). The old sky-blue × 1.05 fill lost all red, and the Neutral tone curve maps anything whose darkest channel is under ~0.08 to 6.25·x², so shaded asphalt landed at #02142e. Now shaded sidewalk ≈ #9898bf, shaded asphalt ≈ #1f1e43 (it is A2's dark albedo; B4 tunes the ramp/grade on top). The golden fill tint was cut to 25 % of a soft pink-lilac: a peach fill made lit pavement pink. Note for A1: the ocean adds `lbSkyFill × fresnel × 0.35`, so it is ~2.5× brighter than before; scale it down there if the limb ocean reads too pale.
- [A3] Alpenglow (supersedes "never orange" for the key light near the eye): inside the air, while the sun is between ~2° below and ~10° above the eye's dipped horizon, the key light turns rose #ff9f86 (`rig.alpen`, faded out by `spaceAmount`), so sun-facing walls and tower tops catch warm light at dusk while the street is in the planet's shadow. In the same window the night fill becomes the sunset sky's pink-lilac (#c49ce0 × 1.9), stronger than the moon. Spec: still golden at most from orbit, and in the air with the sun above ~13°.
- [A3] Sun disc coloured by its elevation over the visible horizon (`discColor`): cream above 40°, golden #ffd27c at 24°, the site amber #FFB84D at 11°, deep amber-orange on the horizon. It is mixed into the sky (×1.08), not added, so it never clips to white. Golden sky: the gold horizon band swells under a low sun (up to 0.34 of the dome), a light warm wash over the lower sky, and a pow-10 peach→gold glow around a sun below ~30° that is ADDED to the blue, so it brightens it rather than going grey.
- [A3] Orbit sky tail: the soft outer fade into space runs on its own angle (`tailAngle`: 1.15 × the angle of the cloud shell's top above the limb, ~6.5° at 380 m, never less than the dome angle). The crisp inner edge stays on the dome angle, so limb clouds sit in the glow instead of hanging in black space.
- [A3] Space is a linear (0.017, 0.02, 0.047), which shows as a deep blue (#0a1030-ish) after the tone curve; the palette's #070B1A rendered #000416. A faint milky band runs across it. Stars: 2200 (high), 2.3 to 6 px, 38 % crowded into the band, 2.5 % big 4-point sparkles that twinkle in size.
- [A3] Painted far cumulus on the dome, seen only from inside the air (faded out from 32 to 56 m): 7 toon cumulus at 6 to 20° elevation in the eye's horizontal frame, drifting slowly. Each is a union of 6 discs with a flat base, its lumps shaded over one rounded body, coloured by the cloud palette at the eye's time of day, with a cluster-edge rim. The real layer can't be seen from the street on this tiny planet (no cloud over the city, and the outskirt clouds sink below the ~23 m horizon).
- [A3] Cloud palette (supersedes the white→navy RGB lerps): stops by the sun's elevation at the cloud. Lit: white → cream #fff2de → peach #ffcab4 → mauve #d8a6d6 → moonlit indigo #7a82c8. Matching shade, belly (day belly #9fa6d8) and rim stops (moonlit rim #9aa3e0). Interpolation only runs between saturated neighbours, so a cloud never goes grey. At night the light direction for the bands and the rim is the moon. The sky's cumulus, the veil and the puffs share it (`cloudPalette` / uniform arrays).
- [A3] Puffs are shaded per fragment from the analytic sphere normal (puff centre from instanceMatrix, so no facets), with two-octave cloud-frame sine-lattice bumps that fade with distance. They have two toon bands plus a soft falloff in the lit band and sky-facing form in the shade. The band edges widen as the sun grazes, which cures the candy stripes at the terminator. The rim is per CLUSTER: a fresnel against the cluster's ellipsoid (new per-instance `aCluster`) times the puff's own, so a cumulus reads as one cloud. The flat base is an exponential soft clamp (a rounded pillow) with a darker, cooler base face. Mesh normals and UVs were dropped. LOD meshes are now 20×12 far and 40×24 near (high tier).
- [A3] Each cloud's time of day: the eye's (dip-aware) value near the eye, its own `lbNightAt` / `lbDuskAt` beyond 50 to 130 m and everywhere from orbit (supersedes the eye-only rule, which lit clouds over the night ocean white).
- [A3] White-out rework (supersedes the old veil line). The amount is a skewed ramp in from 13 m outside a puff's sphere to 1.5 m inside, plus mist under each CLUSTER's base: the distance to its footprint disc, thinning over 13 m. Per-puff base discs had gaps, so the city peeked out and was swallowed again. The scene fog is pulled in log-space toward the cloud colour (visibility down to 9 m), so the city fades by depth instead of showing crisp through a flat overlay. The sky dome sinks into the same mist (`uVeil`). The overlay adds a fill only above 0.75 and domain-warped, high-contrast wisps that stream outward from the screen point the eye flies toward; their phase is the distance flown, so they scale with speed. At veil > 0.985 an opaque blocker at the near plane is drawn right after the dome, so the hidden scene fails the depth test. Measured on the scripted dive: ~0.3 s in, ~0.25 s fully white, ~0.35 s out through mist, with no re-entry flicker.
- [A3] The dive anchor sits 7 m beside the track (radius 11), not on it: the dive clips the cluster's outer ring (a brief white-out) while the crown towers right beside the camera on the way in.
- [A3] Layout: the scatter comes from its own seeded stream (`scatterClusters`), and any scatter cluster that overlaps an anchor is dropped, so moving the dive or a shot no longer reshuffles every cloud on the planet (spec'd).
- [A3] Cloud shadows: a 1024×512 coverage map with no blur pass (crisp shapes), strength 0.6 (×1.4 from orbit, capped at 0.9). Shadows land beside their clouds, but they stay subtle: they only dim direct light, and the stronger fill and the low golden sun dilute them.
- [A3] Twilight belt: −40 %, faded toward the limb, 30 % desaturated (islands no longer turn into pink stains). It can be halved again once B4 lands the luminance-based dusk tint (note above).
- [A3] Named shot `cloudscape` (additive, core/shots.ts): across the top of the cloud layer at 46 m, pitch −12°. The `clouds` shot looks down at −69° with a vertical half-FOV of ~22°, so a cloud in the 36–48 m layer can only enter it as a wall at the frame edge, and no anchor placement shows a lit crown there. The orchestrator could give `clouds` a pitch override of about −35°.

### A3 G1 fix round

- [A3] Puffs are cauliflowers: each is the union of its core sphere and six bulge spheres (five round its shoulders, one on top, per-puff sizes and placement), as a radial function of direction (`PUFF_SHAPE_GLSL`, shared by the vertex stage for the silhouette and the fragment stage for the normal). The shading normal is the winning sphere's, so each lobe has its own lit cap and terminator and the seams are crisp creases; the per-pixel evaluation runs only on triangles near a seam (per-vertex `vSeam`) within 110 m (60 m low), elsewhere the per-vertex normal. A third, stronger octave of silhouette noise grows in within ~25 m. Replaces the sine-lattice bumps that tore the band edges into zig-zags; fine bumps are now a ±3 % value tweak only.
- [A3] Cloud shading reads as one mass: the normal blends toward the cluster ellipsoid's (and the puff's) form with distance (30 → 75 % over 20–120 m), and almost fully on the cluster's underside or whenever the eye looks up at it; a height gradient into the belly colour over the lowest 4.5 m above the flat base (continuous across puffs). Seen from below, the puffs' flattened discs had shown as a pattern of lavender ovals. The time-of-day blend between the eye's and the cloud's own sun keys on the cluster centre's distance, not the fragment's.
- [A3] Cloud palette: day belly #c3c8ef / shade #cdd3f3 (the top half reads white from below); lit band thresholds 0.36 / 0.52. Night stops are given as on-screen colours through `fromScreen` (sky/rig.ts), the inverse of three's Neutral tone curve, which crushed #2c315f to a saturated (12, 25, 85): lit #1c2041, shade #11142d, belly #0d1024. Stops moved to E = −0.04 / 0.01 / 0.07 / 0.2 / 0.42, so mauve and peach sit inside the dusk band (lbDuskAt > 0.8) and clouds are 80 % indigo where lbNightAt reaches 0.5 (spec'd). At night the rim is only a thin moon-side line (fresnel > 0.8). Measured on `night`: cloud median ~(28, 32, 65) against a night ground of ~(2, 22, 19); the ground is darker than the old target assumed, so the ratio is ~3× in linear luminance, but the clouds are now dark slate silhouettes and the city lights are the brightest thing on the night side.
- [A3] Inside the air a cloud is never more than one palette step (0.07 in sun-elevation sine) darker than the eye's own sky: on a 160 m planet a cloud 100 m away is ~36° further into the night and hung as a navy storm blob in a lilac dusk sky.
- [A3] Moonlight fill #4e58ac × 1.1/π (param `sky.moon`; was #6670c8 × 1.25): the night countryside goes from mid-green (5, 45, 28) to a dark (2, 22, 19). Note for B4: the issue also asked to mix the night albedo 30 % toward its luminance; that belongs in render/toon.ts FRAG_FILL (A3 can only colour the fill). Note for A1: the night sea still reads blue-teal around the city disc (darken 45 → 65 %).
- [A3] Night occluder (sky/index.ts): a disc across the planet perpendicular to the sun, 0.085·R past the terminator plane, drawn into the shadow map only (colorWrite and depthWrite off, double-sided, LAYER_NO_INK), and only while the visible cap reaches past it. On this planet a roof or crown 5–10 m up keeps the sun to 14–20° below its local horizon, so sun-facing facets lit up under a starry sky. Roofs in the dusk band still catch alpenglow; past the cut there is no direct light.
- [A3] Shadow camera depth range fitted to what the eye can see: from R + 95 (above the planes) down to the lowest visible receiver along the sun (visible cap round the focus + 0.3 rad for towers past the horizon, − 12 m), quantised to 4 m. The planet's far hemisphere, whose back faces filled the whole map each frame, now falls past the far plane. PCF radius 1 texel near the ground (three's per-pixel rotated Vogel taps showed as a stipple across a 2-texel penumbra), easing to 2 above ~120 m.
- [A3] Perf finding for core / C2 (not changed, core owns `shadowMapSize`): on the M3 (ANGLE/Metal) the shadow update costs ~4 ms at 2048² against ~1 ms at 1024², with or without casters and with any depth format (u16, u24s8, u32, f32), so it is per-texel cost of the target itself, not geometry. 1536 on `high` would likely recover ~2 ms at street level.
- [A3] Cloud LOD: three levels (near 44×26 sphere ≤ 64 puffs within 70/82 m of the surface, far 20×12, tiny 12×8 beyond 165/150 m), whole clusters culled by the planet's horizon and the view frustum (inflated bounding sphere), survivors compacted to the front of the buffers and uploaded only up to the used count (`addUpdateRange`). The near level is sorted front to back each frame (early-z). Cloud triangles: orbit 198k → 60k, city → 11k, street 227k → 5k.
- [A3] The dive's cloud: the body (radius 8, crown capped at 46 m) stands 15 m beside the track on the side away from the city, so it stays at the frame edge through the reveal; a separate bare cloudlet of five small puffs (`CloudAnchor.bare` / `extra` / `top`, additive) sits on the track with its bulk below and outboard, and the eye cuts its inner top corner and leaves through its flat base. Cloud coverage of the frame stays ≤ 17 % (all puffs) before u = 0.40 (measured with param `clouds.mask`, flat magenta); veil ≥ 0.99 for 6 frames at 30 fps (0.2 s), then out through 3 frames of thinning base mist.
- [A3] White-out rework (supersedes the previous one): the veil overlay is driven by being INSIDE a puff, with the eye treated as a ball of 1.3 × the near plane (so the overlay is closed before the near plane can slice a puff open; the near-plane dither is gone), ramping 1.12 → 0.92 of the radius; the scene fog closes toward the mist only within 5 m of a surface (and 4 m under a cluster's base, which also feeds the overlay at 0.85 so leaving through a base thins instead of cutting). Puff surfaces within 7 m of the eye melt into the mist colour (no hard sphere edge across the veil). Wisps exist only for veil > 0.3 (gone the moment the eye is out); the fill carries slow "shells" (big soft masses streaming outward with distance flown) so the inside of a cloud moves past instead of being a flat card. The mist colour (`mistColor`) is a cool grey-lilac between the cloud's shade and belly, below the sky's brightness; the sky dome sinks into it by `SkyService.mist`.
- [A3] Contract (additive): `SkyService.cloudsInView?` (clouds → sky: the painted cumulus fade) and `SkyService.mist?` (clouds → sky: how far the scene fog is closed into the cloud mist; ≥ veil).
- [A3] The mist slab: over the city (plan radius < 72 m, fading out by 92 m), where no cloud floats, a player moving through the layer (sea altitude 31.5 → 38.5 in, 44 → 49 out) crosses the same veil by altitude, only if the eye entered the band while moving (setView and shots never start it) and fading out (0.6 s) if it lingers with < 1.5 m/s vertical motion. Measured with real input: wheel descent maxVeil 1.0 (3 frames full, ~0.2 s total), trackpad-style descent maxVeil 1.0 (5 frames full). No geometry, no shadow.
- [A3] Painted far cumulus: shaded with the puffs' two bands on the same palette, from the cloud's rounded body (lumps only dent it), no white outline (a thin warm sun-side edge instead), and faded out by `cloudsInView`: max over real clusters above the eye's horizon, weighted by azimuth from the view (gone within the horizontal half-FOV + 0.2 rad, back beyond + 0.6). A painted and a 3D cloud no longer share a frame; turning away from a real one brings the painted ones back smoothly.
- [A3] Moon craters re-placed off-axis (the two upper ones side by side read as eyes).

### A3 G2 fix round
- [A3 G2] White-out release (supersedes the instant exit): the veil and the fog-to-mist pull rise at once but release exponentially (veil τ 0.2 s, mist τ 0.4 s, mist ≥ veil; reset on a teleport), so leaving a cloud takes ~6 frames in the 30 fps clip: overlay first, fog last. The sky dome reads the SAME smoothed mist at draw time (`onBeforeRender`, it updates before the clouds) and one curve (`mistBlend`) pulls both the scene fog colour and the dome into the mist, so silhouettes fade into the sky instead of cutting against a gold horizon band.
- [A3 G2] Near clouds: puffs get their own fog far plane floored at 30 m while the white-out fog is closed (the scene fog at 9 m had flattened every puff within ~7 m into a lilac wall), and they melt into the mist only in the last 1–3 m. Lobe shading now carries a cloud only within ~8 m, fading to the cluster form (+ a little puff form) by 32 m; the puff sphere normal fades too (it jumps where puffs overlap), and a crease line is drawn only where two lobes' normals are > ~35° apart: no more stacked translucent discs.
- [A3 G2] Cloud palette (supersedes the dark-indigo night stops): six stops, E = −0.14 / −0.06 / 0.01 / 0.07 / 0.2 / 0.42: moonlit silver-lilac cotton at night (lit #4a4f92, shade #2b3066, belly #23275a, on screen), a blue-hour afterglow stop (lavender tops, pink-mauve bellies), then mauve, peach, cream, white. A silver fresnel rim over the cluster's whole moon-facing half. From orbit, clouds deep in the night (sun below ~−13° at the cloud) are dimmed 35 %. Inside the air a gain (`cloudLift`) keeps the belly ≥ 1.3× the sky's luminance at night/dusk. Spec'd: night lit ≥ 2.5× space, belly ≥ 1.3× space, deep-night lit far below a window.
- [A3 G2] Golden t = 0: the golden window is wider (sun sin 0.5 → 0.88, was 0.3 → 0.72), the key goes fully to gold #ffc87a (still golden by the spec: linear g/r 0.58, b/r 0.2), +10 % key / −12 % sky fill in golden light, a cream-gold fill tint, and the sky's sun glow / gold band key on the city's golden clock as well as the eye's sun (`lowSun = max(…, warm·0.85)`), plus a peach wash over the lowest sky toward the sun. Lit sidewalk in `street`: (240,211,203) → (242,199,167). Not yet the critic's (255,215,150): the lavender fill and the post grade keep it peach, not cream.
- [A3 G2] Night occluder moved from 0.085 R to 0.15 R past the terminator plane: the toon kit already fades direct sun to 0 at up·sun = −0.14, so the cut no longer slices a hard diagonal seam across the city at blue hour (at 0.085 it removed ~35 % direct light in one texel).
- [A3 G2] Orbit atmosphere: the pale inner edge is half as white, the rim 20 % desaturated, the fade into space starts sooner (0.12 of the tail, pow 0.55), the limb haze glow 0.4 → 0.24, and from orbit the limb air turns night from sun sin −0.15 (was −0.26), so the night disc is ringed in indigo with a rose/orange band only near the terminator.
- [A3 G2] Painted far cumulus 7 → 11 (every bearing) and a faint high cirrus streak band in the dome (inside the air, faded out 32 → 56 m, not faded by real clouds in view).
- [A3 G2] Not A3's: the orbit night-half "milky film" (57,50,74) measured on the G2 build came from the post grade's shadow-tint lift (B4: `post.shadowTint=0` → (1,14,25)); it is gone in the current build. The contrail arcs mid-dive are B3's. The midnight lamp-pole shadow wedges no longer reproduce at the critic's exact view and time (there is no direct light at midnight: the toon kit zeroes the sun below −0.14 and the moon casts nothing), with either occluder distance.

## Terrain, ocean, nature (A1)

- [A1] Planet retuned (world/planet.ts, spec updated): land = max of seeded angular blobs (city continent kept compact, a snowy ridge peninsula ~1 rad out across the city's line of sight, 5 composite islands east/south of the city, a far continent + archipelago) bent by one warp field, plus fine coves. ~34 % land overall (the planet is ~2/3 ocean); measured on the visible disc, the default orbit view (380 m straight over the city) is ~23 % ocean and the `orbit` shot ~29 %. 55-60 % ocean in a view centred on the city is not reachable without shrinking the plateau (the city cap is a large share of that disc and must sit on land with a guard ring), so the sea is pulled in close around it instead: beaches ~8-60 m past the plateau edge on most bearings, islands beside it.
- [A1] Plateau contract unchanged (flat cap, smooth blend: the spec's < 0.7 slope now applies to land in the ring; a bay may reach into the outer blend ring under the opaque ocean). New spec checks: no cliff within ±1.5 m of the waterline out to 60 m past the ring (slope < 2.5) and no discontinuity (< 6). Land-fraction bounds tightened to 28-42 %.
- [A1] Beach ramp and seabed both start linearly at the waterline (`easeOut`, no smoothstep plateau at c = 0): the old flat zone right at sea level flooded under the swell and smeared foam across whole beaches.
- [A1] Terrain colour per VERTEX (smooth biome contours from orbit) plus a per-facet colour (corner mean, jittered); the vertex shader blends facet → smooth colour AND facet → smooth normal over 40-130 m from the camera (params `terrain.blendNear/Far`). Orbit reads as painted continents with no per-facet terminator mosaic; up close the crisp low-poly facets stay. Colours stored as sRGB bytes, linearised in the shader.
- [A1] Shadow acne on steep slivers in low sun: terrain (and nature) fade the shadow-map term out on facets lit at < ~9° (`terrain/shader-ext.ts`, an extra onBeforeCompile on the toon material; unique patch keys). The ramp already shades those facets. Ocean shadows are 55 % strength (a tint on water, not a hole). Windmill sails opt out of receiving shadows (thin double-sided quads self-shadowed).
- [A1] `terrain/data.ts`: one cached per-vertex field pass (heights, biome, smooth normal, slope, moisture, tone, plateau weight) shared by terrain, ocean (seabed slope for the foam) and nature. ~50-75 ms of terrain init (was ~55 ms with per-face biome calls).
- [A1] Ocean: depth tint in the shader (lagoon → turquoise → deep), 3-train radial swell faded out beyond 50-160 m, flat facets near the camera from screen derivatives, foam measured in metres from the shore (depth ÷ seabed slope per vertex, so even width on flat and steep shores) with a lapping edge and broken rolling lines, a soft toon sun glint, and anti-aliased sparkle points. One extra attribute pair (aDepth, aGrad); no textures.
- [A1] Nature: every tree on the planet (A2's 'tree' features + terrain scatter, seeded per facet, standing exactly on the facet) in one toon program ('nature' patch: per-instance crown tint on tinted parts, wind sway, windmill sail spin). Kinds: two round trees (far LOD above 80 m, hysteresis 60 m), conifer (snowy high up), palm (beaches), bush (some in bloom), rock (tilted into the slope). Inside the plan the scatter only uses `classify === 'free'` + `isClear` (2-3.5 m), so it adapts to any plan. Reveal: a wave spreading out from the city over 1.7 s. Bushes and rocks stop casting shadows above 70 m.
- [A1] Ground cover (nature/ground-cover.ts): grass tufts (normals pointing up so they shade like the meadow), flowers and pebbles/shells, hashed per terrain facet (persistent), gathered within 20 m of the eye when it moves 1.5 m (the icosphere's face order gives detail-3 spatial buckets for free), shrunk into the ground between 11 and 18.5 m (no dither speckle), hidden above 22 m. In the city only on park/garden, or bare 'free' plateau clear of paving by 1 m. Caps 7000 / 1400 / 900 × density; 3 draw calls; no shadows cast.
- [A1] Landmarks (nature/landmarks.ts, picked from the terrain): three windmills on the highest gentle meadow tops 6-50 m past the blend ring (off the range), sails toward the city; a lighthouse on the coastal spot near the city with the most sea around it, with an additive beam (two sweeping cones + a billboard glow, LAYER_NO_INK, no depth write) that fades in with lbNightAt. Scatter keeps out of their footprints.
- [A1 refine] Planet: the city continent's coast now hugs the plateau (guard: land to ~20 m past the plateau edge, then a 1-2 m lagoon shelf with sandbars for ~40 m, then deep water; the old back lobe is gone), a windmill hill on the sunny west side (drier, open meadow), and a neck of meadows and woods (wetter toward the range) out to a slimmer range peninsula. Measured on the visible disc: default orbit view ~29 % ocean (was 23 %), `orbit` shot ~31 % (was 29 %). Of the `orbit` shot's disc, 37 % is the plateau and ~10 % the range: 55-60 % needs a smaller plateau or a wider orbit frame (orchestrator / A2 / A4).
- [A1 refine] Facet colour = the DOMINANT corner biome (majority, ties → median-height corner) at the facet's own slope, never the corner mean (no olive/khaki bands); snow only where all three corners are snow; steep facets (own slope > 0.19-0.25) turn rock. Jitter mostly from a low-frequency per-vertex field (~10 m; ±4 %) plus ±1.5 % per facet, so neighbours match (no checkerboard). Flower-field mask (~30 m) speckles meadow facets yellow (pink/lilac mixed into green read grey, so those live on the flower meshes).
- [A1 refine] Rock and snow facets are 'crisp' (aFaceCol.a): no facet → smooth blend at any distance (a faceted toy range from orbit instead of grey putty); snow fragments half-desaturated so the golden key doesn't turn them cream. Soft ground keeps 35 % of the smooth normal up close (`terrain.soft`).
- [A1 refine] Ocean: Schlick fresnel toward the palette sky (softened normal), half of the light by luminance (hue kept under the golden key), foam lit by luminance (white), lapping band narrower/softer near a grazing eye, foam lines domain-warped, tapered, limited to 3 m from shore and faded out 15-35 m up; the sea darkens 45 % at night and drops the fresnel; rim 0.25.
- [A1 refine] Round-tree LOD is a dither CROSS-FADE (110-170 m altTerrain), not a swap: near and far meshes share instance buffers, one program ('nature' patch) with per-material uniforms (uLod mode 1/2 = complementary dither, so each pixel is drawn by exactly one), shadows cross-fade too; outside the band only one mesh draws. Bush and rock shadows dither out over 90-150 m (uShFade, depth pass only), then stop casting: no pops in the dive.
- [A1 refine] Nature instances are sorted into 81 spatial chunks (detail-1 icosphere faces + the city's trees); each frame chunks below the horizon (counting each chunk's tallest top) or outside the view frustum inflated by 32-40 m (shadows from just outside) are dropped and the survivors compacted to the front of the instance buffers when the set changes (hysteresis). Street ~1.3 M → ~0.6 M triangles; orbit ~1.0 M → ~0.75 M.
- [A1 refine] Contract (additive): `Services.nature?: NatureService` with `collide(dir, r, out)` — trunk / boulder discs of the countryside scatter (city trees are CityIndex obstacles already). A4: call it after `cityIndex.collide` in the FPV walk (and wherever the camera must not stand in a crown).
- [A1 refine] Ground cover: tufts ×1.5 within 12 m of the eye; flowers redesigned (round six-petal heads ~2× bigger, golden centres, short light stems) and clustered: +5 per facet × mask² inside flower fields, colours in drifts from the slow tone field; nature gets a warm ground bounce on downward faces (trunks and crown undersides never near-black), lighter trunks; palm trunks are one continuous bent tube (the segments used to step).
- [A1 G1] Direct sun only where it is above the fragment's local horizon: terrain, ocean and nature multiply the sun's light by smoothstep(−0.14, 0, up·sun) inside their shadow chunk (terrain/shader-ext.ts `SUN_UP`). The ramp shades by N·L alone and the shadow map never covers the planet's bulk, so downward crown facets lit up at local midnight and sun-facing slopes glowed past the terminator. Night crowns are now ≤ the lit wall next to them (near crown 74,132,35 → 3,47,28 in night `approach`). The toon kit (render/toon.ts, B4) should take the same mask for every material: buildings still catch sun past the terminator.
- [A1 G1] Nature's warm ground bounce only while the sun is ≥ ~3° up over the fragment (it was 1 − night, so it lit crowns through dusk); vegetation (terrain + nature) gets a low-light grade (`LOW_LIGHT_GRADE`): below ~17° of sun, foliage desaturates toward a moonlit blue, full at night, so lime crowns and fields sit in the mauve dusk / moonlit light with the city. A2 lawns could append the same snippet (exported) for an exact match; today they read ~10 levels greener at night.
- [A1 G1] Ocean from orbit: the sea is lit through a soft knee (full sun ≈ its albedo; the 3.3 key light pushed the blue channel into the tone mapper's shoulder: pale cyan), takes half the kit's hemisphere fill (`ocean.fill` 0.5), and from altitude the sky fresnel is only a thin limb at grazing angles < ~10° (`ocean.fresnel` for the near one). Turquoise is a shore band (deep blue from ~5 m; the lagoon tint less milky); far glint = a crisp amber ellipse (~25 px from orbit) instead of a soft white blob. The remaining cyan band on the day-side limb is the sky's additive limb haze (A3).
- [A1 G1] Sand albedo #FCF096 / wet #EEDA88 (was the palette #F6D98B): under the golden key and lavender fill lit sand lands at ≈ (245, 210, 134) on screen, the palette sand, instead of salmon.
- [A1 G1] Ground cover never grows on the paths the city draws over lawns: park / plaza walk edges, the market lane and the house garden paths (re-derived like city/ground.ts, `doorX`) are indexed as plan segments (nature/paths.ts), half-width + 0.25 m rejected. A GroundClass 'path' from A2's classify() would make this generic.
- [A1 G1] Bush and rock shadows: their material casts with FRONT faces (three's default back faces put the undersides, level with the ground, into the map: hollow ring shadows); bushes sink 8 cm + 10 % of their height. Small-caster shadows now fade out over 60-110 m (was 90-150).
- [A1 G1] Countryside structure (terrain/data.ts `field` / `hedge`, colors.ts `FIELDS`): a domain-warped 3D Voronoi farmland patchwork (cells ~24 m) on gentle grass/meadow above 1.4 m, always around the town (the plateau outskirts and blend ring, cityAng 0.4 … edge + 0.12 rad) and elsewhere where a slow mask says so; per-cell pasture / hay / barley / flower-meadow tints (no yellow crops: they read as beach), hedgerow-green border facets with stretched bush segments snapped onto the border and turned along it (NatureFlag.Hedge, `stretch`), the odd hedgerow tree; wild meadow gathers its trees into groves (tone hollows ×5.5) instead of an even sprinkle. Plateau base facets get meadow tone patches + flower speckle, so the first frame reads as landscape before the city's ground arrives.
- [A1 G1] Crown spacing: scatter rejects a tree whose crown centre is closer than 0.8 × (rA + rB) to an earlier one (0.62 in forests; 8 m hash), city trees included as obstacles (spec'd). ~3.9 k nature instances (1.58 k trees, 2.05 k bushes incl. hedges).
- [A1 G1] Zero-alloc nature update: chunk culling is skipped while the camera's matrices are unchanged, compaction no longer builds an array per kind, ground-cover gathers copy with loops instead of subarray() views. Sampled nature allocation ~2 KB → < 50 B per frame (alloc-nature.mjs).
- [A1 G2] Terrain colour: no per-facet flower speckle any more (it tinted whole facets yellow: a triangle quilt under the dive's money frames). Flower fields warm the smooth and facet colour by ≤ 12 % toward #C8D860 at the mask's ≥ 20 m scale; the blooms are ground-cover meshes only. Per-facet jitter ±1 % (value) / ±0.4 % (warmth), the low-frequency field ±3.5 %; meadow tone ramps widened (no staircase on flat facets). The plateau base and the town's outskirts (plateau weight) use lawn-green field variants (`TOWN_FIELDS`, A2's garden green #8ACF55 as the city base) instead of hay / barley.
- [A1 G2] Terrain and ocean draw only the visible chunks (terrain/chunked.ts): the 320 detail-2 faces of the icosphere are contiguous index ranges; each frame (skipped while the camera holds still) chunks below the horizon (with their highest point) or outside the inflated frustum (terrain 40 m for shadows cast into view, ocean 2 m) are dropped and the runs merged into ≤ 4 drawRange views of the same buffers, so both passes follow the view. Terrain + ocean triangles: street 245,760 → 27,648, rooftops → 42,496, orbit → 166,656; draw calls +9 at most (3 → ≤ 12).
- [A1 G2] SUN_UP (direct light only above the local horizon) now lives in the toon kit for every material (B4); terrain/ocean/nature no longer apply it twice. LOW_LIGHT_GRADE stays out of the twilight band (sun −11.5° … +15° over the fragment), where B4's FRAG_DUSK_FOLIAGE owns foliage: the two desaturations stacked into neutral grey at the terminator.
- [A1 G2] Crowns and bushes: cauliflower crowns of 3-4 overlapping lumps (the clouds' language, low jitter) and 3-lump bushes; geometry carries the ellipsoid normal (`aSmoothN`) and the colour pass blends to 92 % smooth within 35 m of the eye, fading to the crisp facets by 70 m (0.75 still read as faceted under the toon ramp). Orbit and the cloud layer keep the low-poly look.
- [A1 G2] Windmills: stone footing, framed dark-blue windows that glow warm at night (aTint −1 = a night-glow flag in the nature program), a framed door with a step, a stage gallery with railing, a wooden cap rim, the cap tinted per mill (palette roofs), a six-bladed fantail, latticed sails (two clothed, two reefed). Heights ×1 / 0.86 / 1.14 and sail speeds vary per mill.
- [A1 G2] Lighthouse beam alpha is computed per fragment in the cone's own cross-section: the eye's path length through the cone ((1 − u²)², u = the axis offset seen from the eye) × (1 − s^0.7)^1.3 along it, ≤ 0.17 per wall (≤ 0.34 with both). Interpolated vertex normals streaked the long thin triangles. Far sun glint = a soft core plus 5-9 twinkling sparkle points on a coarse world lattice along the sun path (the crisp ellipse read as a hole in the sea).
- [A1 G2] Boats (nature/boats.ts): 4 toy sailboats and a fishing boat on seeded closed-form ellipse loops (3 off the city coast at 96-148 m from its centre, a sailboat and the fishing boat by the lighthouse), every loop checked ≥ 0.9 m deep with a 2.5 m margin (spec'd), bobbing, pitching and rolling on the ocean's own swell (ocean/swell.ts: GLSL + CPU twin), masthead lamps and the wheelhouse window glow at night, foam wakes draped over the swell (one transparent instanced mesh, LAYER_NO_INK). Loops are found before any nature mesh enters the scene: a yield between adding meshes and ctx.compile() let a frame build four programs mid-flow. No birds (B3's air could own a flock).
- [A4] Hint row sits on a faint ink backing (rgba(7,11,26,0.34)): white mono text alone vanished over pale pavement.

## City (A2)

- [A2] Layout: a pentagonal plaza loop (5 corners at r ≈ 24 m, gently bowed sides), 5 avenues swirling out from its corners to meet a wobbly ring road (r ≈ 60 m) along its normal (clean T junctions), houses with gardens outside the ring. The brief's "warped downtown grid" does not fit a 90 m plateau with 10-11 m road corridors: a 2×2 or 8-avenue grid left 20 m blocks and ~50 % of the disc as asphalt. Five sector blocks (~29 m deep) keep roads under ~30 % and fit back-to-back buildings.
- [A2] Park (a 70° arc) and stadium sit in the outer band between the ring and the plateau rim (not inside a block): the inner blocks stay downtown and the park runs out into A1's countryside. No coastal road (optional, would leave the plateau).
- [A2] Buildings are placed per street side of each block (plaza side first, then ring, then avenues), fronts on the sidewalk's outer edge, sides run 3 m past their ends into the corners; a second pass fills gaps with narrow attached buildings (gap 6 cm: a terrace). Recursive lot subdivision was tried and dropped (slivers on the curved sector blocks). Houses use the outer face's whole outline (continuous round the T junctions) and get a garden lot each (kind 'garden', non-overlapping).
- [A2] Plan budget: placement through a flat hash grid of keep-out capsules (road corridors decimated to ~3 m chords, +3 cm), junction discs and footprints; connector conflicts tested on every other sample with a +0.2 m margin (conservative) after a bounding-box reject; layout curves sampled at ~0.9 m. Warm ≈ 15-25 ms, cold first run ≈ 45-65 ms on the (heavily shared) dev machine — see the A2 report.
- [A2] Contract (additive): GroundClass 'lot' (paved downtown block ground) and 'water' (a pond; wins over the park it sits in), both drawn at AREA_H (+2 cm for water) by groundH. Before this, lots and the pond classified as 'free'/'park' and A1's ground cover grew grass on paving and in the pond. Feature kinds 'lamp' (plaza globe lamps, collision r 0.2) and 'hydrant' (r 0.18); A2 renders both.
- [A2] Viewpoints: `street` on an avenue sidewalk by the curb looking in toward the plaza (scored by the towers ahead), `rooftops`, `horizon` (ring's outer sidewalk, along the ring), `dusk` (the most westward sidewalk view with room around it, an open run ahead and no lamp post in the middle), and `plaza` (at the plaza's edge on an entry path, looking at the clock tower).
- [A2] Rendering: everything static in two merged meshes sharing one toon program (patch 'city-v1'): buildings + props (cast + receive) and ground (receive only), plus one additive mesh of light pools (LAYER_NO_INK, no depth write). Per-vertex aReveal / aBase drive the reveal (streets pop from their centres outward, then buildings spring up centre-out); procedural detail from aInfo (u, v, kind, param): window grids with lintel shadows and night glow, slab joints, plaza tiles, asphalt speckle, roof tiles / gravel, lawn stripes, water, clock faces, blinking aviation light. Patterns fade to their average below ~3 px (fwidth), so nothing shimmers from orbit.
- [A2] Asphalt is drawn a touch lighter and warmer than PALETTE.road.asphalt (lerp 32 % toward #7A7570): the raw value read navy-black in shadow under the cool fill and the streets swallowed the 120 m view. B4 may move this into the palette.
- [A2] Flat roofs use muted membrane colours by roofColor (clay / slate / grey) or a roof garden (lawn, planters, a parasol) instead of the pale trim colour: from above the town read as white boxes.
- [A2] Merged city meshes draw first (renderOrder −2 / −1): all LITTLEBIG meshes sit at the origin, so three's opaque sort tied and drew the terrain first, then overdrew it with the city ground; city first lets early-z reject the terrain under it. Roof props stay ≤ 3 m above Building.h (water tanks, spire finial), spec'd with the rest of the box (city/buildings.spec.ts).
- [A2] The `street` landing spot keeps 1.8 m clear of every point obstacle (A4's dive spec checks lamp/trunk clearance on the final approach).
- [A2 refine] Layout breaks the five-fold symmetry: each avenue gets its own swirl (0.04–0.42 rad) and tangent lengths; the ring is an oval (±3.4 m) round a centre nudged 1–2 m off the plaza; 2–3 cul-de-sacs (kind 'lane', 5.5 m, turning circle ρ 5.4 m) run off the ring into the houses where the band is ≥ 24.5 m deep. The coastal road stays out: it would leave the plateau (the 'everything inside the plateau' invariant) onto A1's blend terrain.
- [A2 refine] Dead ends: RoadNode.radius = √(ρ² − half²) so the curbs meet the turning circle; the U-turn connector runs round the circle at ρ − 1.6 (circle centre on the left); a 'corner' walk edge rings the circle's sidewalk; block outlines follow it (blocks.ts).
- [A2 refine] Junction corner fillet +2.5 → +0.6 m on the patch radius (less apron); paint fills the big patches: turn arrows on every approach lane (from its connectors' turns), stop lines from the centre line to the edge line's inner edge, dashed guide lines continuing the major road through the junction.
- [A2 refine] Contract (additive): `Area.h` (surface height when not AREA_H; CityIndex.groundH returns it). Downtown lots and the plaza are paved flush with the sidewalks (ROAD_H + CURB_H − 4 mm), courtyard lawns are raised beds (BED_H = sidewalk + 0.25). classify: water first, lots last (beds, squares and lanes inside a lot win). `Building.decor` ('cafe') and `Building.door` (entrance x). Feature kinds 'planter' (r 0.5), 'cafe-table' (0.45), 'statue' (0.8). isClear now also tests corner/sidewalk walk edges.
- [A2 refine] Connector `conflicts` are computed lazily per node on first read (a non-enumerable getter, frozen arrays): only traffic needs them, so they are off the first-frame path. Placement: frontage buildings test neighbours before road capsules, corridor capsules are ~4.5 m chords padded by their exact sag, a far-end precheck rejects taken frontage, viewpoint sight lines use the placement grid. Browser cold `city plan` (plan + dev freeze + index) ≈ 30–33 ms on the loaded dev machine (load avg 7–10), 21–24 ms warm; see the A2 refine report.
