- [A2 refine] Blocks: one downtown block is cut by a pedestrian market lane (walk edge 'footpath', 3.6 m, cobbles) lined with small terraced shops; one has a church (landmark 'church', 19 m with spire) on a little square with a statue; frontage buildings are deepened into the interior where they fit, a back row of courtyard buildings fills the rest, then raised lawn beds with trees and benches, then planter trees. Measured inside r 56 m: buildings ≈ 24 %, lot paving ≈ 22–24 % (35 % was not reachable with rectangles on these curved sector blocks without crushing courtyards; the remaining paving is warm terracotta setts with beds, planters and café tables, not grey car park).
- [A2 refine] Street level: every block/tower ground floor is recessed 0.62 m under the floors above (soffit, ledge, corner columns), with shopfronts (mullions, transoms) on every face that meets a street (CityIndex probe 2.2 m out), sparse windows elsewhere, a door with a canopy on the front, downpipes; shops wrap awnings onto street-facing sides and put ad panels on blank sides; residential shops are gabled corner-shop houses. Window glass is recessed in the shader (eye ray cast onto a plane 5–30 cm behind the wall in the fragment's own tangent frame): lit sill, shaded lintel soffit, jambs bright on the sun side.
- [A2 refine] `street` viewpoint: on an avenue sidewalk looking in toward the clock tower along a bend, with a frontage building ahead on the right that becomes the café (awning wrapping round, blade sign, lettered fascia, tables with parasols, planters); no lamp post within 6 m or tree within 10 m of the view's centre line, and street trees in the first 24 m of the view are not planted. `plaza` viewpoint stands 21–31 m from the clock tower (on the loop's outer sidewalk) so the clock is in frame at eye height, offset so the fountain sits beside the tower.
- [A2 refine] Night: facade far-average glow ×1.25 rising to ×2.3 with distance (was ×0.5), glow colour (1.0, 0.55, 0.22), 72 % of house windows lit; light pools brighten with distance; one Points draw (LAYER_NO_INK, additive) of lamp heads, ~1.5k window sparks and house porch lights, ≥ 3.5 px, fading in past 22–45 m. Clock hands follow the sun's hour angle (t = 0 ≈ 4:16 pm; minute hand once per DAY_LENGTH / 24 s).
- [A2 refine] Paint is its own mesh (same 'city-v2' program) with polygonOffset (−1, −4); road ribbons tuck 5 cm under junction patches (patch asphalt = road asphalt), sidewalk strips 3 cm. Reveal: ground and paint rise out of the plateau per vertex by distance (a wave, no per-tile pops); buildings cascade by distance rank over 1.5 s, towers 0.25 s later and slower (per-vertex aGrow), zero-sized before their delay. Houses with green roofs use a deep teal (#2F7F78) so they don't vanish against gardens.

### A2 G1 fix round

- [A2 G1] Window sparks sit on the facade's own lit windows: Geo records every facade quad (`Geo.facades`), shader.ts has a float32 CPU twin of the window hash (`facadeWindows`), each spark is 5 cm proud of its wall, depth-tested, pulled a hair toward the eye, and shows only where its wall faces the camera and the drawn window pattern is under ~3 px (bay or floor). Nothing floats off tiers, insets or recessed shop floors any more. The facade's far-average glow drops to ×0.75 (no distance boost): the sparks carry the lit windows from afar.
- [A2 G1] Light pools: amber #FFB84D rim → #FFD9A0 core, falloff (1 − r²)² / (1 + 7r²), r ≈ 4.2–5.3 m with ±25 % gain per lamp, no stacking where pools overlap (each pool is weighted by its share of the total per vertex: Σ wᵢkᵢ ≤ max k), fading out between 110 and 170 m of camera altitude. Lamp-head points: a ~2.6 px hot core in a soft warm halo, 5–9 px sprites.
- [A2 G1] Viewpoints `rooftops`, `horizon` and `dusk` are computed on first read (non-enumerable memoised getters on `plan.viewpoints`) with sight-line tests from world/city/views.ts (a Skyline of roofs, crowns, lamp heads, flags): off the `city plan` boot mark. `dusk` stands where the horizon is unbroken within ±4° of the sun at the shot's own time (terrain beyond the plateau included) and turns the view ≤ 10° toward the street; `horizon` keeps its column 3 m from every lamp head / crown / wall and nothing tall in a ±38° cone for 10 m; `rooftops` stands 58–70 m from the clock tower over open ground with clear lines to the tower's top, middle and foot.
- [A2 G1] core/shots.ts: the `rooftops` shot pitches to −24° (the altitude curve's −35° at 16 m puts everything above the eye out of frame, so no tower can ever be whole); from the new viewpoint the plateau's curve drops the whole clock tower into view. One-line, shot-only change.
- [A2 G1] Awnings are one mitred run (`awningWrap`): front and street sides meet on the corner of their offset lines, no gap or sliver; the pitch is shallow (drop = 0.4 × reach) so the awning plane meets a 1.9 m eye only 3+ m out, past the sidewalk — the landing frame stood exactly in the old plane and saw the front awning as a thin line. Café corner columns dropped (they cut the wrap). Spec: every awning vertex within 1.6 m of its wall run.
- [A2 G1] Stadium: the arcade's deep passage now shows the dark interior where the eye ray gets through the arch (the reveal faces used to fill the whole arch as four lit / dark triangles), and the arch's rectangle overlaps its half-disc (no one-pixel seam). Collision: `footprintDistance` / `stadiumRing` (world/city/index-grid.ts) give the superellipse wall the mesh is built from; CityIndex.collide, roofAt, buildingsNear use it, and the four floodlight masts are point obstacles. obbDistance (the box) stays the placement / validation shape. Spec'd. Camera's `buildingDistance` (camera/landing.ts) and `keepOffWalls` (camera/index.ts) still use the box: A4 should switch them to `footprintDistance`.
- [A2 G1] Minors: downtown lot setts #BF9A80 → #D9BBA6 (warm sand-pink); courtyards take up to 9 beds, 14 tree planters and 8 flower planters per block; blank shop sides over ~20 m² get a full window row, a downpipe and an ad panel; street lamps have a rounded cobra head (three chamfered slabs) over a recessed lens.

### A2 G2 fix round

- [A2 G2] Night constellation: from 110–230 m up the asphalt glows amber (×0.2 → 0.3 toward orbit, × night) as filaments — brightest down the carriageway's middle, exp(−5·(d/half)²), fading to the curbs (ribbon param = half width), soft knots on the junction patches (param = −reach, uv relative to the node), the glow showing through the road paint (paint param 7) and a faint 0.025 on sidewalks. Light pools fade out over 120–200 m (was 110–170). Lamp-head sprites grow from 150 to 320 m up to a 4.6 px core in a 15 px halo (×0.8). Facade far glow ×0.75 → ×1.15 over 150–300 m. A broad-pool variant (pools kept from orbit with a flat profile) read as bokeh discs with gaps and was dropped.
- [A2 G2] Pools reach the pavement: 12 × 12 grid per pool (was 8), each vertex lifted to its highest 4-neighbour's ground, so a cell spanning the curb is drawn at the sidewalk (never under it); pool centre 0.3 m off the pole toward the road (was 0.94 m), radius +0.4 m.
- [A2 G2] Night water (pond, fountain, rooftop pools: one water kind): mix toward a moonlit navy #1c2a5a with the ripples and a few twinkling moon glints on the crests; the day look is unchanged.
- [A2 G2] Lit windows: per-window hash colour (60 % warm amber, 25 % soft yellow, 10 % TV-blue, 5 % dimmed), brightness 0.6–1, darker toward the ceiling, 12 % blinds (top band) / 10 % curtains (side bands) — pale fabric by day; glow ×1.35 → ×1.15. Lit share ×0.8 late at night (shops ×0.85) from a shared `uCityLate` uniform (night 60 s ago × 40 s ahead, B2's rule); the window sparks carry the same colour (aTint) and lit hash and drop out by the same rule. `cityPatch(ground, late)` takes the shared uniform (additive, internal).
- [A2 G2] Placeholders: ad panels and roof billboards are illustrated posters on an ink border (sunset over the sea, a curling wave, a soda bottle with a bold title, a two-colour diagonal stripe with a dot); shop, corner-shop and café fascias carry glyph-like lettering (words of x-height blocks with ascenders and a capital, ink chosen by board luminance); stadium floodlights are dark lamp banks of 2 × 4 lamps tipped 26° down toward the pitch (they read as blank scoreboards); the church-square statue is a warm bronze figure in a long coat with a hat, one arm raised holding a torch (it read as a green cactus).
- [A2 G2] Dusk viewpoint: the sun's ±1.5° (the disc) must show an unbroken horizon instead of ±4°, so silhouettes may frame it; the quick reject uses the same limit; preferred a straight sidewalk along a street running into the sunset (+4), a streetlight 7–30° off-axis (+1.5), distance from any turning circle (+3 over 7–14 m), building mass on either side; nothing standing within 8 m in a ±14° cone. It now stands at an avenue's mouth on the ring, looking across into the sunset between tree silhouettes with walkers and houses, not into the plum cul-de-sac disc. House doors: the awning colour a third toward the wall colour, saturation ≤ 50 %.
- [A2 G2] GLSL in city/shader.ts and city/pools.ts carries no comments any more (≈ 4.3 KB raw shipped in the bundle); the notes moved to the TS header.

## Air (B3)

- [B3] Air traffic is closed-form in sim time (air/routes.ts): planes on tilted great circles with a lateral S-weave (that weave is what makes them bank, atan(a_lat / g) × 1.5, capped at 0.55 rad), balloons on slow loops round the outskirts. No fixedUpdate, no onTimeJump: same t ⇒ same sky, nothing spawns, nothing teleports. Contrail ribbons are evaluated on the GPU from the same route formula at past θ, so a trail lies exactly where its plane flew at zero CPU cost.
- [B3] Planes use 63-80 m of the 58-80 m band: cumulus crowns reach ~61 m with A3's bulges (puff top 58 m × the 1.27 bulge reach), so the lowest plane flies at 63.3 m with its engines at ~61.5 m (spec'd against the cloud layout). Routes are stacked 5.4 m apart, since any two great circles cross twice (spec: 14 m apart, or ≥ 4.5 m stacked, over an hour).
- [B3] Two of the four routes cross the city (planes pass over the `street` view and the dive's landing at t = 0, then every ~85 s), two sweep the rest of the planet as accents from orbit. Three hot-air balloons, no blimp: they sit below the cloud base (tops ≤ 34 m) and ≥ 4 m above every roof and mast on their loops (spec'd with CityIndex.maxRoofNear).
- [B3] Sun on things in the air (`airLit`): planes, contrails and balloons keep the sun ~10° past the ground's terminator, not the geometric 45° a 65 m height gives on this planet. The geometric version hung sunlit contrails like searchlights across the night side of the `night` view.
- [B3] Never flown through: a plane within 27 m of the eye (balloon: 22 m) is pushed smoothly away to ≥ 9 m (7.5 m); a balloon never sinks to dodge (the downward part turns sideways), and contrails thin out within 14 m of the eye.
- [B3] Draw cost: 4 draws (planes and balloons share one toon program, `air-v1` patch; contrails one instanced ribbon; nav lights and burner flames one additive Points) plus 2 shadow draws. Contrails and lights are LAYER_NO_INK with no depth write.

### B3 refine round (after the critic, 7.1/10)

- [B3] One angular speed for every plane (`PLANE_LAP` 90 s, ≈ 16 m/s; weave and wobble are whole multiples of θ), so relative phases never drift and the sky repeats every lap: the spec checks one lap for all time. Supersedes the per-route speeds and the "14 m apart, or stacked ≥ 4.5 m" rule (planes 0 and 1 passed 0.4 m apart horizontally at t = 8 s, merging into one glowing wedge from orbit). Spec now: ≥ 18 m horizontal separation at every moment, no stacking exemption (actual minimum 43 m).
- [B3] Routes re-laid by a scored search (separation, planes in frame from orbit and above the street horizon): headings 344°, 230°, 6°, 132° on four circles (no more eastbound squadron). Over the town a plane passes at t = 0 (route 1, still in the `street` shot's sky), 17.5, 47.5 and 70 s of every lap (spec: gaps 15-50 s). Above the street horizon (buildings ignored) no plane 3 % of the time (was 54 %, longest gap 60 s → 3 s); from orbit over the city never 0 planes (was 11 %).
- [B3] Things in the air take the eye's time of day inside the air, A3's cloud rule (`airSunE`): never more than 0.07 in sun-elevation sine darker than the eye's dip-aware sky (`uAirEye`, computed like clouds/index.ts), the eye's exactly within 60 m, their own beyond 200 m and from orbit. Planes, balloons, nav lights and contrails share it, so a plane past the terminator seen from the sunlit city is no longer a black cut-out with lit trails. Where the kit had cut the sun (its cut follows the ground), the key light comes back through the same toon ramp (`getGradientIrradiance`, unshadowed: nothing shadows a plane but itself).
- [B3] The air patch replaces the kit's hemisphere fill with its own: down-facing surfaces trade 85 % of the warm ground bounce for a cool sky fill (the underside read salmon-brown from the street). BELLY #E6EAF6; a livery belly stripe and wing-root flashes on the underside. Measured underside from the street: (200, 212, 245) on screen (was tan-brown).
- [B3] Plane face: a wrap-around windscreen visor (a band between two elevations seen from a point inside the nose) with pane posts; intakes get a light metal lip and a grey fan disc with 8 twisted blades (paint), so the engines no longer read as tyres.
- [B3] Night: light cores exp(−6r²), min sizes nav 7 / strobe 11 / beacons 8 px (halved toward orbit so a beacon stays a spark), ~70 ms flat-topped strobes, a belly beacon in antiphase with the top one (the light seen from the street), logo lights on the fin, liveries 50 % toward luminance under the moon (amber went mud-brown), a moonlit fresnel rim on the silhouette.
- [B3] Balloons: envelope profile through a Catmull-Rom spline (12 → 34 rings, +~8k triangles for three); the flame is a tall teardrop above the can (it sat inside it, depth-hidden); ropes, basket rim and can glow in bursts only (aGlow < 0). The burst glow fades out over 60-160 m and the steady lantern to 10 % (from orbit a burst read as a fireball strobing over the city); flames vanish beyond ~170 m.
- [B3] Contrails thin out end-on (`smoothstep(0.15, 0.5, |T × V|)`, the searchlight beams), beyond the planet's limb (to 15 %), and from orbit (alpha × 0.6, visible length 55 %: B4's "rings round the globe"); colour kept ≤ 0.96.
- [B3] GLSL strings carry no comments (they ship in the bundle, ~1.5 KB gz): the notes moved to TS doc comments above each shader.
- [B3 G2] Contrails (supersede the refine round's length and width rules): the visible trail is capped by screen length as well as age. A segment fades out between 12 and 25 % of the frame width from its plane, the old end fades quadratically over the last 40 % of the age span (55 % of it from orbit), and the width tapers with those fades. The trail grows only to σ ≈ 0.35 m, and its twin engine lines converge to 35 % spacing by 5 s. A trail fades out as its plane leaves the frame (gone ~10° past the edge), and segments behind the eye are dropped. Mid-dive the 9 s trail had been a white comet sweeping a third of the frame, and from the rooftops an off-screen plane's arc read as a lens scratch.
- [B3 G2] Trail shape: for the first 0.5 s it is the flown path, then by 2.5 s it eases onto the great circle tangent to the plane's current heading, so the trail is a straight line behind the plane, not the S-weave. The old trail swings with the heading (≤ ~7°/s). I tried and rejected the critic's literal "un-weaved great circle". The weave moves a plane up to 24 m sideways and turns its heading up to 24°, so the trail would kink. Converging to the current lateral offset bent a trail that had been straight (the street shot).
- [B3 G2] Plane dodge (`dodgeAcross`): the push now runs across the plane's own flight, along the part of (P − E) orthogonal to its nose, with a 0.25 m local-up tie-break. Its size is m = dmin·(1 − (d/reach)²)², which is flat at 0 and at reach. A plane on a collision course rises and slides past, never closer than ~dmin. The old straight-away push held a head-on plane in front of the eye, then jumped it ~16 m behind (3.5 km/s²). Probe at the critic's setup: route 1 minD 8.2 m (dmin 8.3 at scale 0.92), max second difference 61 m/s² at 15 Hz; route 0 9.0 m, 56 m/s². Spec'd at 16 m/s through and beside the eye: ≥ 8.6 m, ≤ 40 m/s² at 60 Hz. Balloons keep the radial `dodge` (slow, and never sinking).

## People and street life (B2)

- [B2] Walkers are persistent agents in plan space on the walk graph (people/sim.ts, pure, zero-alloc step). They steer at a point 1.1 m ahead, offset right of travel, and pick the lateral offset as a 1D interval problem: props, idlers, oncoming people and the player are intervals to stay out of. They follow slower people, yield to crossers by id, never go off the paving except on a crossing, and turn back after ~3.5 s stuck. Same t ⇒ same crowd: time jumps re-scatter from a hash of t and settle 1.5 s of sim.
- [B2] Crossings: wait at the kerb while `crossings.blocked` (or outside a per-crossing 6-of-13 s walk window, so people cross in little groups), write `busy` from commit until past the far kerb. No contract change.
- [B2] Lateral room per walk edge is asymmetric (`hiR` / `hiL`), narrowed where the paving under an edge is narrower than its width: A2's 30 m 'corner' edges run onto the intersection patch at their ends (lat ≥ 0.6 m classifies 'intersection'), so a walker at its preferred offset stepped onto the road there.
- [B2] Idlers are static and animate only in the shader: bench and café sitters, bus-stop waiters, a ring round the fountain, pairs/trios chatting on the plaza, church square and park lawns. Their footprints are obstacles for walkers.
- [B2] One toon program (`people` patch) for people (two LODs: full figure within 11 m, ~half the segments beyond), dogs and their leashes (one LineSegments, LAYER_NO_INK). Instances are packed every frame from the people in a view cone (draw count and vertex cost follow the screen). Pose, gait (phase from distance walked: feet never slide), head glance at a nearby player and per-person look (texture row) are all in the vertex shader.
- [B2] LOD: dithered in by `altTerrain` from 31 m down to 25 m (was 32 → 23: the dither band read as noise for too long). Personal space: someone within 0.6–1.2 m of the eye (3D, to the body's middle) dithers out with their shadow instead of filling the frame; on a camera cut to street level (eye jumps > 4 m), walkers in a 1.6 m disc or a 5 m corridor along the view step along their own edge out of it (invisible: the whole view just changed).
- [B2] The colour material sets `PP_COLOR`: three defines DEPTH_PACKING for the fragment stage only, so the vertex patch could not tell the shadow program from the colour one (the depth program has no transformedNormal / vNormal and failed to link).
- [B2 refine] Crossing contract made strict: nobody commits while `blocked` is up; someone committed but still on the kerb steps back if it comes up; commit ends 0.3 m past the far kerb (no busy flicker); an uncommitted walker can never be shoved off the kerb; the stuck turn-back keeps `commit` only if the walker is on the carriageway. Spec: 120 s against fake traffic blocking each crossing ~60 % of the time in 2–5 s bursts → busy never rises on a step where blocked is 1 (was 721 times in 300 s).
- [B2 refine] Kerb waiters stand in the right half (pref ≥ 0.8 of the room), so arrivals pass on their left; queueing counts toward giving up slowly (~17 s); the last 1.5 m of a crossing never counts as stuck; crossers keep inside the painted strip (lateral room width/2 − 0.5).
- [B2 refine] Dogs are followers inside PeopleSim (the separate stepper and clearAround are gone): ahead on the owner's left, at heel while the owner stands or waits at a kerb, never onto the carriageway unless the owner is committed.
- [B2 refine] Toy proportions (figure.ts J): hip 0.68, knee 0.36, head r 0.20 (¼ of the height), limbs ~1.5× thicker, mitten hands, chunky shoes, per-person torso width 0.9–1.16 (shader). Hair is one shell per style whose hairline follows the azimuth (fringe line in front, over the ears, nape behind; long hair stretched down the back), plus a side-swept fringe, per-person brow tilt and blush.
- [B2 refine] Three person LODs by FOV-scaled distance (10 / 21 m at 70°): 3700 / 1230 / 320 tris (the far one boxes and balls with the same parts and colours, umbrella and hat kept). The mid LOD keeps the smile. Part visibility and colour are one GLSL const table (PT); the GLSL ships without comments or indentation.
- [B2 refine] No more dither in the 25–31 m band: each person pops in with the reveal spring at their own altitude in 26–30.5 m (feet-origin scale, shadows follow); nothing draws above 31 m. The near-eye dither now measures to the nearest point of the body's feet-to-head segment and reaches 1.6 m when looking down.
- [B2 refine] Night: people in a lamp pool get a warm fill (CPU: nearest streetlight / globe lamp, packed into aAnim.z); late at night (night factor both 60 s ago and 40 s ahead, so evenings stay busy) up to 40 % of walkers and 55 % of idlers are gone, toggled only while out of view (time jumps set it at once).
- [B2 refine] Idlers: café sitters at ±0.54 (front of the seat, clear of the backrests); seated dresses hide the skirt and colour the lap; the fountain has three rim sitters facing out, a kid leaning over the rim (Pose.Lean), a pair chatting with their backs to the water and two watching the jet, instead of a ring of seven.
- [B2 refine] Size: people/ bundles to 16.6 KB gzip (was 16.5) with the above added; the ≤ 11 KB target would mean dropping LODs, hair or the night work. Draw calls: 4 instanced meshes + leash (+ shadow pass) instead of 3.
- [B2 G2fix] Crossing landings always empty (crossing 143 froze crossers on the road for 80–95 s): obstacles past a walk edge's end are measured along the end tangent (a café table 0.7 m beyond a node no longer sat "at" it and stopped walkers 0.75 m short of the transition) and, where the walker turns there, left to the next edge's list; a walker already closer to its next edge near a sharp joint moves onto it; kerb waiters stand 0.5 m back and step back 0.6 m for a crosser coming off at them; a crosser held up on the crossing for 2.5 s squeezes past standing people and idlers (room +0.6 m; a brief overlap beats a jam); "held" means no headway along the path (sideways shuffling no longer hides a jam); a committed walker still on the kerb when the walk window closes waits for the next one; commit (= busy) ends 0.15 m past the far kerb (recommit only 0.2 m before it: no flicker); the walk window is 4.5 of 13 s. Idlers keep 2.5 m clear of both ends of every crossing. Spec: 20 sim-min against the live traffic sim: longest walker on a carriageway 12 s (was 74 s), longest busy 24 s (was 95 s; a busy zebra is a union of small groups). Spec bound worstOff 0.7 → 1.25 m: the crowd parting for a squeezer drifts on the pavement (offKerb stays 0).
- [B2 G2fix] Push-outs (people, props) are rate-limited: never further than the walker's own step + 1.6 m/s·dt from its last position, the rest carries over (no 0.8 m sideways jumps in a crowd).
- [B2 G2fix] Personal space: someone at a street-level eye dithers over a 0.2 m band ending at 1.0 m (1.5 m looking down), its first visible step coarse (35 % coverage), and is skipped (colour and shadow) below it: no 1/16 dot screen. A camera cut to street level (eye jump > 4 m, or a time jump while there) steps walkers out of a 1.6 m disc and a corridor ahead (5.5 m × ±1 m; 8 m × ±1.7 m for someone walking at the lens) along their own edge, to the nearest spot clear of people and props. A player standing still for 0.5 s gets a 1.15 m berth (0.75 m moving), and walkers stop a body length short of it.
- [B2 G2fix] Draw radius √(2·162·(altTerrain + 0.3)) + 30 m: past where a person's head drops below the horizon, so nobody pops in view (was 40 + 2.6·alt: 22 pops / 20 vanishes in 90 s from `horizon`); the last 6 m scale out with the reveal spring. Pop altitudes spread over 27–58 m by hash (skewed low): the crowd builds over ~1.3 s of the 30 fps dive (was 0 → 145 people in 0.3 s at 30.5–26 m). Far people stop casting shadows above 36 m. Under reduced motion both fades are a dither (no spring).
- [B2 G2fix] Allocation (dev build, sampled heap, steady state): people/ ~1.5 KB/frame of its own plus the CityIndex calls it still makes (~17 classify + ~18 collide per step, from ~460 + dogs; their internal allocation is A2's). No Math.hypot anywhere in people/ (V8 allocates its argument list); scratch objects are private classes; hot helpers take integers/objects only (doubles crossing a non-inlined call are boxed); a walker well inside its edge's precomputed paving room (and an uncommitted walker on a crossing landing checked at init, or a committed one on the carriageway) makes no CityIndex query; dogs reuse their owner's room; dogs stand on the owner's surface height (no groundH per frame); the renderer pre-culls in plan space and maps people to the sphere inline. Frame total at `street` 471 → 63 KB/frame (the rest is other systems).
- [B2 G2fix] Size: people/ grows ~2 KB gzip (sim 7.5 → 9.4, index 4.8 → 5.9 KB, type-stripped, unminified).

## Traffic (B1)

- [B1] A fixed fleet of 35 (19 cars, 9 compacts, 4 delivery trucks, 3 buses) drives a closed network of A2's lanes + connectors: nothing spawns, despawns or teleports. State is a front-bumper arc position per vehicle; a time jump (and init) replays from the seeded layout for 40 s of warm-up plus min(t, 90) s, so the same t always gives the same streets (≈ 150–200 ms of sim for t ≥ 90, time-sliced in init, synchronous on setTime).
- [B1] Car-following is IDM; junctions use grants: only the first ungranted vehicle per lane asks, highest (connector priority + time waited) first; a grant needs room for the whole body on the exit lane, no busy zebra on the way, and every earlier-granted conflicting vehicle out of the shared zone first. Hard clamps to the leader, the stop line and zone entries make overlap impossible by construction; grant order is a total order, so no deadlock cycle; a vehicle kept at a full exit re-routes.
- [B1] Conflicts are A2's list PLUS every connector pair whose bus-sized bodies touch when swept along both turns (24 pairs here, e.g. two right turns 3.2 m apart: a bus off-tracks and swings its overhangs). Found with an hour-long run under heavy pedestrian load (two buses overlapped once in 30 min). A2's VEHICLE_CLEARANCE is a centreline test and cannot see body swing, so traffic owns the stricter check.
- [B1] ~~Zebra claims~~ (a car held at its stop line set `blocked` on its zebras 9 s in every 14): removed in the refine round, see below. The last replay step of a jump still publishes `blocked`, so people placed after it never stand in front of a committed car.
- [B1] Rendering: one instanced toon mesh per kind sharing ONE program (`traffic` patch): wheels spin and the front pair steers, bodies bob, pitch under braking and roll in turns (all in the vertex shader from per-instance attributes), brake lights flare, indicators blink before turns, glass carries painted-on highlight streaks. Rounded boxes with a radius under 6 cm are chamfered instead of rounded (≈ 3.5 k triangles per car, was 6.4 k).
- [B1] Night: headlight pools on the asphalt (instanced additive quads hugging the curve, LAYER_NO_INK) at full strength below ~30 m, thinning to faint warm fans up to ~170 m and gone by 260 m, so from the `city` view the streets read as strands of moving light; head/tail sparks (points, LAYER_NO_INK) take over past ~15 m and read from directly above too, so from orbit the ring road carries white and red beads.
- [B1 refine] No zebra claims at all. Measured traffic + the real PeopleSim (both pure TS) for 30 sim-min: every claim variant tried (the old 9-of-14 s duty cycle, a "bridge" that only held a clear zebra for ≤ 7-8 s) did worse than none — min share of vehicles moving per minute 0.77 / 0.82 with, 0.83 / 0.87 without; worst unexcused stop 56 / 77 s with, 42 / 62 s without — and against a synthetic crowd on a walk cycle the bridge claim even produced a full minute at 0 m. People cross in groups, so gaps come by themselves.
- [B1 refine] Re-route on walkers: a vehicle refused for > 4 s because its planned exit's start zebra is busy (and waiting > 1.2 s) takes another exit with room and a clear zebra (as it already did for a full exit). One stalled crossing (B2's crossing 143, busy for the whole run) now holds only the lane that must cross it; the city never locks (worst minute median 63 m vs 0 before).
- [B1 refine] Exit-lane fairness: a vehicle kept > 2 s by a full exit marks that lane, and lower-scored askers (the loop runs highest score first, waiting time raises the score) are not granted into it until it has room. A bus can no longer be starved by cars refilling a 12 m lane.
- [B1 refine] Bus tail swing: every connector is swept with a bus body (approach and exit lanes included) against `CityIndex.classify`; turns where a corner leaves the road/junction patch (most left turns and the U-turns here: the rear overhang swings over the corner kerb where people wait) are closed to buses, as is any lane that then only leads to such turns (pruned to a fixed point). Buses use 29 of 81 connectors (straights and right turns: bus routes). Trucks clear every turn. The sweep is 5 cm stricter than the spec, which checks all four body corners of every vehicle every 0.5 s (0 off-carriageway samples in an hour). ~3 ms at init.
- [B1 refine] Fleet 45 (25 cars, 11 compacts, 5 trucks, 4 buses), was 35; kind weights unchanged (density per lane is ~uniform, 3-4 vehicles / 100 m: the critic's "weight the ring" would have emptied the downtown street the street and rooftop shots look down). Warm-up 20 s (was 40); init yields every 50 steps (was 400): `init traffic` ≈ 125 ms wall, time-sliced.
- [B1 refine] Specs: an hour of heavy synthetic foot traffic on a 6-of-13 s walk window per crossing (overlap, body corners, zebra, ≥ 20 m per vehicle per minute), and 30 sim-min with the real PeopleSim: no overlaps, no body on a busy zebra, no vehicle still ≥ 60 s with every zebra at its junction clear (worst 26 s), ≥ 95 % of vehicles cover 20 m every minute or yield to people on a zebra for 20 s of it, the median vehicle > 40 m every minute.
- [B1 refine] Far LOD per kind (~110-350 triangles: the same builder with plain flat boxes, parts under 0.45 m dropped, octagonal wheels), dithered over altTerrain 40-50 m with the toon kit's `fade` (both materials share one program), sharing the near mesh's instance buffers (matrix, colour, motion, state, reveal). Near meshes trimmed to 2.5-3k triangles (12-sided wheels, plain glass panes and indicators, chamfered bumpers). Shadows cast only below 60 m and only on `high`. The whole fleet (and its light points/pools) culls as one sphere round the city cap. City view: traffic now ≈ 20 k triangles (was ≈ 250 k).
- [B1 refine] Charm: wheels +12 % (toy proportions; bodies raised 5 cm), dark wheel-arch bands laid onto the rounded body side, door mirrors, bubblier glasshouses with bigger glass, and three character variants from the same meshes via `aHub.w = 3 + v` parts collapsed by a per-instance `aVar`: two taxis (amber roof sign that glows at night), a police car (navy door stripe, red/blue light bar flashing in antiphase) and an ice-cream van (waffle cone + strawberry scoop on the roof). Glass streaks fade with (1 − night)²; the bus destination sign is lit on its front face only, with a dark route number.
- [B1 refine] Size: traffic/ is 12.6 KB gzip (rolldown minify, per-file), over the 10 KB target: the LOD, variants, kerb sweep and fairness rules cost ~1.5 KB; GLSL whitespace compacted.
- [B1 G2fix] Packing: every frame each vehicle in the frustum (sphere + 7 m shadow margin) and nearer than `view.horizon + 34 m` (a 3 m bus below the planet's horizon past that) is written into its kind's NEAR pack within `24 / tan(fov/2)` m (34 m at 70°, ~60 m at 43°) and/or its FAR pack beyond, so neither pass draws the whole fleet's near meshes (street: traffic 44 k triangles colour + shadow, was ~250 k; rooftops 40 k; clouds 22 k; city 9 k). Each pack has its own dynamic instance buffers (matrix, colour, motion, state, reveal, variant); the meshes are frustumCulled = false (culled per vehicle here).
- [B1 G2fix] LOD cross-fade is COMPLEMENTARY and by fragment distance (±4 m around the switch): uLodM 1 draws where 1 − s ≥ Bayer, uLodM 2 where 1 − s < Bayer (the A1 tree-LOD rule), in the colour and the shadow pass, so each pixel is drawn by exactly one mesh (was two mirrored toon `fade` ranges = the same threshold test: ~50 % see-through across 40–50 m). The toon kit's `fade` option is no longer used by traffic. Shadows dither out over altTerrain 60–100 m (depth patch), then castShadow off (was a one-frame fleet-wide switch at 60 m).
- [B1 G2fix] The player is an obstacle: below 4 m altTerrain inside the city, `sim.setObstacle(view.cityX/Z)` lists every lane / connector passing within 1.65 m (arc position, lateral distance; recomputed only when the eye moves > 10 cm); a vehicle whose half-width + 0.45 m reaches the eye treats it as a stationary leader (IDM to S0, hard clamp, brake lights) unless the eye is already alongside its body, and no grant is given through it (askers count it like a busy zebra and re-route after 4 s). Spec: standing mid-lane and inside a junction for 3 min, no body comes within 0.35 m of the eye and > 60 % of the fleet still covers 100 m. In the browser (lane 39, the G2 probe's lane): the nearest body stops 2.1 m from the eye.
- [B1 G2fix] Contract (additive): `services.traffic?.collide(x, z, r, out)`, a zero-alloc push-out of a plan-space disc from every drawn body box (the interpolated poses of the last draw), for A4's FPV walk after `cityIndex.collide`, like `services.people.pushOut`.
- [B1 G2fix] Flow: fleet 35 (20 cars, 8 compacts, 4 trucks, 3 buses; was 45). Grant requests start at braking distance + 2 m + 1 s of travel (a free junction no longer slows anyone); vehicles already granted onward do not count in a lane's free tail (a platoon flows through a short lane: they wait only for vehicles granted before them, so grant order stays a total order); routes are picked one junction ahead with an exit whose queue already reaches its start weighted ×0.08, and a full planned exit is re-planned while still rolling up (> 6 m from the line); a bus needs only 0.7 m beyond its body on the exit lane (it may nose in behind a car queued in a 14–16 m core lane, its tail ≤ 0.7 m in the box; every inbound avenue's only bus exit is such a lane); turns at 2.8 m/s² lateral (was 2.2); zone margin 0.6 s (was 0.9). Measured with the real crowd, 4 seeds × 20 sim-min: median vehicle 179–190 m/min (was 107–174 at 45), moving share 0.76–0.78 (0.63–0.74), p99 stop 18–25 s (28–38), long stops > 30 s ~4 per 20 min (9–16 at 45), worst stop with every zebra at the junction clear 12–19 s. NOT met: the critic's 250 m/min median. With no pedestrians 35 vehicles reach ~240 m/min and a lone car ~380: on a network of 42 lanes, most 11–17 m long with a zebra at each end (busy ~23 %), the remaining stops are yields to people. The critic's bus rule ("no lane whose bus-legal exits are all < 25 m, or only one") prunes the bus network to nothing here (every ring segment is 13–17 m with one straight continuation), so it is replaced by the bus room rule above.
- [B1 G2fix] `blocked` covers the whole body: every zebra on any of the up to three segments a body spans stays blocked until the REAR is 0.6 m past its strip (a bus's tail mid-turn kept a zebra it had released), plus the front lane's start zebra ahead of the front and the zebras a granted vehicle is about to cross.
- [B1 G2fix] Night: far headlight fans warm to (1, 0.66, 0.33) linear (≈ #FFD9A0 on screen) and shorten to ~6 m above 60 m (from 10.5 m), at half strength from 90 m: a warm smudge ahead of each car from the `city` view instead of white comet streaks. Box trucks carry a per-instance livery on the cargo box (aTint 2, motif by aVar = slot % 3): a cream flower in a body-colour disc over a base band (also on the back), a diagonal body-colour band with an amber pinstripe, a body-colour wave with a darker crest line; painted in the fragment patch as a ratio on the lit colour, edges antialiased with fwidth (the old cargo stripe box is gone).
- [B1 G2fix] Specs: the hour of synthetic foot traffic now needs ≥ 40 m per vehicle per minute and a mean speed > 3 m/s; 30 min with the real PeopleSim needs no vehicle still ≥ 30 s with every zebra at its junction clear (measured 16 s) and a worst-minute median > 110 m (measured 139–147); the player-obstacle spec above. Size: traffic/ ≈ 14.8 KB gzip per-module (was 12.9): packing, the obstacle, collide and the liveries.

## Look and post (B4)

- [B4] Post chain (render/post), refine round: the scene renders into an R11G11B10F target (RGBA16F without EXT_color_buffer_float; sRGB8 on low) with a DEPTH24 depth texture, NO MSAA by default; ONE ¼-res MRT draw writes the bloom source and a plain colour copy, each with hardware mips (both blur pyramids, no down/up chains, no full-res mips); the composite draws ink, tilt-shift (cubic reads of the ¼ colour mips), bloom, the sun glow, Neutral tone mapping, a grade and ±½ LSB dither, sRGB-encoded with luma in alpha; FXAA (Lottes console variant) on the finished frame, so ink lines are smoothed with their colour edges. ≤ 4 draws per frame. Paired A/B in one session (post.on toggled, 1280×800 DPR 1): full chain +0.7…1.9 ms over a bare render (was +10…13 ms with the MSAA'd half-float target in the same harness the same day).
- [B4] Engine render path (core/engine.ts, the only engine edits): `createPost(renderer, q.post, …)` installs `services.render`; scene programs are compiled with a render target bound (`compileScene`, linear output, no tone mapping, exactly as drawn); `post.compile()` joins both warm-ups; `sizePost()` on boot and resize (drawing-buffer px). With no renderable float targets createPost returns null and the engine renders directly.
- [B4] `QUALITY.high.antialias` false. MSAA is an opt-in only (`?p.post.msaa=4`, read at boot): its resolve (colour + depth blit) was the whole cost of the old chain. FXAA is on for high at DPR < 1.75 (`post.fxaa`): at DPR 2 the pixels are too small to stair-step and its pass costs ~4×. `low` (`q.post` false) keeps ink, grade and the night bloom (3 levels) on an sRGB8 target; no tilt-shift, FXAA or night-side darkening: +0.4…1.9 ms paired (C2: still measure it on a throttled mobile profile).
- [B4] Ink: depth-only (Δ² of 1/z, scale-free), ~1.5 px at any DPR, distance fade `1/(1+(d/230)²)` (≈0.27 from orbit), fogged with the scene fog, off inside a cloud (veil) and in the tilt-blurred bands. Persistence test: the edge is min(±1 px stencil, ±2 px stencil), so 1-px features (dither dots of LOD / personal-space fades, holes, B2's leash) never ink and neither do their neighbours (the first round's spike filter alone turned 1/16 dither into a '+' screen). The sky side of a silhouette gets full weight (bolder outer contour); above 30 m (clouds) an interior edge between two surfaces needs a 3× bigger relative depth step, so puff overlaps no longer crack into patchy hairlines. `LAYER_NO_INK` needs no pass of its own: every NO_INK object writes no depth.
- [B4] Bloom by night only, measured on WARMTH on chroma, (1, 0.25, −1.25)·rgb: white/grey 0, window amber ≈ 0.86, #FFB84D ≈ 1, moonlit blue < 0 — so moonlit ground, clouds, white walls, sunlit contrails and depthless additive white things never bloom. Each ¼-res source pixel is capped (1.2, hue kept) and the summed bloom soft-clipped on its max channel (b/(1+b/1.2)): 1-px windows from orbit keep their ×7 gain, a balloon lantern or a lit facade no longer blooms into a disc. Level weights DECAY (0.85 per level from orbit, 0.6 at the street; Σw-normalised): a local amber glow, not the beige halo slab over the whole city. Night orbit: ground between lights (13, 8, 33), was (49, 31, 37).
- [B4] Sun glow is analytic in the composite: a multiplicative lift (scales with the sky it sits on: the disc core overexposes to cream through the tone curve's shoulder) plus a small additive cream core and warm corona, × (1 + 3·dusk) for a low sun as the eye sees it, a soft warm wash below the sun's height, occluded by 5 depth taps at the sun's screen position, 0.3× over geometry. Dusk disc (248,135,69) → (255,224,210).
- [B4] Night-side darkening (high tier only; ×0.7 from orbit, ×0.92 at street level) keeps warm pixels (same chroma warmth as the bloom) and skips anything above 26–36 m: it was turning the night clouds off the limb into dark rocks.
- [B4] Tilt-shift: `tiltAmount` log-ramps in over 7–18 m and out over 110–200 m (0 at street level and orbit), sharp band ±18 % of the frame height, blur up to full-res mip 2.8 read from the ¼-res colour pyramid (cross-fade from the sharp image over the first two mips).
- [B4] Scene NaNs: the composite borrows a neighbour for any NaN/Inf pixel (they printed as dotted black rings: the lighthouse beam's silhouette, see A1 note) and ignores a NaN tilt blur; the prefilter zeroes them. `?p.post.debug=3` shows them.
- [B4] Toon kit: direct sun × `smoothstep(−0.14, 0, up·sun)` for every toon material (A1's G1 request: sun-facing walls lit past the terminator); the shade passes through a dusk-violet fill tint in the twilight band (multiply, so greens stay green); the dusk tint on luminance and the 30 %-desaturated moonlight albedo (A3's requests) kept from the first B4 pass.
- [B4] Note for A3 (clouds/): the night clouds off the limb still read as asteroids — dark indigo bodies with a thin moon line against the #0a1030 space. From orbit (uAirSpace) the night palette wants moonlit cotton: a silver-lilac lit band toward uMoonDir at ≥ 2.5× the space luminance, and the rim on the whole moon-facing half; or fade the night-side puffs down from orbit.
- [B4] Note for A1 (nature/beam.ts): the lighthouse beam writes NaN on its silhouette (dotted black rings in orbit, a ring in the dusk sky; ~90 % of the scene's NaN pixels): probably pow() of a negative base in its fresnel/edge term — clamp it. Also terrain/nature can drop their own SUN_UP multiply now that the toon kit applies it (it is squared there today: slightly darker in the 0…−8° band).
- [B4] Note for B3 (air/): from orbit the contrails run a quarter of the way round the planet and read as rings around the globe in the first dive frames; a shorter (or faster-fading) trail would keep them reading as planes.
- [B4] Toon kit, refine round: from orbit (lbOrb = smoothstep(70, 300, lbCamAlt)) the dusk band widens to ~33° of sun angle and the violet twilight multiply + dusk-luminance blend strengthen, so the terminator reads rose-violet instead of grey-sage → brown. Dark albedos (luminance < 0.3) in the twilight band — or past it while A3's night fill is the pink sunset sky (detected from lbNightFill's R − G) — lose 70 % of the light's chroma, get a capped violet multiply and a lifted fill (× up to 1.9): the dusk cul-de-sac (48,14,60) plum → (83,52,84) slate-lilac. Post shadow tint at dusk is warm lilac (0.30, 0.20, 0.42) at up to 4× strength. GLSL strings carry no comments (they shipped in the bundle); the rationale is in TS comments.
- [B4] Engine render path: unchanged from round one (createPost / compileScene / sizePost / services.render); `post.compile()` now compiles the composite against its LDR target and the FXAA pass against the canvas.
- [B4] Note for A2 (city/): water glows saturated cyan at night (park pond, rooftop pools, the plaza fountain) — the most saturated non-warm thing on the night side; darken it with lbNightAt toward the moonlit sky (~#1c2a5a) with a few sparkles. The dusk shot's door is a pure saturated blue next to the crimson wall: tone it down.
- [B4] Note for B2 (people/): a walker inside personal-space range is left as a 1/16 dither residue (a faint dot screen at the bottom-right of the `street` shot); discard fully once the fade passes ~0.9. Ink no longer amplifies it.
- [B4] Note for B3 (air/): the night balloon lantern no longer blooms into a fireball (bloom cap), but a lantern at ×2 emissive still reads as a bright orange blob from orbit: fade the envelope glow by altitude (off above ~150 m) or cap it ~0.6.
- [B4] Note for B1 (traffic/): `traffic:depth` compiles mid-flow at the dusk shot (dev warning every run).
- [B4 G2] The milky lavender film over the night half in `orbit` (57,50,74 over a 3,18,32 scene) was the post grade's shadow tint: its eye-based dusk boost (×4 lilac, meant for the dusk street) fired from orbit because the eye sat near the terminator. The dusk boost now fades out by orbit (× (1 − bloomFar)) and the base tint is ×0.25 there: night side (0,13,26), space stays deep blue.
- [B4 G2] ¼-res pass on real need only: bloom when lit windows can be in view — the visible ground reaching the night side from orbit (× smooth(0.25, 0.6, bloomFar)) or under the eye itself, or a building past the terminator that is in the frustum and over the plateau's horizon (one probe per building from the plan, a few hundred dot products a frame, no allocation). `nightInView`'s ramp moved to 88°…96° (lights a third → two thirds on) with no margin. The ¼ target is single (no MRT, no colour copy): tilt-shift no longer needs it (below). By day the frame is shadow + scene + composite + FXAA at every altitude (busy GPU: post chain 3.2–3.5 ms at street, was 7.5–13; tilt 0.4–0.9 ms at rooftops/clouds, was 2.4–2.9). `services.render.stats` (ground / city / tilt) says why the pass ran (review tooling).
- [B4 G2] Night bloom from orbit: per-level weight 1.0 (was 0.85) and the two widest levels +30 % on amber light (warmth/max > ~0.55, `post.bloomWideGain`), faded in over bloomFar 0.5…0.9: a city-scale warm haze over the dense blocks instead of hard pinpricks; the soft clip still caps the sum. Street-level decay unchanged.
- [B4 G2] Foliage at dusk (toon kit, `FRAG_DUSK_FOLIAGE`): runs after every system patch, so it has the last word over A1's LOW_LIGHT_GRADE inside the twilight band (the two desaturations stacked into neutral grey). Green-dominant albedos, sun −11.5°…+15° over the fragment (strongest at the terminator) or under A3's pink sunset-sky night fill near the eye, blend 68 % toward luminance × violet (rose on sun-facing sides), plus a rose rim on sunward faces (lbRimColor = A3's alpenglow rim near the eye). Measured: dusk crown (85,84,89) → (98,70,119), bushes → (89,62,107); orbit terminator grass (151,130,130)/(65,56,67) grey → (158,115,179) lilac / (75,46,95) violet. Note for A1: LOW_LIGHT_GRADE can now skip the twilight band (it is overridden there); outside it, at night, it still applies.
- [B4 G2] Tilt-shift subtler and passless: a 12-tap golden-angle disc of the scene colour inside the composite, only in the blurred bands (radius (2^(t·1.8) − 1)·2.4 px × the ink px scale; NaN taps dropped), replacing the cubic reads of the ¼-res colour mips; blur ≤ 1.8 mip (was 2.8), sharp band ±24 % (was ±18 %), `tiltAmount` peaks 28…60 m, ≈0.43 at 16 m and 0.5 at 120 m (× post.tilt 0.85): a hint at the rooftops and over the city, full miniature only in the cloud layer.

## Core (G2 fix round)

- [core G2fix] Scripted dive moves the world: `dive(u, glide, dt)` advances the sim by dt (default = glide) with the frame, and the hook's `frameNow` advances ALL of a scripted dt (in MAX_STEPS chunks; the live loop's 8-step cap had frozen 60 % of a 6 fps dive). Hook `diveSeconds` (additive). Shot tool: `--dive N` frames are diveSeconds/(N−1) apart in camera glide AND sim time (was a fixed 1/30 glide, so the review dive was not the real path); `--interval` overrides; `--t` is the dive's start time. The /play clip is the true path only at 30 fps: `--dive 301 --t 0 --size 1280x800`.
- [core G2fix] Contract: roots a stage-2 system adds to the scene are not drawn until its `ctx.compile()` has warmed them, even across `ctx.yield()` (frames hide them; compile marks them warm). Found by logging program creation: nature added its meshes, then yielded, and that frame built 4 programs synchronously (`nature:near:depth`, `nature:small:depth`, `nature`, `lighthouse beam`) on top of the reveal. Every program is now built inside a `ctx.compile()`; the dev "compiled mid-flow" warning is gone at boot.
- [core G2fix] three's `checkShaderErrors` is off in production (param `core.shaderChecks`, on in dev): its info-log reads cost a synchronous GPU round trip per program at first use (the cold "first frame" mark 219–240 ms → 11–22 ms). Shot-tool timing runs (`--boot`, `--perf`, `--cold`) measure with it off.
- [core G2fix] Cold boot, not met (for C2, on a quiet GPU): first-frame mark 470–800 ms cold under 10–17 shared headless browsers, then one ~200–330 ms rAF gap that is mostly NOT main-thread work (long tasks ≤ 76 ms): ANGLE/Metal building pipelines in the GPU process at first draw. The G2 warm-draw idea (hidden 1×1 draws with the real formats) was A/B'd in this round and showed no measurable gain under that noise, so it is not in. Reveal stutter after each stage-2 init (50–117 ms frames) is the same per-system first-draw cost.
- [core G2fix] Context loss: the engine releases itself on `webglcontextlost` (GL deletes are silent no-ops while lost; released after restore they hit the new context: ~30 INVALID_OPERATION) and skips `forceContextLoss`. `EngineOptions.resume` (additive) `{ view, t }`: littlebig-canvas.tsx keeps the last camera view and sim time on loss, and the rebooted engine starts there with the reveal instant (0 bad logs, view and t carried over: ctx-loss.txt).
- [core G2fix] Quality: `low` terrain detail 6 → 5 (terrain, ocean and nature all follow `q.terrainDetail`; d5/d6 shots indistinguishable at every altitude), and `low` on a coarse pointer caps DPR at 1.25 (`qualitySettings()`; `ctx.q` may be a per-session copy of `QUALITY[tier]`). Busy GPU, best of 4, 1280×800 DPR 1: low/high net street 7.2/9.0, rooftops 7.7/11.7, city 11.6/14.9, orbit 11.0/13.7 ms; triangles −33…40 %. Not core and still open (B4/A3/B1/B2): no shadow casters beyond buildings + near trees on low, far LODs only for traffic/people, density thinning bushes/rocks.
- [core G2fix] Perf tool: `--perf F --reps N` (defaults 90 frames × 5) runs short measurements interleaved across shots and prints one JSON line per shot with `bestNet` (≈ quiet GPU) and `medianNet` (the noise); `--ab 'name:key=v,…;…'` adds param-override variants paired inside each round (`deltaNet` = median of per-round differences). It warns when other headless browsers share the GPU. Record budget numbers only on a quiet machine; ANGLE/Metal timer queries return command-buffer latency, so there is no per-pass GPU split.

## Load, size, performance (C2)

- [C2] Size budget re-baselined (orchestrator decision): LITTLEBIG's own production JS ≤ 150 KB gzip, three.js excluded (shared with Snake). BRIEF §1 updated. Measured as the JS a production `/planet/` visit loads that `/about/` does not (`littlebig-shot.mjs --bundle` against a served `out/`); the G2 figure (169.5 KB) predates the G2 fix round, whose code put HEAD at 181.7 KB (engine 167.3 + a 14.4 KB shared chunk) plus 2.6 KB for the canvas wrapper. Now 162.6 + 2.6: the 150 KB target is not met yet (PERF.md lists what is left).
- [C2] `/* glsl */` template literals are minified at build time by `scripts/glsl-minify.cjs`, a Turbopack loader registered in `next.config.mjs` (`turbopack.rules['*.ts']`, only `components/littlebig/**` files that contain the tag), no new dependency: comments go, whitespace collapses, spaces next to punctuation go, floats lose redundant zeros. Preprocessor lines and lines holding a `${…}` keep their own line; nothing next to an interpolation that could carry a sign is fused. On in dev too, so review shots run the production shader text. Spec'd token-for-token over every tagged literal (`core/glsl-minify.spec.ts`). −11.0 KB gzip; every shot pixel-identical.
- [C2] Review tooling is off the production path. The debug chunk (`core/debug.ts`, `core/shots.ts`, `camera/dive.ts`, `world/city/views.ts`) imports no runtime value from an engine module: Turbopack pulls any module shared by two chunks out of the engine's scope-hoisted chunk into its own (no constant inlining or mangling across it; that alone was ~4.5 KB). Those modules read the engine modules they need from `core/debug-kit.ts`, which the engine fills from `core/kit.ts` before it loads `debug.ts` (specs import `core/kit-fill.ts` first). The debug chunk is ~9 KB gzip, loaded only with `?shot` or in dev.
- [C2] Clouds no longer build the scripted dive at boot to place the cloud the camera punches through: `clouds/dive-anchors.ts` holds the dive's layer crossings as constants (bit-exact doubles), `dive-anchors.spec.ts` recomputes them from the live dive and prints the fresh table when the plan or dive moves. Also takes ~24 ms of `buildDivePath` off the boot's CPU.
- [C2] The plan's review viewpoints (`rooftops`, `horizon`, `dusk`) are solved by `world/city/views.ts` through a registry (`world/city/shot-views.ts`); `core/shots.ts` registers it. The getters on `plan.viewpoints` stay lazy and non-enumerable; `street` and `plaza` stay in plan.ts (the dive and the plan use them).
- [C2] Zero allocation: `world/hyp.ts` replaces `Math.hypot` in every per-frame path (V8's builtin allocates an argument array per call). `hyp` / `hyp3` are V8's own algorithm for 2 / 3 finite values, bit-identical (spec'd over 300 k random triples; checked in Chromium too), so every seeded sim replays exactly.
- [C2] Contract (additive): `CityIndex.q` (Float64Array) + `classifyQ()` / `collideQ(out)`. V8 boxes (allocates) every double passed to or returned from a call it does not inline; the people sim's ~900 classify / collide calls per step allocated on each. The index's hot paths are rewritten with flat per-building / per-segment arrays and inlined maths (same results: 200 k random points compared against the old index, and the people sim replays identically over 300 steps with either). The people sim and dogs call the Q forms.
- [C2] Traffic draw: wheel/bob scratch in a Float64Array (a closure `let` holding a double allocates on every write), `basis()` takes the position as a Vec3, the frustum test inlined.
- [C2] Stage 2 is pipelined: the next system starts building on the frame after the current one calls `ctx.compile()`, so CPU builds overlap the driver's compiles (cold visits spent most of stage 2 waiting on shader compiles). One system builds at a time; a system's compile resolves only once the system before it has finished, so reveals keep systems.ts order. Roots claimed by an in-flight compile are not marked warm by another (frames never draw an unlinked program). `BootEntry.wait` (additive) records each init's compile wait; the shot tool's `--boot` prints it.
- [C2] Contract (additive): `System.prepare?(ctx)` (stage 1) and `ctx.prewarm(objects)`. Right after the sky's init, terrain and ocean create their materials and hand stand-in meshes to `ctx.prewarm`, so their (largest, ~0.45 s cold) programs compile while the inits build geometry. The stand-in geometry carries a normal: three's program key depends on its presence.
- [C2] The engine's warm mesh no longer compiles its plain toon colour program: no first-frame surface has used that variant since terrain and ocean got patches, and its ~0.5 s cold compile held the first frame, then was released unused. It still stands in for the default shadow depth program.
- [C2] Kept as is after measuring: gating the night-bloom ¼ pass to "a city building or the eye past the terminator" saves ~3.5 ms by day from orbit, but a time sweep showed visible differences at dawn and dusk (up to 27 k pixels > 8/255 over the city at 200 m), so B4's rule stays. Limiting its mip chain (`TEXTURE_MAX_LEVEL`) saved nothing on ANGLE/Metal.
- [C2] Finding for B2 (not changed): a dog's gait phase (`dg`, its odometer) is not reset on a time jump, so a dog's leg pose depends on how many settles ran before a shot (shot order, boot timing). Pixel-diffing review shots shows it as dog-only differences.
- [C1] People make way for a lens settling onto the pavement (`people/sim.ts` `LensWatch`, `step(…, lens)`; additive, optional). A continuous descent (the dive, scroll, fly-to) has no camera cut, so `clearAround` never ran, and walkers saw the camera only below 3 m as a 0.75 m disc. A walker coming up the pavement walked up to the landing and stood ~1.1 m in front of the lens (the dive from t = 0: a face filling the frame). Now a camera below 6 m that is descending (held 0.6 s, since a scripted 30 fps descent moves on every other 60 Hz step) or standing still is a lens. Walkers see it from 5 m + its radius ahead and give it a 1.6 m berth. One heading at it inside the berth, or with no way round it, turns round there and then (the stuck turn-back, early), instead of stopping at its feet. While it settles, walkers see its predicted resting point, not its current position: the horizontal / vertical speed × the height left to eye level over the pavement × 1.5, or the stopping distance v² / 2a once it brakes, whichever is shorter, capped at 15 m. On the dive this closes from 4.6 m short at 5.5 m up to within 0.1 m from 2.4 m up. Without it, walkers reacted as the camera swept up to them. Spec: `people/lens.spec.ts` (nobody in view comes within 2.2 m walking at the lens or standing, from the landing through a 3 s hold, over several starts; it fails without the change). Not covered: someone walking away who is already at the landing when the camera comes down behind them. The clip's start time is scanned to avoid that.
- [C1] The /play clip starts at t0 = 11 (was DIVE_T0 = 1: since the traffic and people fixes, t = 1 lands with a truck passing ~5 m off the lens and a quarter of the frame). Picked by `scripts/play-media/littlebig-scan.mjs` (A4's scan2, moved to scripts and extended with a hold and a low-swoop metric) over t0 ∈ [0, 14], then checked by eye. Recipe in `docs/play-media.md`. **For core:** set `DIVE_T0 = 11` in `core/shots.ts`, so the `landing` and `approach` shots match the clip (not changed here: core's file).

## Final fix round (game)

- [final] `DIVE_T0 = 11` in `core/shots.ts` (C1's handoff): the `landing` / `approach` shots match the /play clip again.
- [final] Resolution: `high` caps DPR at 1.5 (was 2: DPR 2 doubled a per-pixel-bound frame, 1280×800 city 15 → 33 ms), and the live loop steps the pixel ratio down a ladder (1.5 → 1.25 → 1 → 0.85) when the median rAF interval over ~1 s passes 18 ms, back up after 3 calm seconds, with a per-level backoff (8 s doubling to 64 s) so a scene that cannot hold a level never oscillates. Only after the world is complete, never in shot mode; `?p.core.adaptive=false` turns it off. A change only resizes the canvas and post targets (no recompiles). /planet at 1512×945 DPR 2, live wheel dive: ~50 fps, 3 frames over 34 ms (was 29 fps, 82). `littlebig-shot.mjs --dpr N` (default 1).
- [final] Production leak: ctx.world's city getters live at module scope in `core/engine.ts` (`WORLD_CITY`). Written inline in createEngine, the minifier inlined getCityIndex/createCityIndex into the getter, so the page-lifetime index cache's closures held the first engine. `--leak` now loads `/?shot=1`, so it runs against a served `out/` too: 9 engines tracked, 0 alive.
- [final] Stage-2 warm draw (`core/engine.ts` warmDraw, `Post.warm` additive): after a system's compile, its roots are drawn once — every descendant visible and unculled, everything else drawable hidden (lights stay: they key every program), clipped to one pixel of the scene target, colour and shadow pass. Buffer uploads (2.5 MB of near-only geometry that landed mid-dive) and the driver's pipeline builds happen behind the reveal: 0 KB uploaded during the dive. Costs ~80–100 ms of world-complete (warm); `?p.core.warmDraw=false` for A/B.
- [final] ↗ expand keeps the player's place: the window canvas registers a reader in `components/littlebig/handoff.ts` (engine-free), onExpand stashes view + sim time, the /planet canvas boots with them as `EngineOptions.resume` (the context-loss path: reveal instant). The same module holds the one `import('./littlebig-canvas')` that next/dynamic (window host, app/planet) and the prefetch share (two call sites were two chunks), and prefetchLittlebig (re-exported from the window host). The window opens with the prefetch kicked first; '/' typed into a field no longer prefetches (here and in GOLDENLINE's host); the dynamic import's placeholder is the space colour (no black → navy flash).
- [final] The sea steer (camera): a zoom heading below 160 m over open sea (no land within 30 m) flies on to the nearest land (a ~10 m Fibonacci lattice of firm, low ground built on first use) or the city when it is less than 1.5× + 40 m further, once per descent (re-armed above 260 m). Zooming during the flight sets where it ends (not below 14 m: it has looked for no landing spot); zooming out past 260 m calls it off. Hovering low over water the hint row says `scroll out to fly · double-click land to fly there`. Not done (log): sea detail at mid altitude, a third landmass.
- [final] Portrait canvases fit the whole planet at orbit: `orbitFitFov` widens the vertical FOV (blended in from 120 to 260 m, capped at 80°) until the disc spans ≤ 85 % of the width; landscape untouched.
- [final] Zooming out from a level view (pitch above −55°) drops the zoom anchor: the cursor ray grazed the horizon, and its far point dragged the climb kilometres along the look direction. A steeper view keeps map-style zoom.
- [final] Keys: a release always lands (before the focus and modifier guards), and the window canvas drops held keys on blur.
- [final] Terrain: hedgerow lines are faint in the smooth (far) colour and absent on the city plateau (the bare first frame's jagged dark "veins"); facets on a border between soft-ground biomes take their smooth corner colours (`FACE_EDGE`, alpha 0.5 in `aFaceCol`), so sand/grass/forest edges are one-facet gradients instead of per-triangle sawtooth; shading stays faceted. Snow: line 16.2 m (down to 14.6 in the heart of the range) with a softer jitter, a cap per peak instead of stray facets.
- [final] Hedgerows: denser (0.75 per border facet), varied sizes and lengths, so they read as hedges, not a row of boulders.
- [final] Sky: from orbit the day/night band of the rim glow is wider (−0.3 … 0.16), so the halo fades round the terminator instead of ending in a straight vertical edge.
- [final] Clouds: the mist slab over the city is 36–45.5 m (was 31.5–49), the white-out release 0.12 s, the veil never fully opaque (shells modulate it; the opaque blocker is off), the scene fog at full white-out reaches 16 m (was 9), the wisps are faint and the shells fade toward the travel point (they pinched into an X-shaped pinwheel). The dive's pass: ~4 frames at full veil (was ~12).
- [final] City: buildings draw both faces into the shadow map (light leaked through segment seams in thin lit lines at floor levels; no acne seen at any shot); turn arrows are a 45° arm with a head square to its end (an offset head on a skewed arm read as a sawtooth).
- [final] Time-slicing: yields in the city's light pools (per 8 lamps), the cloud coverage map (per 8 puffs), traffic's warm-up replay (per 8 steps) and nature's scatter (per 1024 facets). Phone profile ×4: the stage-2 long tasks are 52–67 ms (were 56–91); the first-frame task is unchanged.

## Final fix round (perf)

- [final perf] `low` drops the night bloom (BRIEF §1: no expensive post on low). The ¼-res pass is not compiled or drawn there; the emissive windows and light pools carry the night. Low tier, production, 1280×800: orbit 11.6 → 5.6–7.2 ms net, night 11.2 → 7.4, the scripted dive's median 15.6 → 10.8 ms. `high` is unchanged (and the /play clip, rendered on high, with it).
- [final perf] The night bloom on high stays as it is. Paired toggles on production (orbit, high, DPR 1) split its ~3.5 ms into the hardware mip chain ~0.7 ms, the ¼-res draw ~1.5 ms and the composite's taps ~1.4 ms. A manual downsample chain would add dependent passes, not remove one. An exact early-out in the composite (coarsest level first, skipping the finer taps where it is zero) was pixel-identical but saved nothing measurable, because sunlit cloud tops reach the day threshold's soft knee and the coarse levels are non-zero over most of an orbit frame. It was reverted.
- [final perf] The city index takes the planet as an object (`CityIndexOptions.terrain`), not a `terrainH` closure. A closure created in a page-lifetime cache captures the scope of whatever function the minifier inlines its caller into. The engine-side fix (`WORLD_CITY`, game owner) moved the getters. This one removes the closure itself. Production `--leak 10`: 9 engines tracked, 0 alive. A heap-snapshot retainer check (canvas WeakRefs) shows both canvases collected.
- [final perf] The hero plasma (`components/hero-shader.tsx`, `public/hero-shader.js` `pause()`) holds its rAF while a snake, surf or planet window is open. It subscribes to the three buses' open/close events. Expanding a game navigates to an excluded route, which unmounts it. The canvas keeps its last frame and the clock skips the held time. Measured: 60 → 0 hero draws/s while the planet or snake window is open, and back to 60 on close.
- [final perf] GLSL identifier minifier: not built. A prototype that renames every identifier declared in our GLSL (and used nowhere else in our JS or three's chunks) to 1–2 characters saves 0.5 KB gzip (24.3 → 23.8 KB over all tagged literals), not the 4–6 KB estimated, against a real risk of breaking three's chunk hooks.
- [final perf] Debug hook `diveT0` (core/shots.ts `DIVE_T0`): the shot tool's `--dive` starts the sim there unless `--t` is given (A4's and C1's handoff).

## Orchestrator, release (2026-10-05)

- **Size budget:** shipped at 168 KB gzip against the ≤ 150 KB target. Accepted for this release
  rather than re-baselined again. C2 and the final perf owner found no dead code; the remaining
  levers are feature trims (city render, city plan, nature, people) that the dive shows on screen.
  The whole visit stays procedural with zero asset downloads.
- **/play clip:** re-rendered after the final fix round (cloud pass, rim glow, terrain blending,
  snowcaps changed what it shows). The poster is re-rendered from the same build. The loop is
  rotated to open on the poster frame (`littlebig-assemble.py --start 66`), so the poster fades
  into an identical video frame.
- **Receipt time:** 17 hours wall clock, Oct 4 15:02 → Oct 5 ~08:30 EEST, overnight, including two
  usage-limit pauses.
- **Crossing commit holes (found by CI on x86-64):** the 30-minute traffic + people spec failed only
  on x86-64, where last-bit floating-point differences send the chaotic sims down another path. On that path a
  waiting walker was shoved a little past a rounded kerb corner and then (a) counted as "already on
  the road" and committed, or (b) turned back after ~17 s and gained a commitment from `onRoad`,
  both while a car held the zebra. Fixes in `people/sim.ts`: an uncommitted walker cannot advance
  past kerb + 0.1; the on-road auto-commit needs the walker 0.5 m clear of the far kerb; a turn-back
  only keeps a commitment it already had. Specs pass on x64 (Rosetta, Node 24) and arm64; the
  named shots are pixel-identical.

## v2

Spec: `V2.md`. Tag lines `[v2-<task>]`.

- [v2-orch] Rides, picking and labels are contracts written up front (`core/contracts.ts` v2 block, `core/track.ts`), so five agents can build against them at once: `Trackable` / `TrackService` (`ctx.services.track`, a generic CPU ray-vs-bounding-sphere `pick` in front of the planet), `LabelService` (`ctx.services.labels`), optional `CameraService.mode / ride / cycle / fly / exitMode`, `ViewState.mode / ride`.
- [v2-orch] The space layer is `SPACE_MIN`–`SPACE_MAX` = 110–300 m above sea level (`world/config.ts`): above the planes (≤ 80), below the orbit camera (≤ 420). `TALLEST` stays the planes' bound; D1 extends the far plane for the space layer.
- [v2-orch] Outside the plateau, the region network is world space with its own sim (V1); the capital's tuned v1 sim is untouched. The two meet at gate plazas and never share a lane. One chart for the whole continent was rejected: the exponential map squeezes circumferential distance by sin θ/θ (−22 % at 1.2 rad), so towns planned in the capital's chart would overlap on the sphere.
- [v2-orch] Size budget raised to ≤ 260 KB gzip for v2 (the user lifted the limit "somewhat"); still zero asset downloads and no new fonts.
- [v2-S1] Contract (additive): optional `CameraService.subject?(out: Vector3): number` — the thing the camera follows right now (world centre into `out`, returns its bounding radius, 0 = none). For D1: return the bird in bird mode (and optionally the ridden Trackable in a ride). The clouds keep the subject visible over the falling-through-clouds overlay (a soft screen-space hole round it, puffs dissolved in front of it, the white-out fog pushed past it); without it they fall back to `ctx.services.track.get(ctx.view.ride).pose()` / `.radius`.
- [v2-L1] Everything that moves is a Trackable, ids in registration order and seeded, so the same id names the same thing every visit: `person:<walker>` (eyes; idlers are not followable: they do not move), `car:<n>` (cars and compacts), `truck:<n>`, `bus:<n>` (chase), `plane:<i>`, `balloon:<i>`, `boat:<loop>` (chase). V1's region vehicles should continue each kind's numbering after the capital's (e.g. `car:28`). No contract change: only one read-only field on my own `TrafficSim` (`next`, the planned connector) for the card's "turning left onto …".
- [v2-L1] The crowd's size follows the quality tier (low draws 150 walkers, high 230), so `person:150`+ exist on high only; `person:0`–`149` are the same people (look, traits, card) on every tier.
- [v2-L1] Cars get no driver's-seat 'eyes' variant: one id per vehicle keeps the UI's prev / next and the dock simple, and a chase reads better than a camera inside a body built to be seen from outside (no interior, culled back faces).
- [v2-L1] A walker's 'eyes' pose is the head's centre 11 cm forward, from the same walk cycle the shader poses (people/track.ts `walkerEye`: kids' 1.22× head about the neck, the vertical bob and forward lean); under reduced motion no bob. The ride camera keeps that bob (camera/rides/rig.ts filters it only for reduced motion), so nobody adds a second one.
- [v2-L1] The ridden walker's published forward is its facing plus 60 % of its kerb glance through a critically damped follower (ω 4.2 rad/s) capped at 77°/s and 5 rad/s² (round 1 had 150°/s and 14 rad/s²; the critic measured 133°/s on the camera: too fast for first person). A walker giving up on a kerb turns right round, and the raw facing then swings at up to ~450°/s; the rig springs the heading again, which only lowers the peak (measured on the camera: ≤ 78°/s). A ridden walker shuffling or sidestepping slowly (< 0.9 m/s) faces its way along the path rather than its velocity, so a sidestep slides the view instead of swinging it. Waiting walkers (anyone, not only the ridden) glance at the traffic with their head at full amplitude: left (near-lane traffic, right-hand driving), right, left, ahead, on a 6.2 s loop, smoothed by how much they are standing at a kerb.
- [v2-L1] The ridden walker hides its head, face, hair and backpack in the COLOUR pass only (uniform `uPpRide`, `#ifdef PP_COLOR`): its shadow keeps its head, and its body shows when you look down. Hidden on `setRidden(true)` while the eye is within 1.2 m of the head, or whenever the eye is inside it (< 0.35 m).
- [v2-L1] `pose()` is false for walkers that are not drawn (above the people LOD band, gone home) — except one "featured" walker nearest the view's focus (5 m hysteresis), which stays rideable from any altitude so the dock's people mode works from orbit (D1's `ride` refuses a pose-less trackable) without a click on the city from up there landing on someone invisible elsewhere.
- [v2-L1] Outside `explore` the camera is no pedestrian: walkers neither dodge it nor clear its lens (`LensWatch`, `clearAround`), and traffic does not stop for it (`setObstacle`). Riding someone's eyes the camera IS that walker; a chase camera low behind a car would otherwise halt the traffic behind it. The ridden plane or balloon never dodges the eye (its chase camera sits inside the dodge radius on purpose).
- [v2-L1] Sailboat sails are trimmed out 24° on a boom and bellied: flat sails on the centreline vanished edge-on from dead astern, exactly where a chase camera sits.
- [v2-U1] The POP HUD (`ui/`) ships in the canvas chunk and imports no engine module at runtime: the palette hex, `R` and the day constants are mirrored in `ui/theme.ts` (`ui/ui.spec.ts` keeps them equal to `render/palette.ts` and `world/config.ts`), and Vector3s come from `ctx.camera.position.constructor`. Importing them would pull three and world modules into the canvas chunk and split them out of the engine's scope-hoisted chunk. ~17 KB gzip.
- [v2-U1] One rAF drives the labels, the hover tip, the time warp and the camera-mode poll; React re-renders only on change (the card's live line ≤ 4 Hz). Its JS cost is recorded as `ctx.perf.systemMs.hud` (~0.02 ms a frame). Labels are imperative DOM with their own view-projection maths, hidden behind the sea-level sphere (limb fade), faded by altitude in log space and at the screen edges, decluttered greedily by kind priority with memory (last frame's winners keep their place), and kept clear of the HUD's own panels and the back link. A drag that starts on a tag is handed to the canvas, and the wheel over a tag zooms.
- [v2-U1] Round shapes: the site's `* { border-radius: 0 !important }` is beaten by specificity inside the HUD root (every rounded rule is `.lbh .x { border-radius: … !important }`, 0-2-0 over 0-0-0); `/planet`'s back link has its own `a.lb-back` rule (it shows before the game chunk arrives). Nothing outside them is restyled. The touch stick is drawn with SVG circles. Fonts: a rounded heavy system face (`ui-rounded`, SF Pro Rounded, Arial Rounded MT Bold…) plus the site's mono; nothing downloaded.
- [v2-U1] Contract (additive): optional `CameraService.hover(): string | null`, the camera's own ≤ 10 Hz hover pick (the one that sets the pointer cursor; D1 wired it), so the "click to follow" tip does not pick a second time. Without it (or while the review hook has locked the camera) the HUD picks itself at ≤ 10 Hz.
- [v2-U1] Dock: explore · bird · plane (planes, balloons) · drive (bus, car, truck, train, ferry, boat) · space (station, satellites) · people; keys 1–6. A mode picks the drawn candidate nearest the middle of the view (its mode's first kinds preferred: the station before the satellites); pressing it again rides the next. Disabled while nothing of its kinds is registered or the camera lacks the method. If nothing can be ridden (walkers are posed only while drawn, plus L1's one featured walker), "people" and "drive" fly the explore camera to the capital (12 m / 34 m) and hop on as soon as a candidate is drawn: retried at 4 Hz, cancelled by user input, 9 s timeout.
- [v2-U1] Prev / next is the camera's `cycle` within a kind (also on `[ ]`, D1) for one-kind modes; see refine 1 below for modes of several kinds. The card stays mounted across rides (a focused arrow keeps its focus) and pops again on each new ride. Esc stays with the camera (D1 catches it before the floating window's close).
- [v2-U1] Time button: eases `core.timeScale` up to the param's max (8×) and brakes into the target: the next evening (sun −5°, lights on) by day, the next morning (sun +9°) by night, over the view's focus. The param's value is written directly (no change listeners). A time jump was rejected: it would teleport traffic. The engine runs at most 8 fixed steps a frame, so the warp tops out at ~8× at 60 Hz: the short hop to the first evening takes ~5 s, a night to morning ~30 s (progress ring; pressing again brakes to a stop). A faster warp needs core to allow more fixed steps per frame while `timeScale` > 8; the HUD already reads the param's max.
- [v2-U1] After a mouse press on the HUD, focus goes back to the canvas so WASD and the camera's keys keep working; keyboard users keep focus on the HUD. Tab order: dock, card, time button, then the visible labels. The hint row: see refine 1 for where it sits; below the back link when the touch stick is up (the dock then stands up bottom right, away from the left thumb). The coach mark shows once (localStorage `littlebig.coach.v2`), 2.6 s after the world is up, only while exploring above the street with something to ride; done on "got it", on the first ride or mode, or after 14 s.
- [v2-U1] Shot mode hides the HUD and the back link unless `?hud=1` (shot tool `--hud`; `--coach` adds the coach mark).
- [v2-U1, refine 1] **The HUD never covers the planet or the thing you follow.** Landscape (width ≥ 1.05 × height, not phone-narrow): the planet fills the height and leaves the sides empty, so the dock is a rail on the right edge (vertically centred; below the time button when short) and the hint sits bottom left; the coach mark takes the bottom-left corner too (no tail: a satellite or the station may be anywhere beside the planet). Portrait keeps the bottom dock under the planet. Chosen over the critic's alternative (publish a bottom safe-area inset for the camera to shift its framing) because it needs no camera change and also clears the chase plane, the bus and the alongside views without touching D1's framing. Riding or flying in landscape, the hint tucks under the card (`--lbh-cb`, the card's layout bottom); if the followed thing's screen circle reaches it there, it moves bottom left for the rest of the ride, and if that is covered too it stays hidden. The phone layouts hide the hint when it would cross the followed thing.
- [v2-U1, refine 1] **World labels keep off the followed thing.** While riding or flying the bird, the subject (`camera.subject`, else the ridden trackable's pose) is projected to a screen circle (bounding radius × 0.92 + 10 px); any tag whose box touches it is faded out. Skipped when the eye is inside the subject (a walker's eyes). A moving anchor's tag (the station) now stands above its model on screen, 0.75 × its bounding radius projected plus 5 px, instead of 1.5 m along its up vector (which from orbit projected onto the station itself).
- [v2-U1, refine 1] **Prev / next for a mode of several kinds is the HUD's ring** (space: the station, then each satellite; plane: planes, then balloons; drive: buses, cars, trucks, …), from the card arrows, the dock pressed again and `[ ]`, so prev always undoes next. `[ ]` in such a ride is caught on the window in the capture phase and stopped there, ahead of the camera's own key listener (the camera does the same for Esc); a one-kind mode (people) keeps the camera's `cycle`. Space mode rides the station whenever it is in sight and not behind the planet (`ModeDef.lead`), satellites otherwise.
