- [v2-U1, refine 1] A label click aims the fly-to short of the place by alt / tan|pitch| along the heading the camera will arrive with (its fly-to carries the heading along the great circle and ends directly above the target at the altitude-driven pitch: −68° at a town's 42 m, which put the town at the bottom edge with its tag off screen). `ui/label-math.ts` mirrors `camera/model.ts pitchForAlt` and `world/sphere.ts headingVector` (spec'd equal) rather than importing them into the canvas chunk. Flight altitudes stay R1's `flyAlt`.
- [v2-U1, refine 1] Type: one display voice (the rounded heavy face) for the hint row (with ink keycap chips), the card's kind badge and sub-line, the back link and the small captions; mono stays only for the numbers in the live pill. Floor 10.5 px (keycap digits 10 px bold), live line 12 px, up to 3 lines with `text-wrap: balance`; dots get a no-break space before them and short segments are held together, so a wrap never strands '·' or splits '53 km/h'. Label text is ink on every fill (white on sky was 2.5:1, on teal 3.2:1; ink is 6.8:1 and 5.5:1). The dock's availability is recomputed the frame the track registry changes (not on the next 250 ms tick), and the hint waits for a first-time visitor's coach mark instead of flashing under it. The page's back link is first in the DOM (first in the tab order).
- [v2-U1f] **No panel ever shares space with another, by construction, not by measuring.** The card and the hint now live in one top-left column (`.lbh-tl`, a flex column below the page's back link and clear of the rail), so riding in landscape the hint is simply the next item under the card, whatever the card's height, from the first frame. (Round 1 positioned it from the card's measured bottom, refreshed on the 250 ms tick: for the first frames of a ride, or after the card grew a line, it sat at `top: 12px`, under the back link — the orchestrator's `flight` shot.) The hint has four slots (`hud.tsx hintSlot`): `stack` (under the card, or under the back link when the touch stick owns the bottom left), `bl` (landscape, beside the planet), `top` (roomy portrait: centred with the same inset on both sides, `--lbh-ti` = the larger of the back link's and the time button's reach), `bottom` (phones and compact portrait: on top of the dock, `--lbh-dt` = the dock's measured top). The top-centre slot is gone from compact layouts. When the pill is wider than its slot, the least important segments drop out (each hint segment carries a keep rank), never cut mid-word. The slow tick runs every frame for 0.6 s after a mode change (and always in shot mode), so the card, the slot and the time button are settled within a few frames; shot mode skips the card's exit animation, so a review frame never shows the previous ride's card.
- [v2-U1f] **The live line is laid out by hand** (`ui/live-text.ts`, spec'd): the card measures how many monospace columns its box holds (a 40-character probe; offsets, so the pop-in's scale does not skew it) and renders explicit lines. Pieces split on ' · ' (the dot ends its piece, so no line starts with '·'); a number stays with its unit; a piece no longer than 60 % of the line never breaks. Every layout of at most 3 lines (2 on a short screen) is scored and the cheapest wins: a break inside a long piece costs 6 (2 right after a colon, 4 before a preposition, 18 after an article), each extra line 7, the spread between the longest and shortest line a little, a stub line (under 40 % of the longest: '13 km/h ·' stranded) 2, each piece left out 30 (the heading, then the place: dropped, never clipped). So a piece breaks only when that saves a line or it cannot fit, and a forced break goes in the long piece, at its most natural space, keeping place names whole. Chosen over round 2's greedy wrap + balance, which split place names ('on the / ring road', 'a / lap every 1:36') when a break was forced. No '…' unless one word is wider than the box. Compact, narrow and short layouts use 11 px mono and less padding, and the compact card is 344 px (short 328 px) so its live line holds ~28 columns like the desktop card's: a bus reads '24 km/h · on the ring road · / next stop: clockwork avenue' in the window too (was three broken lines at 22 columns).
- [v2-U1f] **The coach bubble finds a corner clear of the planet.** Landscape tries bottom left, then top left (below the back link; not while a card is up), each at a few widths from wide to narrow (a narrower bubble is taller but tucks further into the corner), against the planet's true screen disc (`label-math planetDisc`: asin(r / d) silhouette, + 8 m of rim glow); the first that clears wins, else the least covered. Portrait keeps it on the dock with its tail. Compact layouts (the window, phones) get a smaller bubble with a round 'got it' sticker on its corner instead of a pill below the text, and drop the 'keys 1–6' aside (the dock shows the keys); a phone on its side drops the second line. In the 960 × 600 window it now sits beside the planet instead of over its south-west limb.
- [v2-U1f] Short screens (a phone on its side) get a slimmer card (38 px badge, no kind chip — the badge shows the kind — 15 px title, two live lines). On touch every world tag is a ≥ 40 px target (6/7 px padding, 13 px names; was 36 px) with a little extra hit slop; the label layer re-measures its tags when the layout or input changes (`LabelLayer.remeasure`). The card's sub-line no longer shares the title's ×-clearance padding, so it wraps less.
- [v2-U1f] "plane" rides a plane: the mode leads with its first kind (`ModeDef.lead`, like the station in space), so a balloon drifting over a roof near the middle of the view no longer wins; balloons follow planes in the mode's prev / next ring.
- [v2-U1f] The bird's card reads the bird (`camera.subject`), not the chase camera: its altitude, its speed (over real time live, over sim time in shot mode, where the review tool moves the bird with the sim) and where it is flying (`ctx.view.heading`, a compass point). Round 2 measured the camera, which reads ~1,400 km/h while it swoops in from orbit, and 0 km/h in every review shot; no speed is shown until it has been measured.
- [v2-U1f] World tags keep clear of the card by its layout box (+ its shadow and its ×), not its rect, which the pop-in scales from 0.7; and a tag that is fading out (hidden by the declutter) vanishes at once if its anchor carries it under a panel during a fast camera move, so a tag is never drawn over the HUD. The followed thing's keep-out circle is its full bounding radius (+ 10 px; was × 0.92, which left a chased plane's wingtips open to tags).
- [v2-U1f] A dock button clicked with the mouse keeps its tooltip down until the pointer leaves it: the tip is for finding a mode, and hung over the view the mode had just opened (keyboard focus still shows it).
- [v2-U1f] On a mode change the old hint row goes at once (no 0.6 s fade showing the new mode's words in the old row's slot) and the new one fades in after 0.9 s, once the camera has arrived (`camera.mode().blend` = 1). The chased thing sweeps across the screen during the fly-in; round 2 let that sweep decide the hint's slot for the whole ride (the fallback slot, or none): in the floating window a plane ride never showed its hint.
- [v2-L1] people/sim.spec "never steps onto a crossing a car is committed to" counted walkers the time-jump placement had left mid-crossing as stepping onto a blocked zebra at the very first step (its `prev` busy started all zero). R1's gate-avenue layout happened to settle two walkers onto crossings (avenue 6, ring 13) and tripped it. The spec now passes its busy / blocked arrays to `placeAt` (as the real-traffic spec does), so the sim's own settle writes the zebras' starting state; every rise during the run still counts. (Round 1 hand-seeded busy[] in the spec; the critic preferred the sim writing it.)
- [v2-L1] The ridden walker keeps a camera's berth (people/sim.ts `rider` and `berthPair`, set from `ctx.view.ride` each fixed step; with no rider every v1 spec runs the old sim). Round 2 kept people out of a ±45° view cone to ~0.8 m on fake traffic, but the critic found the berth switched off on zebras (0.54 m behind someone for 3 s), tuned for adults (a kid's eye sees torsos), and face-offs at 1.5 m. Round 3, measured on 96 independent 30 s rides against the live traffic (every fourth a kid): 94 never closer than 1.0 m, two at 0.8–0.9 m (a corner waiter the lagging view swings past); under 1 m in 0.01–0.04 % of frames (was ~2 %); a face looking into the lens within 1.8 m in ~0.6 %; standing 7–9 % of the time (was 30 %); 28 m walked per 30 s (was 22). The parts, all rider-only: **lateral** a soft 1.2 m pass seen 5 m ahead (taken whenever the paving has room, so an oncoming walker veers off early) over a hard 1.0 m one (no way round: the rider stops 1.25 m short, the other turns back while still 4 m away instead of walking up to the lens); the intervals are planned in two passes (soft ones count only while there is a way round them all). **Brakes** only for someone the line of travel would pass closer than the hard gap (round 2 braked for anyone within ±50°, so a pass at the berth stopped both at 1.5 m: the face-off); braked with a way round, a walker sidesteps there (standing, it had no walk to carry the shift). **Following** at 1.8 m, dropping back up to 1.6 m more after 2 s on the same back, overtaking a slower one where the paving has room (soft), and after 4 s going another way than them at the next join. **Zebras** the rider steps off only when nobody going its way on the zebra is within its follow distance (`zebraClear`) and with 1.5 s of the walk window left (then only a car stops it); out on the carriageway nobody stops for the berth (the usual gaps, a soft 1.0 m pass) but the rider follows at ≥ 0.5 m/s 1.8 m back (the in-line gap ahead of it on a carriageway: ≥ 1.4 m, was 0.54). Coming off onto the far landing, the people waiting there step aside, away from it (a step back is toward the corner it makes for), and it never turns back past its near kerb. **Kids** (`riderK` 1.3) scale every distance in front and the lateral gaps. **Props** get a soft 0.3 m more (a lamp post a hand's breadth from the lens fills the frame). The rest is round 2's: a soft push of others off to 0.9 m (1.3 m × riderK in front of the face), never of the rider; held up by the berth, others give up 2.5× sooner; the inside-corner steering fix.
- [v2-L1] The ridden walker waits at a kerb 1.25 m back (others 0.5 m), stepping back to it facing the road if the ride starts at the kerb: the critic saw a turning taxi and a bus pass 0.7 m from the eye of a kerb waiter. Riding someone's eyes, the people renderer's near-eye dither band moves in from 0.83–1.0 m to 0.45–0.62 m: the berth keeps everyone outside it, and people standing at the berth were otherwise drawn as a dot screen. Round 3: on a pavement the rider walks on the side away from the carriageway (`EdgeInfo.roadSide`; the critic saw a turning bus 1 m from the eye): the nearest vehicle body to the eye after a ride's first seconds is ≥ ~1.0 m over 96 rides (round 2: 0.55 m), ≥ 1.2 m in all but a few corner turns. The dither band follows `ctx.view.ride` as well as `setRidden` (riding a kid, D1's setRidden was off at moments and a torso was dot-screened at 0.9 m), and passers-by within ~2 m of the lens look where they are going, not into the camera.
- [v2-L1] Names: every walker and driver a different name. Pools of 145 women's, 159 men's and 49 kids' names (traffic/names.ts); walkers draw from each pool in walker order counting up from a seeded start, drivers count down from just before it (`nameAt`), so the two never meet (126 men walkers + ≤ 20 men drivers fit). Walker i's name depends only on walkers 0…i, so the low tier keeps the high tier's names. Round 3: the line under a walker's card is counted too (people/track.ts `walkerCards`): a dog walker's dog from a 40-name pool (every dog a different name; 'rufus' had four owners), everyone else's from 32 lines stepping 7 at a time (no line on more than 7 walkers, never on two in a row; 'thinking about lunch' was on 22).
- [v2-L1] A bus's live line always names its next stop, never the road it is on or turning onto (round 2 said 'next stop: ring road' on every card: the plan has only two shelters, and one is named after the ring road): a shelter ahead on its lane, else the junction it is coming to by its landmark ('clock tower', 'the plaza', 'park gate') or cross street, else a shelter or the junction past its next turn, else the nearest landmark in front (traffic/track.ts `nextStop`, `junctionName`; names.ts `landmarks`). Buses roam the v1 graph (no fixed route) and do not stop; real routes and stops are V1's (phase 2), which should replace `nextStop` with the route's.
- [v2-L1] The featured walker (rideable from orbit) is the best of: distance from the view's focus (counted up to 40 m, so from orbit everyone is equally far), walking (−8), company within 8 m (−3.5 each, up to 6; counted twice a second) and the plaza or park (−6), with 5 points of hysteresis. Round 1 took the nearest, which from the named orbit view was a lone walker on an empty street at the city's edge. Round 3: walking briskly (> 1 m/s) scores −8 and ambling −3, and someone walking at them within 4 m (or right in front within 1.8 m) +12, so a ride from orbit never lands on a face-off.
- [v2-L1] The rider skips crossings it would stand at: 4 m before a crossing it predicts the walk window on arrival, and if it would wait more than 4 s (or arrive in the window's last 1.5 s), or the landing is too short for its berth behind people already waiting there, it takes another way on (`chooseNext(i, dry)`). Standing at a kerb more than 5.5 s with the window not about to open (a car holding it), or a back right in front of it on a short landing, it gives up and walks on along the pavement. Held up anywhere else, it turns round after ~1.75 s (others 3.5 s). The critic saw 11.7 s of 20 standing at one zebra.
- [v2-L1] The walker just ridden walks on out of the camera: for 4 s after a ride ends the camera is no obstacle to it (`PeopleSim.free`); the critic saw it freeze 0.3 m behind a still camera, which explore treated as a person in its path. (Its body crosses the near-eye dither band for ~0.25 s as it walks off.)
- [v2-L1] In shot mode the traffic sim is on its first pack mesh's `userData.trafficSim` (as the people sim is), so review scripts measure vehicle bodies against the eye. The boat loops search further rings when the listed ones do not hold every boat (R1's terrain moved the coast: the lighthouse anchor found one boat fewer).
- [v2-D1] The camera director (`camera/director.ts`) layers the modes on top of v1's explore model, which is untouched: its update moved into `exploreUpdate()` and runs only while the mode is explore (or blending back into it); the director watches clicks and hover there and otherwise stays out. Rides live in `camera/rides/` (`rig.ts` chase / eyes / alongside, `blend.ts` transitions), the bird in `camera/bird/` (`flight.ts`, `cam.ts`, `mesh.ts`, a stage-2 `bird` system registered in `core/systems.ts`, `shared.ts` for its render state). Pure parts are spec'd (`rides.spec.ts`, `bird/flight.spec.ts`), and `rides/director.spec.ts` drives the real system with synthetic trackables on the real track service.
- [v2-D1] (Round 1; since refine 1 this is the hand-back to explore only — enters are planned paths, below.) Transitions blend from a *coasting snapshot* of the drawn camera (it carries on with its own velocity and turn, decaying at 3/s, so a hand-over never freezes a moving camera) to the live pose of the new mode, eased by smootherstep (zero velocity and acceleration at both ends) over 1.2–2 s by distance (×1.4 with reduced motion). Positions travel round the planet centre (direction slerped, altitude in log space, plus a hop); the *look point* travels the same way and the camera keeps looking at it, so the view stays on the world (a plain quaternion slerp spent half of an orbit → far-side plane trip staring into space). The lift over the arc is planned to clear what lies between (`LiftProfile`: per-sample needs over roofs under the arc, crowns and lamp heads it would pass through, + 1.5 m, at least a sin hop for long trips; slope-limited ramps, smoothed, read back C1), so a downtown street start climbs just over the roofs instead of a symmetric hop tall enough for the worst sample (that flung it 400 m up); a last-resort lift fades out with the blend. The gaze leads the eye by a sixth of the trip (look where you are going, then go).
- [v2-D1] Leaving a mode: the snapshot coasts to rest and explore is handed that rest pose (`handoff()`: focus, zoom altitude net of any roof lift, heading; the pitch as a look offset at street level, else as the setView-style override, held 0.5 s then eased onto the altitude curve over 1.7 s, 2.4 s reduced). Roll, lens and shift blend back over 0.95 s. Exiting a walker's eyes lands you walking where they stood; a plane at 70 m hands you a gentle tip-down into the orbit view.
- [v2-D1] Chase and alongside framings raise their elevation with the horizon dip (`elevationFor`: the limb sits ~40 % up the frame). On this planet the horizon is 46° below level at a plane's 70 m and 64° beside the station: a classic low chase showed only sky. The station's default framing is therefore high and beside it, the station against the planet's disc; dragging up to 88° is the cupola look-down. The bird camera does the same.
- [v2-D1] Chase rigs are attached to the target (no positional lag, the subject stays put in frame); only the heading is smoothed (a critically damped angular spring, so a U-turn rotates instead of collapsing a vector), the height lightly (suspension), and a third of a plane's bank is passed to the camera. Buildings, hills, crowns and lamp heads between the target and the camera pull it in fast and let it out gently; it never dips under terrain, water or a roof.
- [v2-D1] Eyes: the wheel pulls back over the shoulder (up to 9 m, occlusion-aware) and in again, so eyes ↔ third person is one zoom; looks drift back to ahead after 2.5 s idle; head bob is the owner's (in the pose) and is filtered out (ω 3) with reduced motion. `setRidden(true)` from the frame the drawn camera is within 1.1 m of the eye anchor (1.6 m since refine 2), so the body never fills the lens on the way in.
- [v2-D1] Input in a ride: drag orbits / looks, wheel / pinch / Q-E set the distance, zooming out past the widest framing exits (a stop since refine 1, below), `[ ]` cycle within the kind, a click on another trackable rides it, a double-click on the ground exits and flies there, and the movement keys *take over* (exit here; the held keys carry on walking or panning in explore). Esc exits: caught on `window` in the capture phase while a mode is active and the game has the user's attention, so the floating window's close-on-Esc does not fire; the next Esc closes the window as before.
- [v2-D1] Bird: an arcade flight model (coordinated-turn banking, flight-path angle springs, gravity along the path mostly paid back by flapping on a climb, drag to a 13 m/s cruise, dive to ~38 m/s). Soft floor with a 1.6 s look-ahead and a *swerve*: when the floor ahead asks for a steep climb it looks ±38° and steers to the clearer side, so the bird weaves round towers instead of climbing their walls; facades taller than the bird push it along (wall first, then the hard floor, so a roof never pops it up); crowns and lamp heads are soft only (it may brush a crown, never jumps). Ceiling `SPACE_MIN − 12`. Steering: keys, the mouse drag as a joystick (relative to where the press began), the touch stick (offered in bird mode too); a click or tap flaps. Its camera swings round after the flight line (critically damped, ω 5) but looks ahead along the live one, so in a long carve the bird stays near the middle with its way ahead in view; a climb counts less than a dive in the camera's offset (never below the bird staring at the sky); buildings and countryside crowns between bird and camera pull it in, facades push it 2.4 m out (since refine 2: the boom swings away instead). Exit lets the bird fly on (autopilot) and pop away when ≥ 55 m off or after 7 s; `fly()` within 70 m takes it back.
- [v2-D1] The bird is a stage-2 system so its one toon program compiles behind the reveal: a lofted bluebird (vertex colours, ink by depth), flap / elbow / tuck in the vertex shader with normals rotated in the colour pass only (`LB_BIRD_COLOR`), one draw + shadow while out, none otherwise. It pops in with a springy scale (an ease with reduced motion).
- [v2-D1] The far plane reaches the space layer from anywhere (`clipPlanes`: horizon(eye) + horizon(`SPACE_MAX`) + 40); depth precision is set by the near plane, so the cost is nil. Rides set their own near plane (0.05 in the eyes, up to 2 m beside the station).
- [v2-D1] In a ride, `ctx.view` describes the drawn camera (focus under it, `alt` above the ground reference, `street` false); `view.mode / ride` are written every frame and at once on a switch. `CameraService.subject()` (S1) returns the ridden trackable's pose or the bird; `hover()` (U1) is the camera's own ≤ 10 Hz pick (~0.03 ms), which also sets the pointer cursor. `getView()` in a ride returns the drawn camera (the window → `/planet` hand-off).
- [v2-D1] With time frozen (`?shot=1`) the director and explore advance with `ctx.time.dt`, not wall time, so the review tool's rAF warm-ups never move a ride, the bird or a hand-back between frames. v1's shots snap every spring, so they are unchanged.
- [v2-D1] Review hooks: `ride(id, {live})`, `fly({live})`, `exitMode({live})` (settled at once unless live), `mode()`, `trackables(kind?)`, `birdInput({steer, climb, flap, dive} | null)`, `advance(seconds, fps)`; shot flags `--ride <id>`, `--bird`, `--live`, `--pre <s>`, `--steer s,c[,flap,dive]`, `--tracks`; riding `--seq` frames advance at 60 Hz. No synthetic trackables were needed (L1 and S1 registered theirs during the phase).
- [v2-D1, refine 1] Enter transitions are planned paths (`camera/rides/path.ts`; the coasting blend stays for hand-backs). The ground track is the great circle to an approach point Q on the new view's own axis behind the end pose (25 % of the trip, ≤ 45 m, shortened by what is in the way; into someone's eyes, from above their shoulder), and the last stretch Q → end runs straight along that axis, so the camera arrives looking where it will keep looking. Height along the track: log-height eased between the ends (a climb rises early along a ≥ 45° line, a descent stays high and drops late), raised over what stands within 4 m of the track (roofs + 3.5 m, lamp heads and crowns + 1.2 m, terrain + 1.2 m) by slope-limited ramps (0.9 m/m). A start under that envelope begins with a vertical rise and an end under it finishes with a drop; leaving a street for further than the next block, the first rise clears everything within 9 m (a crane up out of the street, so it never travels past upper floors). A hop that would climb over a tower tries tracks bulging round it either side (18–55 % of the trip, ≤ 160 m) and keeps the cheapest; a low hop never climbs into the cloud band. Corners rounded by Chaikin. Critic r1: the round-1 arc flew into and along facades and skimmed roofs (it only reacted to what was under the eye).
- [v2-D1, refine 1] (Superseded by refine 2's clock and time map, below.) Enter timing: one smootherstep clock over the path's *effective* length — metres weighted by 1 / (height + 30 m, or distance to the nearer end + 12 m)^0.65, so what you see streams past at a steadier angular rate (an unhurried climb out of a street, a quick trip round the planet, a dolly-like settle onto the subject), ×2.2 in the cloud band, + 2.2 per radian the view would turn toward the subject; the density averaged over ±4 % of the path so no change of pace steps the speed. Duration 0.85 + 0.45 ln(1 + effective length), 1.2–2.4 s, +0.3 s when the path falls through the cloud layer (the user asked for that moment to last), stretched (≤ 3 s) until it peaks under 420 m/s (≤ 7 m a frame; S1 treats a 25 m frame step as a teleport). Deviation from V2 §4's ≈ 1.2–2 s for the long trips: up to a satellite round the planet takes ~3 s. Critic r1: speeds jumped 8 → 228 m/s in a step and streaked at 600+ m/s.
- [v2-D1, refine 1] (Superseded by refine 2's enter orientation, below.) Enter orientation: the look direction turns from the direction of the old look point to the direction of the new one, as seen from where the camera is (an even turn however far either point is); each end's own orientation is swung (least rotation) onto that look and the two twisted into each other about it (no up-vector singularity looking straight down: round 1's spun the view at 700°/s), pulled toward a level horizon mid-way unless looking steeply. The gaze holds while the camera pops up out of a street and starts turning once it can see 30 m toward the subject (otherwise it turned to face the facade the subject was behind); something up in the sky (a plane, a satellite) is looked up at from the start. Falling through the clouds the gaze is already on the subject: the turn ends at the band, and a climb is given the time it needs before it (`EnterPath.delayCross`, a smooth time multiplier), so S1's overlay plays with the plane or satellite on top of it. A subject behind the planet at the crossing cannot be seen: the crossing then passes without it (S1's wait). Critic r1: 400–600°/s end-of-move pans; the satellite never on screen in the clouds.
- [v2-D1, refine 1] A ride starts framed from the side the camera arrives from (the heading it had: chase ±75°, eyes ±50°, alongside ±120° from its default), held through the transition, then swings round behind (eyes: back to ahead) on a slow critically damped spring (ω 2 chase, 1.5 alongside, 2.2 eyes). No half-turn of the whole view on the way down; the swing after arrival reads as the camera settling in.
- [v2-D1, refine 1] (Superseded by refine 2's follower, below.) The drawn camera's turn is capped at 170°/s during a transition and for 0.6 s after (200°/s in bird mode): a last resort the planning keeps idle — never engaged for city → car, orbit → plane, street → plane, street → walker, car → next car, exits, orbit → bird, orbit → station; engaged ~1 s for street → a satellite on the far side of the planet (the turn before the clouds and the last approach).
- [v2-D1, refine 1] A frame with no time step (the review tool's rAFs while time is frozen) only redraws: no spring, occlusion or collision update. They used to snap on dt = 0 (heading, occlusion, lift): the critic's step log had 83 one-frame spikes up to 33°. Frozen-time runs are now identical with and without rAFs between steps.
- [v2-D1, refine 1] Bird: a facade push-out no longer projects the heading onto the wall in one step (a 58° snap the camera followed); the heading turns onto the wall's tangent at ≤ 2.4 rad/s (1.3 since refine 2), never reflected. The swerve picks a side (the clearer one, or the way the player leans) and keeps it ≥ 0.8 s (it flipped left-right-left at a tower). The camera's look-ahead follows a smoothed flight direction (ω 7) and its own facade push goes through a spring. Found while spec'ing it: the chase rig and the bird camera fed their floor lift back into its own target (`need = floor − (h − lift)`), so a camera held under a roof climbed without bound — fixed in both (likely the critic's "cut to an unrelated view"). Camera 3.5 m back (was 4.4): the bird is ~17 % of the frame's height. Markings: an orange bar across each wing, cream wing tips and tail band, a two-tuft crest; it reads from behind over sea and grass.
- [v2-D1, refine 1] The wheel's widest framing is a stop: a flick that reaches it holds there; a new gesture after 0.3 s at rest there, or 0.4 s of zooming on out against it, exits (keys and pinch the same). Critic r1: one ordinary flick left a plane ride. (A "scroll again to leave" hint would be U1's.)
- [v2-D1, refine 1] Contract (additive): optional `ViewState.lastRide` / `rideFade` — the Trackable ridden last, and 1 → 0 over 1.5 s after its ride ends. For L1: ease the plane / balloon dodge back in by (1 − rideFade) when `lastRide` is its id, instead of switching it on the frame `ride` clears (the plane's jump on exit, critic r1).
- [v2-D1, refine 1] Alongside: the look point sits 11 % of the camera distance left of the station, so it frames right of centre, clear of U1's follow card (top left).
- [v2-D1, refine 1] Specs: `rides/path.spec.ts` (exact ends; straight up beside a 20 m block before travelling; 4 m from what it passes; round a lone tower, never into the clouds on a hop; < 20 % speed change frame to frame for street → space, orbit → car and car → car; the crossing delay without a speed step), `bird/cam.spec.ts` (a minute of random steering and dives into a grid of 14–34 m blocks, three seeds: camera turn < 4° a frame, no step 3× its neighbours, the bird never inside a block), `rides/director.spec.ts` (+ turn per frame ≤ the cap on every enter, street → walker keeps ≥ 2 m from facades once off the start, the wheel stop). Long camera specs carry explicit 30 s timeouts (two timed out under load in round 1).
- [v2-D1, refine 2] Enter timing, replacing refine 1's single smootherstep clock and `delayCross` stretch: a clock (`clockU`) that ramps in over 0.28 s and out over 42 % of the trip (a click is answered at once: from orbit the old clock barely moved for 0.6–0.8 s), then a *time map* (`TimeMap`) from the clock's progress to the path's: 128 samples along the planned path, each given the larger of its share of the planned pace, the time its planned view turn needs at ≤ 120°/s, and the time its metres need at ≤ 2.4·(h + 8) m/s; no sample under 1/1.18 of its neighbour's time (so the speed changes < 18 % per sample); read back as a monotone cubic (no speed step at a sample). Short of time the turn limit gives way first, never the speed limit (round 1 of this rework squeezed the rest of the trip to 6× the speed limit). Duration 1.2–3.6 s (×1.4 reduced); a start high up sets off at once (the near-end slowdown counts its own height). Deviation: up to 3.6 s (V2 §4 says ≈ 1.2–2 s); the long ones are a satellite round the planet and orbit → a plane on the far side. Critic r2: dead starts from high views, hard stops in rotation.
- [v2-D1, refine 2] A target that goes off its predicted course (a car turning, a U-turn in a turnaround) is followed live, so the view could turn faster than planned: the clock slows there (`stepEnter`: trial steps, to ≥ half speed, recovering at 2/s, never past ~1.4× the planned length). Moving targets are planned twice, the second time for when the camera really gets there. A last-resort lift keeps the camera out of a roof the live target drew it under, seen 0.15 s ahead and risen to through a spring (it used to jump up to 7 m in a frame).
- [v2-D1, refine 2] The drawn camera follows the wanted orientation within a rate *and an acceleration* (`TurnFollower`: 170°/s and 1400°/s² in a transition and its 0.6 s tail, 200°/s and 1400°/s² for the bird; the wanted turn fed forward, the error closed along a braking curve), so a turn always eases in and out — refine 1's rate-only cap held a whip-pan at 170°/s and released it within a frame. Explore is never limited outside a hand-back's tail (the follower opens up over 0.3 s and lets go).
- [v2-D1, refine 2] Enter orientation, replacing refine 1's: the gaze turns from the old look direction to the new one; a start looking steeply down (orbit, the city view) keeps its direction from the first metre (the point left under it tipped the gaze back past the vertical); mid-way the gaze is never steeper than 50° low down rising to 65° (more where the planet fills the view, never past 80°), lifted with its own heading kept — looking straight down, a heading change could only be a spin of the city (critic r2: street → walker spun while looking down); looking up at a plane or satellite it dips ≤ 16° under it so the planet's limb stays in the lower frame. Roll: a level horizon mid-way, each end's own at its ends, blended as angles kept continuous against the plan (a quaternion slerp between frames half a turn apart flipped). Into someone's eyes the gaze aims 25 m out along the view's axis (their own look point 8 m ahead swung the gaze round as the camera came down onto it). A subject behind the planet is aimed at on the horizon below it, where it will rise.
- [v2-D1, refine 2] Up to something behind the planet (a satellite on the far side), the path stays under the cloud band (28 m) and travels round toward it until it has risen over the horizon as seen from the band's bottom, then climbs through at 58°: S1's falling-through-clouds overlay then always has the satellite in frame (critic r2: 7 of 10 satellites were below the horizon from the street, and their crossings played with no overlay at all). The height profile is one arc per trip — up to a single peak and down, never a dip between two climbs (critic r2: a roller coaster over the plaza) — and a descent never rises over its start. The turn onto the final approach is a curve over ±35 % of the approach (≤ 25 m), not a corner taken at 70 m/s.
- [v2-D1, refine 2] Into someone's eyes: the final approach comes down over the shoulder at 45° (was 33°: the head showed at the bottom of the frame) and `setRidden(true)` from 1.6 m (the owner hides the head within 1.2 m of the lens): no head blob, no lens inside it. The eyes may arrive looking up to 95° off the walking direction — framed along the way the camera came in, so the trip never loops round the walker or passes over the point it will look at — and turn back to ahead like a head (ω 1.5).
- [v2-D1, refine 2] Exits: velocity across frames is tracked with explicit teleport flags (setView, shots, instant switches), not a `> 400 m/s` guess — the guess zeroed real 900 m/s motion, so Esc mid-trip stopped the camera dead (critic r2). The coast keeps the camera's own speed and bleeds it off within min(150 m, 0.3·h + 6 m) (it was clamped to 60 m/s at once). Up in the air or half way through a trip, explore takes over looking at least 20° down at the world (Esc left the critic staring at the sky).
- [v2-D1, refine 2] Bird camera: never shoved sideways. A building between bird and camera shortens the boom along the line of sight (a zoom, not a turn; ω 9 in, 2.5 out, never through the blocker); a facade within 2.4 m of where the camera would be swings the boom round the bird, away from it, through a spring on the angle (ω 3.2), with the lens kept out of walls only as a last resort. Round 1 pushed the camera out of the clearance through a stiff spring: at a corner the push flipped and threw it across the street at 70 m/s with the bird out of the frame (critic r2 blocker). Its look-ahead follows a smoother flight direction (ω 4.5), the floor lift ω 8.
- [v2-D1, refine 2] Bird flight: walls ahead on the turn it is actually flying (the arc, not the straight line; the soft floor's look-ahead too) are steered off at a 2.8 m berth — the player's steer into the wall gives way smoothly — so it slides past a facade instead of scraping along it; the heading turns ≤ 72°/s (a slow bird at full bank carved a 4 m circle) and, with a wall slide on top, ≤ 83°/s. `bird/cam.spec.ts` gains: 6 s held low among 14–34 m blocks steering hard into them (5 starts) and 3 s steering into a 60 m tower (6 starts): bird within |NDC| < 0.8, camera speed < 2× the bird's, view turn < 120°/s. Measured: ≤ 0.54 NDC, ≤ 1.07×, ≤ 80°/s.
- [v2-D1, refine 2] Bird launch: from anywhere under the space layer the bird appears 2 m ahead of and under the lens, flying 8 m/s, its chase camera running from the first frame with a 2 m boom easing out to 3.5 m and no facade swing yet; a 0.4 s blend looks at the bird from the start (critic r2: it flew off to a speck while a 1 s transition caught up). From orbit it is still an enter down to it.
- [v2-D1, refine 2] Specs (additive): `rides/director.spec.ts` — orbit → plane, high over the city → a car that turns a corner the prediction did not expect, street → a walker 50 m off: worst view turn < 150°/s and no frame-to-frame change of angular velocity > 40°/s; Esc half way through a fast trip: every frame keeps ≥ 75 % of the last one's speed, then rest, looking down. `rides/rides.spec.ts`: the coast keeps its speed and glides ≤ maxGlide. `rides/path.spec.ts`: the frame-to-frame speed criterion gains 0.05 % of the height as slack (from orbit it now sets off at once). Known: re-riding mid-crossing lets S1's episode for the old subject play out with the new one off-screen (S1 could shorten it when `view.ride` changes).
- [v2-S1] Falling through the clouds is ONE overlay owned by the clouds system (the v1 veil and its unused scene blocker are gone, so there is never a double white-out). An episode (`clouds/crossing.ts`, pure + spec'd) starts on a real CROSSING of the layer (anywhere on the planet: zoom, dive, ride, bird): the eye inside the 34–50 m band (or jumping over it in a frame), moving vertically > 1.2 m/s and headed out of its FAR side (altitude predicted 0.3 s ahead in log space, which is how the zoom spring moves, beyond the band); or on entering a puff. A zoom that stops inside the layer to look at the clouds never fires one (refine round: entering the band was enough, and a 2-tick zoom to 37 m whited out clear air). Each direction re-arms once the eye is back on its starting side (above the middle and climbing, or out of the top; mirrored), so a bird porpoising in the layer fires at most once. Teleports (setView, shots) never start one: the `clouds` / `cloudscape` shots are unchanged.
- [v2-S1] The overlay's look: cartoon puffs in log-polar cells round the focus of expansion ((θ, ln r) is conformal: round on screen, small near the focus, big at the edges; streaming the cells outward in ln r is how lumps of cloud grow and rush past). Two layers (near 6 columns, far 11, different stream rates: parallax), three toon domes per puff (lit cap, lavender shade, crease, belly edge, the 3D clouds' palette at the eye's time of day), a soft body behind them once covered, speed lines along the motion. Coverage is evaluated at each puff's centre, so the fronts are made of whole puffs growing and shrinking, never a circle. A first per-pixel version (neighbour search over both layers) cost +5 ms at 1280×800: each puff is now one instanced screen-space sprite whose cell, size and streaming come from the vertex shader (static instance attributes, uniforms only): +0.3 ms at full cover (the shared GPU makes single runs swing to ~1 ms).
- [v2-S1] Refine round (superseded in round 3, below), timing: in 0.08 s, hold 0.1 s, out 0.34 s (params `clouds.crossIn / crossHold / crossOut`; reduced motion 0.2 / 0.04 / 0.3 with a 0.85 ceiling, so the fade never flashes and the fogged city stays faintly visible). That is ~0.5 s from the first puff to clear, of which ~0.29 s has at least half the frame covered (spec'd: 0.25–0.4 s at 30/60/144 fps) — the user's "like 0.25 s" read as visible cover, with the reveal on top. The hold ends early once the eye is 6 m clear of the band, so a fast zoom shows the city rising through the hole instead of a white wall ending at street level. The hole's radius eases out (x^0.75: the uncovered area grows with its square), and the whole overlay fades over the last 28 % of the opening: from ≥ 90 % to ≤ 2 % cover takes ≥ 8 frames at 30 fps wherever the eye flies (spec'd for five focus positions, no frame removing more than a fifth of the frame).
- [v2-S1] Refine round, fronts and puffs: the fronts are measured in ON-SCREEN radius fractions (`frontRange`: nearest screen point → farthest corner) — with the focus of expansion off-screen the old opening swept mostly outside the frame and the overlay vanished in 1–3 frames. The focus is clamped to 0.3 beyond the frame (far off it every on-screen log-polar puff was a frame-sized arc), puff radii are floored near the focus (no popcorn) and capped (0.24 / 0.15 of the frame height), each puff grows from nothing with the front and leaves the hole a step before the body's edge, and the body's edges are scalloped into round lumps with a shaded rim (the hard body edge between puffs left torn slivers). During an episode the clouds keep streaming at ≥ 8 m/s along the last motion and the speed lines keep a floor, so a slowing zoom no longer freezes into a still pale wall.
- [v2-S1] Refine round, rides: if the ridden thing is off-screen when a crossing fires (D1's transition still turning to it), the episode waits up to 0.4 s for it to come into frame and then plays, so the station / plane is drawn on top of the clouds instead of being lost behind them; if it never shows, the crossing passes without an episode (a real puff still whites out). Live ride to the station from `rooftops`: the trigger waits ~0.15 s, the station is on top of the overlay throughout. Riding a plane through the layer can't be seen live yet (no flight crosses it until A2); `?p.clouds.force=1` holds an episode for stills and paired perf A/B (`--p clouds.force=1 --shot flight`).
- [v2-S1] Refine round, the dive: at the /play clip's time the cloud layer has drifted ~3.9° about the city's axis since t = 0, and 26° off that axis it put the cloudlet 6 m off the track: the clip flew past it (no contact; the old band trigger hid it). The dive's anchors are now placed where the drifting layer will be at `DIVE_CROSS_T` (15.471 s, spec'd against the live dive in `dive-anchors.spec.ts`), so the clip really flies into the cloud: it fills the right of the frame, the camera enters it, ~0.3 s of cartoon cloud, and the hole opens over downtown through the base mist.
- [v2-S1] Contract (additive): `SkyService.cross?` — the overlay's level (0..1). REQUEST for post (not S1's file): fade tilt-shift by it, `tilt *= 1 - (sky.cross ?? 0)` next to the existing `(1 - veil)`, but not the ink (the followed thing over the overlay keeps its outline). Tilt-shift is full strength at cloud altitude and blurs the overlay's top and bottom bands.
- [v2-S1] REQUEST for post (not S1's file): the night bloom keys on warm colour where `lbNightAt(world)` says night, which includes the space layer above the night side, where bodies are often sunlit: gold foil and goldie's mirror bloom into lantern halos up close. `n *= 1.0 - smoothstep(60.0, 100.0, length(wp) - R)` in the bloom prefilter would keep window lights (all near the ground) and drop it up there.
- [v2-S1] (Round 3 replaced the single depth with a near-plane overlay and a window round the subject, below.) The overlay draws INSIDE the scene pass (renderOrder 1000–1001, LAYER_NO_INK), at the NDC depth just behind the followed thing (`CameraService.subject` or the ridden Trackable; the near plane in explore), with the depth test on: whatever is nearer (the bird, the plane, a satellite on the way to a ride) stays on top, pixel-exact, inked. A screen-space window round the subject was tried first and read as a porthole. The overlay also writes that depth where it is ≥ 50 % opaque (its antialiased fringe below that is discarded, FXAA smooths the edge), so post's depth ink never draws the hidden city over the clouds. Reduced motion: the soft body alone fades in and out (no puffs, fronts, streaming or speed lines; no depth write, so post's veil fades the ink with it). While a subject is near the layer the 3D puffs in front of it dissolve (dithered, uniform-gated `uHole` in the puff shader) and the white-out fog is pushed past it.
- [v2-S1] The overlay's clock is sim time in shot mode (`ctx.time.dt`) and real time live: the review tool's warm frames (frozen sim, real rAFs) used to age the v1 veil between clip frames.
- [v2-S1] Space layer (`space/`): a cartoon station (truss with gold masts, eight blue wings, banded modules, radiators, a cupola, a docked capsule) and ten satellites with distinct silhouettes (chatterbox: gold box, long wings, big dish; drizzle-1: drum with ONE wing and a lens; peeper: telescope tube with its lid flipped open; tri-cubes; kite: pink/lilac solar sail; pathfinder: four panels and an antenna forest; bat: radar wing; spinny: spinning cell drum; beep: the shiny ball with whiskers; goldie: five-layer sunshield and a gold hex mirror). Closed-form inclined orbits, each body at its own height (shells ≥ r₁ + r₂ + 4 m apart, spec'd: they never meet); a body passing near the eye slides aside (`dodge`, spec'd) except the ridden one. One merged geometry + one custom toon program for every body (per-body transform, panel angle, sunlit factor, reveal in a uniform array: ONE draw), one points draw for beacons and glints: 2 draw calls for the layer.
- [v2-S1] Space look: wings turn 70 % of the way to the sun (fully tracking panels are edge-on from above and below: a toy's wings stay readable); the sail and goldie face the sun whole; thin membranes glow through when backlit; goldie's mirror always gleams. The planet's shadow is a soft cylinder (R ± 6 m): at this scale a body 150 m up stays sunlit over most of the night side, so the station glides over the city at night as a lit toy and goes dark only near local midnight. A sunlit glint per body makes far satellites read as moving stars from the ground at night (gone in the shadow, gone up close). From inside the air by day the bodies haze toward the sky (tiny pale shapes). Warm colours were kept off the big surfaces: post's night bloom keys on warm light above the night side (window lights), and amber wings or a coral sail glowed like lanterns there.
- [v2-S1] Orbits are composed for the first look: the station crosses the `orbit` shot over the dusk side at t = 0 (sunlit against dark ground) and sweeps across downtown in the /play clip's first second (t ≈ 12); it is retrograde, with a 96 s lap. Refine round: the satellites' nodes, phases and periods (inclinations kept) were searched against the `orbit` view's frustum and the planet's occlusion so it always has some in frame — two or more bodies ~90 % of the time over two hours (was 55 %; mean 3.1 in frame), an empty sky only for moments — and they are drawn 1.4× (`SAT_SCALE`, radii and shell spacing re-spec'd: heights 115…292 m). Each body trails a dotted line of 22 dots back along its orbit (80 m), in the lights draw (still 2 draw calls), seen only from space and only while the body is under ~36 px: from orbit a satellite reads as a moving thing on a path, not a speck. Trackables `station:0`, `satellite:0…9` (view 'alongside', labels and lines in the site voice, `detail()` with height, speed, lap and what it is over); the station has a `WorldLabel` that tracks it. Param `space.show` (A/B and review).
- [v2-S1] Refine round, space look: in the planet's shadow a flat cartoon fill (#5a5f9e × 0.62) keeps each part's colour (satellites went to navy blobs) plus a stronger cool rim. Post's depth ink barely reaches the station (above 30 m its crease damping is tripled for the clouds, and dark ink on dark space vanishes), so the body shader inks its own curved silhouettes: a ~1.5 px line where |N·V| < 2.4 fwidth(N·V), gated to surfaces with a normal gradient (modules, tanks, the telescope tube; flat panels and boxes keep post's outline). Peeper: an open aperture (dark disc in a purple rim ring on the capped tube), a hinge pin and bracket holding the lid, a coral band, a star tracker and an antenna (the lid floated, the tube read as a bottle).
- [v2-S1] Round 3, the fill is seen: in 0.24 s, hold 0.02 s, out 0.34 s (~0.3 s at least half covered, ~0.58 s first puff to clear). At tIn = 0.08 s a 30 fps clip went from clear to covered in one frame. The fill front's radius now goes as an eased progress to the ¾ power (the area it covers, ∝ r², then grows evenly) and the body holds back while the fill is young (puffs bloom first; a bare scalloped disc at the focus read as a sticker). Spec'd: from the first cover to ≥ 90 % takes ≥ 4 frames at 30 fps for three focus positions, both directions, no frame adding more than 40 % of the frame. A re-trigger while the hole opens buys the time to close it again (with a 0.02 s hold it bought nothing).
- [v2-S1] Round 3, flying into a puff is anticipated: the eye's straight path 0.85 × tIn ahead (≈ 0.2 s) is tested against every nearby puff's contact ball (the `inside` test's ball, above its flat base, closing in, at > 2 m/s); a hit starts the episode then, so the frame is covered as the eye goes in and the puffs bloom out of the real cloud it is entering (it lies on the path: at the focus of expansion). Contact's instant floor (the near plane must never slice a puff open) used to replace the 3D cloud with the overlay in one frame. The /play dive now blooms over frames 125–129 out of the cloudlet filling the right of the frame.
- [v2-S1] Round 3, flying BACKWARD (climbing out while looking down: a focus of contraction) the fronts run the other way (`Crossing.dir`, latched at the episode's start from the motion's sign, `uDir` in the shaders: fractions measured from the farthest corner): the cloud closes in from the corners like an iris and, leaving, shrinks away into the point the eye climbs from, instead of opening a hole there like a zoom in. Mirror-image fill curve, a near-linear opening (k^0.7, no margin at the focus) so the last cloud shrinks at an even pace (spec'd: ≥ 8 frames from 90 % to 2 % cover at 30 fps, corners clear before the centre).
- [v2-S1] Round 3, one art style: the overlay's puffs carry the 3D clouds' ink (a ~1.4 px line inside each puff's silhouette, 0.6 on the near layer, 0.32 on the far one, so each puff's line falls on the puffs behind it; a faint crease where lobes overlap on the shaded side) and the body's lumpy fronts are inked too; two soft toon bands replaced the hard lavender crescents. Dusk / night contrast: the belly, rim and seam weights deepen by `uTone` = clamp(0.82 / luma(lit), 1, 1.9) (the overlay was one flat lilac sheet at dusk).
- [v2-S1] Round 3, depth: the overlay sits at the near plane and covers everything; following something on screen, a window round it (its projected bounding circle × 1.08, plus a ramp of max(40 px, 5 % of the height)) is drawn by a second pair of programs (`LB_WINDOW`) with a per-pixel `gl_FragDepth` just behind the subject, so it stays on top while a nearer building or 3D puff can only show inside that small window (the ride from `rooftops` to the station showed a building corner and an inked 3D puff over the overlay, frames 016–017). The window's edge is a smoothstep ramp in log depth, wide enough that post's depth ink (a scale-free second difference of 1/z) never finds an edge in it, meeting the plain pair's depth exactly at its rim: no ring round the subject. The plain pair skips the window's pixels, so only the window pays for gl_FragDepth.
- [v2-S1] Round 3, overdraw: the overlay is fragment-bound (~3.4 layers of puff quads plus the body at full cover). The sprites are octagons round the three domes (−20 % fragments vs squares), drawn front to back (near layer outer rows first, then the far layer, then the body, each row slot a 2e-6 NDC step further), so GPUs that test depth early with `discard` reject hidden fragments (Apple's ANGLE path does not: there the gain is the octagons and a leaner puff shader: linear ramps, the dome select branchless, uniform-only work on the CPU). Param `clouds.crossParts` (1 body, 2 puffs) isolates the parts for perf A/B. Measured on a GPU shared with 10–13 other headless browsers, the A/B is noise-dominated (paired medians swing 0.1–0.6 ms between identical runs); best-of numbers are in the report.
- [v2-S1] Round 3, space ink: every face edge is inked by the body shader from per-face edge coordinates (`aEdge`: box faces' UVs, cylinder and cone rims and cap edges, lathe lips, plate outlines; a ~1.2 px line inside each face border, dropped on faces under ~7 px across so far bodies never go solid, except a dish's lip, a thin strip by design). Post's depth ink barely reaches the space layer (its crease damping triples above 30 m), and the station's truss, radiators and wings read as an un-inked plastic render. Capsules (a module cylinder ending in spheres) are not rim-inked: their buried rims z-fought into dashes.
- [v2-S1] Round 3, space look: from space, a body under 21 px (bounding radius) is drawn bigger, up to 3× (never the ridden one; nothing changes from the air or up close), so from the `orbit` view the satellites keep a readable cartoon silhouette instead of a speck; `Trackable.radius` stays the true size (D1 frames rides by it). The sky haze fades off a body as it grows on screen (to 0.3 of it by 40 px) and, seen from inside the air by day, the underside gets a soft warm fill (`uSkyFill`): the station over the street by day reads cream with blue wings instead of a blue-grey ghost. A dish is shaded as a bowl (darker toward its centre, a ring, a bright lip). Spinny lies along its flight and spins about it (`spinZ`), with a coral band and two whip antennas: from above, below, the street or its 'alongside' ride it shows its side, not a ring round a disc.
- [v2-S1] Round 3, still REQUESTS for post (not S1's files), both visible in this round's shots: (1) fade tilt-shift by the overlay, `tilt *= 1 - (sky.cross ?? 0)` (not the ink: the followed thing keeps its outline); (2) drop the night bloom above the space layer's floor, `n *= 1.0 - smoothstep(60.0, 100.0, length(wp) - R)` in the bloom prefilter: sunlit cream hulls, gold foil, a white dish and the pink sail bloom into lantern halos whenever a body is over the night side, up close and from orbit.
- [v2-R1] The region (`world/region/`, contract in `types.ts`) is world space: unit directions plus heights above sea level, `WPath` polylines (≤ 1 m, true-metre arc length) for every centreline, lane, connector and the ferry lane. Each settlement has its own chart (radius R + pad height, +x east / +z south like `CITY_CHART`) for T1's lots; small things (departure angles, turn curves, conflicts) are built in a chart round each node (`network.ts`, the world-space twin of `city/graph.ts`: trimmed centrelines, right-hand lanes at ±width/4, one-way rings on the centreline, Hermite turns, U-turns round end circles, lazy symmetric conflicts, priorities with roundabout entries yielding).
- [v2-R1] Contract (additive): `WorldData.region` (engine: one getter in `WORLD_CITY`, and `void planet.region` inside the `planet` boot mark so the build is attributed there); `Planet.anchors` (range, ridge, windmill hill, neck, headlands, islands, far continent, archipelago, third mass), `Planet.baseHeightAt` (natural + plateau), `Planet.region` (lazy), `createPlanet(seed, { region })`; `Biome.City` also marks the region's paved ground (carriageways, gate plazas, runways, aprons: no scatter, no landmark there). `heightAt()` is the carved terrain; the region is routed over `baseHeightAt` only (no cycle) and built on the first `heightAt()`.
- [v2-R1] The carve never touches the plateau (`heightAt === baseHeightAt` inside `PLATEAU_RADIUS`, fading in over 3 m outside the rim). Primitives (pads, plazas, runways as flat discs / capsules; road beds as 2 m capsule chains) live in a dense 3D grid (6 m cells, 175 k-entry CSR): a query off the network is one array read. A road's target is a soft minimum over its segments (1.5 m), so a tight climbing bend never steps; flat cores lock their level; banks widen 2.6 m per metre of cut / fill up to a per-primitive cap measured from the ground round it (so each primitive lives in few cells).
- [v2-R1] (Round 0; refine 1 below supersedes parts.) Settlements, chosen by scored searches round the anchors (coarse then fine grids, pad flatness from 17 samples): **port pebble** (harbour) on the SW headland — the N headland's tip, where the lighthouse stands (`nature/landmarks.ts` picks the spot with the most sea round it; the search mirrors that score), is kept free, so the lighthouse is where v1 had it on both tiers; **millbrook** (farm) on the windmill hill's city side, off its summit (the three windmills stay put, spec'd); **snowberry** (alpine) on the neck at the range's foot, scored against how far its banks climb the mountain; **coral cove** (resort) on the island nearest a gate, over a bridge; **far haven** (metro, 44 m pad) on the far continent's coast. Pads are flat (±0.05 spec'd), at least 1.75 m (lower reads as beach and floods), and on the capital's continent no higher than a ≤ 8.5 % road can climb from the nearest gate plaza.
- [v2-R1] Capital gates: the v1 cul-de-sacs ARE the gate avenues, run out to the rim (`layout.ts`: the end node sits where the turning circle's sidewalk touches the rim, `GATE_RIM_GAP` 5 cm), kind 'lane' and 5.5 m unchanged so the tuned sims, the viewpoints and the dive treat them as before (city, traffic, people and dive specs green). The region's gate plaza (`GatePlaza`) is a disc on the other side of the touch point, flat at `PLATEAU_HEIGHT` (paved continuously with the city's turnaround), carrying a one-lane roundabout (ring radius from its arm spacing, arms ≥ 80° apart, steered off water).
- [v2-R1] Topology: the rim road joins consecutive gates round the plateau, leaving the widest gap open (the park side: a chain, not a ring); towns hang off gates on spurs; a spur whose straight line would cross the plateau runs round it (A* in a polar band round the capital); the harbour's waterfront street takes the through traffic when it sits on the rim road. Land masses are components (capital's continent, far continent), each strongly connected (spec'd with a lane SCC); the ferry joins them.
- [v2-R1] Roads: A* on a strip along the great circle (or the polar band), 2.5 m cells, costs for grade, turns, water (bridges only where allowed), the shore (keeps banks off the beach), the plateau (rim + 7 m keep-out, preference for 10–16 m out), other roads (no crossing), pads, piers, runways, the windmill summit; string-pulled, filleted, then relaxed (60 Laplacian passes) so bends open to ≳ 6 m radius (spec'd). Profile: smoothed base, ≥ 1 m above the sea on land, decks ≥ 2.4 m over water with abutments on dry ground, pinned to plaza / pad / apron levels inside them, graded ≤ 10 % (spec ≤ 12 %), kinks rounded.
- [v2-R1] Airports: the capital's on a strip reclaimed from the lagoon (0.4–3.2 m deep, water 12 m either side and past both ends, approaches under 1:12 clear, in the afternoon light when it can be), a causeway from a T junction split into the nearest country road (≥ 21 m from the apron) or from a gate within 45 m; far haven's on its flattest inland strip, the city's land gate turned toward it, its height within an 8 % climb. Runway banks in water are steep revetments (×0.35 batter).
- [v2-R1] Ferry: A* over water ≥ 2.5 m deep (1.4 m within 30 m of a berth) in 7 m cells, clear of runways; the two legs pass port to port 6 m apart, the separation easing to nothing at each berth (the ferry turns alongside the pier); berths at the pier ends in ≥ 1.8 m of water.
- [v2-R1] No rail line: the capital's continent is a thin ring of land round the plateau that the rim road already fills (a parallel track would need level crossings at every gate), and the far continent has one city (a line with one station is not a line). `Region.rail` exists (empty) for a later pass.
- [v2-R1] (Round 0; refine 1 below supersedes parts.) Town pads keep the countryside's scatter for now (`Biome.City` on pads took 293 trees, 1579 → 1286, below the scatter spec's living-world floor of 1500): N2 / T1 own clearing them (`region.surface(dir).cls === 'pad'`) and planting the difference elsewhere. Carriageways, plazas, runways are clear.
- [v2-R1] planet.spec's plateau-edge slope check now reads `baseHeightAt` (the plateau's own shape); the carve is checked separately (no seam anywhere across the ring; banks spec'd in `region.spec.ts`: on land ≤ max(0.9, 1.2 × natural + 0.6)). The rim road's fill on the south coast makes a 0.81 bank there.
- [v2-R1] (Round 0; refine 1 below supersedes parts.) `region/` (stage 2, after space): registers a `WorldLabel` per settlement (`town:<id>`, kind capital / city / harbour / village) and per airport (`airport:<code>`), and draws the network debug overlay behind `?p.region.debug=1` (ribbons by kind, bridges red, lanes yellow, connectors cyan, node dots, pads / plazas / greens as rings, runway lines, piers, the ferry lane). New shots (`core/shots.ts`): `region-west / -east / -south / -far`, `town-<id>`, `gate-0..2`, `gate-street`, `bridge-0`, `airport-lbx / -fhv`.
- [v2-R1] Lanes, connectors and the ferry lane are built on first read (non-enumerable getters on the region, and on each edge's `lanesAB` / `lanesBA`): the carve, on the terrain's first-frame path, needs only centrelines. V1's init pays for them (a few ms); the dev deep-freeze leaves them alone.
- [v2-R1] (Round 0; met in refine 1, below.) Build budget (≤ 20 ms) NOT met: cold in the browser (dev build, inside the `planet` boot mark) the region takes 32–45 ms on this machine under heavy load (load average 15–200 during the measurements, with the 30 ms-budget `city plan` measuring 24–34 ms alongside), warm 15–20 ms. Best cold run: sites 8 ms (2.8 k samples), airports 3, towns 2, roads 11 (A* 7, 2.5–3 m cells, weighted heuristic 1.35), carve 6 (typed-array CSR). What is left: the lighthouse-tip mirror and the harbour search (~1 k samples), the capital airport's lagoon scan (~2 k samples), and A* on the 225 m rim road; or bake the region's routed polylines into a generated module at build time (deterministic for the seed).
- [v2-R1, refine 1] (Superseded in parts by refine 2, below.) Geography (`world/planet.ts`, additive exports `VALLEY`, anchors `shoulder` / `valley` / `passAxis`): the windmill hill grew (r 0.245) so millbrook fits beside the three windmills; an outer shelf blob on the SW headland (0.87 rad off the N tip, not the headland itself: enlarging that moved the lighthouse) holds port pebble; island 3 grew (r 0.12) for coral cove; and an alpine VALLEY is shaped between the two peaks (`valleyShape`: a meadow floor plus a ramp corridor up the pass, fading to nothing at the shore over the first 30 % so its mouth has no cliff, dry only on the floor so the tree count holds at 1549 ≥ 1500). The lighthouse tip still wins v1's sea-round score by 4.594 vs 4.590 (the runner-up is another headland): any coast edit near the N tip can flip it, so the spec pins it.
- [v2-R1, refine 1] (Superseded in parts by refine 2, below.) Six settlements, six plans (`towns.ts`, spec'd by street-graph degree signature and per-style shape): **port pebble** (harbour: a back street between two junctions, two streets down to the quay and the pier head), **millbrook** (farm: a crossroads, lanes out to the fields, one on to a second gate), **snowberry** (alpine: the street climbs past two lanes to the chapel square's turning circle; a fixed site in the valley), **coral cove** (resort: a one-way promenade loop round its roundabout), **far haven** (metro: a 4 × 4 grid on a 48 m pad), **clover** (farm, at the far end of far haven's highway). Every town ≥ 4 streets, pads 23–26 m (critic: 22–30), ≥ padR + padR' + 8 m apart, ≥ CITY_PLAN_RADIUS + padR + 3 m off the plateau.
- [v2-R1, refine 1] Network geometry (`network.ts`, spec'd): trimmed edges ≥ 2.5 m (shortest 2.67), every connector a true circular fillet ≥ `RHO_MIN` 4 m (a near-parallel pair, |cross| < 0.035, gets an S-Hermite instead of a 0-radius arc), U-turns swing out round the end circle at ≥ turnR − `U_INSET` (3.8 m), lanes ≥ 3.5 m (3.74). A node's patch radius grows until every arm pair fillets at ≥ 4 m and no two arms leave together (they would read as a U-turn), capped at `R_MAX` 11. One-way edges through a roundabout node (the resort's promenade) circulate with the ring's priority.
- [v2-R1, refine 1] (Superseded in parts by refine 2, below.) Topology: rim road (its north arc dropped: puffin point blocked it; puffin point was dropped too), mill road, coast road millbrook → port pebble (a 10 m bridge), harbour road (split by the airport causeway's T, 7.8 m), alpine road (103 m, two 5.75 m hairpins laid in a pass frame), cove bridge road (27 m span), far haven's airport road, the clover highway (88 m: the long road away from the rim, to the far continent's second settlement), and the lookout road to a viewpoint car park (`Region.lookouts`; the car park sits no higher or lower than its road can climb to it). Two lane SCCs (one per land mass, joined by the ferry).
- [v2-R1, refine 1] Build budget met by a memo (`region/bake.ts`): the build's costly searches (sites, airports, A* routes as f32 control polygons, profile samples in mm, carve depth caps in cm) are cached decisions replayed from a generated string (`region/baked.ts`, 41 entries, ~3.6 KB). Each entry carries a hash of its inputs rounded to 1e-6 (V8 builds differ in the last bits of Math.sin / pow: the browser's terrain must still match) and the bake the base terrain's fingerprint; a mismatch recomputes live (correct, slower). Outputs pass through their encoding on the fresh path too, so a replay is bit-identical; `bake.spec.ts` fails on a stale bake and regenerates it with `LB_REGION_BAKE=1 pnpm vitest run components/littlebig/world/region/bake.spec.ts` (`LB_REGION_NOBAKE=1` for live probes). In the browser: 16.0–16.6 ms with 0 misses and 2 terrain samples (load average ~15), 20–25 ms at load average 25–37 (other agents' browsers); boot: first frame at 221 ms, `init region` 9 ms.
- [v2-R1, refine 1] (Superseded in parts by refine 2, below.) Carve: a road group's soft minimum splits into runs by arc length (`RUN_SPAN` 6 m; a hairpin's legs are two runs blending over `SOFT_FAR` 5 m, so the bank between them eases instead of stepping); bank width / batter / cap are run-weighted too; a segment fades out over the last 2 m of its reach (it is bucketed only in the cells it reaches, so a cell boundary used to cut it off: a 0.9 m seam under the cove bridge). Pads may widen their banks up to `PAD_BATTER_CAP` 11 (others `BATTER_CAP` 6), measured at 0.5, 1 and 1.6 × blend (× 0.7 beyond the blend): snowberry's north edge cuts 20 m into the range, and a 6 m cap made a 49° bank.
- [v2-R1, refine 1] (Superseded in parts by refine 2, below.) Bridges: a span starts ≥ 9 m past the last tighter-than-5 m bend of the raw line at its node (the corner the node's patch swallows), so a causeway leads to every abutment (≥ 5.5 m on the trimmed edge, spec'd). Contract (carve, additive): `Abutment` planes: a road segment near a span carries the plane through the span's end, and past it its fill fades `ABUT_K` 1.5× faster, so the cove bridge crosses open water (spec: a span over 20 m has sea under its middle). The coast road's 10 m span stands over fill: millbrook's and port pebble's pad banks meet across the shallow inlet. A last "clear the water under every span" primitive was tried and dropped: wherever it yielded to the pads it left a filled middle, wherever it did not it cut the pads' edges and left underwater cliffs at its ends.
- [v2-R1, refine 1] Grade: where two pinned samples are closer than `MAX_GRADE` allows (far haven's gate down to its apron, 1.55 m in ~14 m), `gradeProfile` spreads the excess over the stretch (11.5 %, under the 12 % spec) instead of stepping 0.24 m at the pinned end.
- [v2-R1, refine 1] (Superseded in parts by refine 2, below.) Airports (contract additive: `Airport.clear`, `Airport.landEnd`, `Airport.approach` docs): the capital's strip sits on organic island discs in the south lagoon, its causeway a T off the harbour road; both strips keep ≥ 200 m clear under the 1:12 glide past their landing threshold (spec'd).
- [v2-R1, refine 1] (Superseded in parts by refine 2, below.) I disagree with the critic's "≥ 3 towns visible in the orbit shot, facing > 0.45 and lit": the orbit view (lat −6 / lon +26 of the city, 380 m) is composed on the terminator, looking at the capital; its continent is a ring of land ~40–60 m wide round the 90 m plateau, and the sites that face the camera at > 0.45 are on the plateau or in the sea, or on the night side. Moving the v1 composition to show towns would break the v1 shot; the towns are shown by `region-west / -east / -south / -far` instead (all lit, several towns each).

The dead ends are now the gate turning circles out on the rim, an open, empty view the v1 picker started choosing; the dusk shot is now a park-side sidewalk with a lamp and walkers toward the sun (not v1's bus-and-car corner, which the longer gate avenues re-ranked).

New land for it (`world/planet.ts`, additive exports `SHELF`, `CAPE`, anchors `shelf` / `cape`): a broad shelf SW of the windmill hill (millbrook, 250°, 140 m) and a cape on the south coast (port pebble, 172°, 140 m); v1's headlands, the windmill hill and the lighthouse's headland unchanged. Island 3 is back to v1's size; island 0 (east, beyond the shoulder) grew (r 0.15) and stands `ISLE_OUT` 0.045 rad further out, so coral cove's pad and most of its blend ring lie on the island's own land (wet ring ≤ 3/12 in the search) with a channel for the cove road's 23 m bridge. Every gate meets ≥ 2 roads (critic: "a full roundabout for one road"). The capital's airport (lbx) search allows beach to 0.9 m under the strip and five headings, and keeps the strip's island 20 m off any town pad; it now lies in the NW lagoon beside the mill road (the old south lagoon is the cape).

At t = 0 the lit (sun > 7°), face-on, ≥ 25-m-off-the-plateau ground of that view is a crescent ~10 m deep south of the capital: it holds one town, and port pebble stands in it (facing 0.47, sun 11°; its site search prefers the orbit view). Coral cove (0.59) and snowberry (0.61) face it on the night side (their lights are T1's); millbrook is on the western limb. In the game's own start view (camera/index.ts: over the city) port pebble and millbrook are face-on (0.42) in daylight. Spec: ≥ 3 towns facing > 0.18, ≥ 1 lit and > 0.45, ≥ 2 at night and > 0.5; start view ≥ 2 lit and > 0.4.

The alpine road climbs ~10 m in one wide hairpin (r 9.5, legs 19 m apart, so the bank between them stays a slope; it leaves the gate's arm radially), spec'd as needed: the end-to-end grade would be > 1.5 × MAX_GRADE. Its pad is ≤ 26 m: the valley's far side drops to the sea (a bigger pad filled the sea there).
- [v2-R1, refine 2] Town plans by town, not only by style (`towns.ts`, `TownSpecIn.variant`, additive): every town has a 14 m main street and a loop. Farm v0 (millbrook): a crossroads (side arms angled 12° forward: long enough to trim) with green street up to a one-way loop round the village green; farm v1 (clover): a fork, two streets round the green to a top junction and a lane on; alpine: the high street past a lane to a three-node one-way loop round the chapel; harbour: the main street from the back gate down to the pier head on the quay, the quay street along the water round the harbour block, the back street to a corner gate (or a turning circle); resort: a one-way promenade just in from the gate and a villa close. Lane names come from a per-town pool. - [v2-R1, refine 2] Carve (`carve.ts`): a group's target is one continuous soft minimum over its segments (width `SOFT_NEAR` 1.5 m on the carriageway, widening `SOFT_K` 0.8 per metre past the core), each segment pulling with its own bank weight; the run split (RUN_SPAN / SOFT_FAR) is gone: it stepped inside the hairpin's bend and where a far run took over. Road segments are 3 m (was 2: −30 % carve build, specs green). `PAD_BATTER_CAP` 13 (snowberry's sea-side fill). The seam spec now ignores pairs where neither sample is carved by > 5 cm (the planet's own cliffs are planet.spec's); the pad-bank spec ignores a road's own banks (≤ 13 m from a road).

Town street paths are built on first use (EdgeSpec.raw is a getter for them: the carve never reads them).
- [v2-S1f] Fix round for S1 (critic r3: 7.8). **The followed thing is re-drawn on top of the overlay; no window, no porthole.** Round 3's window (the overlay at a per-pixel depth just behind the subject in a disc round it) let everything nearer than a far subject show through as a round hole of landscape (rooftops → plane:3: grass, a tree, the sea in an 80 px disc, the plane itself behind the hill). A depth "seat" in that disc with the subject re-drawn into it was tried next: post reads depth for its night grade and ink, so the seat showed as a lighter, ungraded disc with an inked rim at night (`v2-S1f/r1/forced_sky`): a porthole again. Now the overlay draws over the scene whatever its depth (depth test 'always', back to front: body, far puffs, near puffs, inner rows first, streaks) and writes ONE depth just short of the far plane (NDC 1 − 5e-7) where ≥ 50 % opaque, so post sees a flat far surface (no ink inside it, no night grade by a fake height; the hidden city never inked over it). Then the subject's own scene objects are drawn again with their own materials (`renderer.renderBufferDirect` from the ring mesh's `onAfterRender`, inside the scene pass, after the overlay), scissored to its projected bounding circle's square: depth-tested against that far plane, it lands on top pixel-exact and self-occluded, and post inks its outline against the far plane like against the sky. A subject behind a hill is drawn over the clouds that hide the hill (the user asked for it on top). Under reduced motion (no overlay depth) the re-draw repaints its visible pixels over the fade. The close path (the whole overlay just behind a near subject) is gone: one mechanism at every distance. Cost: one extra draw of the subject's mesh, scissored, during episodes only.
- [v2-S1f] Contract (additive): `Trackable.objects?: readonly Object3D[]` — the scene objects that draw it, which the clouds re-draw over the overlay. Without it the clouds find them once per subject: the opaque mesh or instanced mesh (≤ 3000 instances) with an instance or centre within 1.8 m + 0.8 × radius of the pose, plus any with one at the same point (a body in parts); found again at most once a second while nothing is. That finds planes, balloons, cars, buses, boats, people and the bird (CPU-placed instances / the bird's matrix); space declares `objects: [mesh]` (its bodies are placed in the shader). REQUEST (L1, V1, A2, D1): declare `objects` on your trackables (and the bird via its camera subject, if it ever moves into a shader) so the scan never guesses.
- [v2-S1f] With the re-draw, a crossing never waits for the subject to come into frame (it lands on top whenever it does); only a subject that cannot be re-drawn is waited for (CROSS_WAIT). The world changed under phase 1: rooftops → plane:3 now climbs through the band looking at the city, and the old wait skipped the episode entirely.
- [v2-S1f] A far subject (bounding radius < 14 px, fading in from 9 px), or one with no objects found, gets a POP target ring while covered: an amber `#FFB84D` band between two ink lines, radius max(11 px, 1.35 r + 5), only where the body covers (never over the scene in the hole), fading with the episode.
- [v2-S1f] Reduced motion never cuts: the look-ahead into a puff now fires under reduced motion too (it only decides when the fade starts), and the fade's level is rate-limited both ways (`stepRmLevel`: 0 → 1 in ≥ 0.24 s, 1 → 0 in ≥ 0.3 s, ≤ 0.14 a frame at 30 fps). Spec'd (`crossing.spec.ts`: contact stepping 0 → 1 in one frame at 30/60/144 fps, with and without the look-ahead: no frame changes the level by more than 0.2 at 30 fps, and with the look-ahead it is ≥ 0.5 as the eye goes in). Live dive under reduced motion: sky.cross rises 0.07, 0.21, 0.35 … 0.85 over 7 frames (max +0.139), was 0 → 0.85 in one.
- [v2-S1f] The reveal pops crisp: while the hole opens the mist is capped at max(contact, 1 − 3.5 × (opening progress + 0.15)), so it is mostly gone by the opening's first frame and fully a fifth of the way in (the city sat under a lavender fog for 4–6 frames at the dive's money shot).
- [v2-S1f] The hold streams: each puff is drawn in its own frame (x out from the focus) as a cartoon cumulus (the base dome with its belly flattened away from the sky, three bumps overlapping its rim on the sky side: a scalloped top, placed, sized and leaning per puff), squashed per puff and stretched along the motion (+10 % at rest, up to +40 % at speed toward the edges); sizes capped per puff (near 0.13–0.23, far 0.085–0.145 of the height: one cap made every edge puff identical); a deeper shade band (light 0.42 out of the screen, the lit band from λ > −0.12). Speed streaks: 44 thin tapered two-tone strokes (24 on low) stream outward in ln r over the puffs with the motion, inside the cloud only; the streaming floor during an episode is 14 m/s (8 read as a still wall). Reduced motion: none of it, the still body only.
- [v2-S1f] Station beacons read from the street at night: never under 9 px (a 3–4 px saturated core and a halo of their own, not left to the bloom, the core not so hot that the tone map bleaches it white), pulled 1 m toward the eye so the hull never buries them, and sharp blinks inside a second: red / green nav lights on 55 % of a 1 s beat half a beat apart (they alternate), a 0.14 s strobe every 0.9 s, a double strobe every 1.1 s.
- [v2-S1f] From orbit, small bodies are boosted up to 5× to a bounding radius of 28 px (the station 34): a satellite's body is a third of its bounding radius, and at 21 px / 3× it was a speck. From space a satellite's beacons fade out under 30–48 px (coloured specks round a small body read as a smear).
- [v2-S1f] Still REQUESTS for post (not S1's files), both still visible: (1) `tilt *= 1 - (sky.cross ?? 0)` (the overlay's top and bottom bands and the re-drawn subject are blurred by tilt-shift at cloud altitude); (2) `n *= 1.0 - smoothstep(60.0, 100.0, length(wp) - R)` in the bloom prefilter (a small satellite's gold foil still blooms into a halo over the night side from orbit).
- [v2-D1f] (Supersedes refine 1's entry yaw and refine 2's DUR_MAX.) A ride arrives settled: the trip ends on the ride's own rest framing (behind a car or a plane, ahead in someone's eyes) and once `mode().blend` reports 1 nothing swings on (`blend` is exactly 1 only when settled, ≤ 0.9999 before). Round 1–2 arrived framed from the side the camera came from and swung 60–95° round after arriving (the felt trip was 4–6 s). Beside the station or a satellite every side is a good view, so the alongside framing is taken from the side the camera comes from and kept (drag orbits).
- [v2-D1f] Coming from the far side of what it rides (> 75° round from where it comes in), the approach swings round onto the camera's side (`EnterPath.plan` pivot, to 55° off) and the last stretch spirals back round behind it at the ride's own distance — leaving the swung axis and reaching the real one along their axes (smoothstep on the swing) — instead of a track over the roof or a whip round the subject (street → station spun at 640°/s over the top of it). A spiral that would pass through a facade (a car in a street canyon: 7 m to its side is in the buildings) swings two-thirds, a third, then rises over what it meets and drops in behind down the clear line (`spiralCost`, the height coming down as t^2.6); none clear, no pivot. The spiral is lifted as a last resort (slope-limited, 0 at the end).
- [v2-D1f] Live, a ride's approach is carried in the subject's own frame: moved with it and turned with its heading (`updateLive`: the change of the ride camera's heading since the plan, unwrapped frame to frame), what is left taken up over the last quarter. A car's U-turn in a turnaround swings the approach round it; it used to pass over the roof at 1 m.
