- [v2-D1f] Snappy trips: ordinary ones (across town, onto a car, into someone's eyes) ≤ 2 s, through the cloud band / up to space / round the planet ≤ 2.5 s (`DUR_ORD`, `DUR_MAX`; +0.25 s through the band, the user's ~0.25 s). A plan whose view would turn faster than 240°/s to fit gets what it needs up to 2.5 s (the drawn follower keeps up: no catch-up after arriving). Planned turns ≤ 150°/s (was 120), the follower 190°/s / 1500°/s² (was 170 / 1400), the speed scale 2.8 heights/s (was 2.4). The clock slows only where the live view turns faster than the plan does there (it slowed big-turn trips past 2.5 s). The gaze may start turning during the crane up out of a street once its way to the subject is clear (`firstSight`), not only after it. Measured: street → plane 2.5 s, street → station 2.5, orbit → plane 2.5, orbit → station 2.0, car → next car 1.85, street → car next door 2.0; a big turn from a canyon 2.5.
- [v2-D1f] No dead air: up to something behind the planet the path goes up and over above the clouds (round 2's low route under the band skimmed a dark hillside for 1.2 s); an aim behind the planet looks LIMB_DROP (8.8°) under the horizon so the limb — where it will rise — sits on the upper third, and a look up at something in the sky stays within 5° of the horizon (was 16°) unless the subject itself is higher: the planet fills at least the lower third. "Behind the planet" now means the line passes > 1 m under sea level and the look point is > 30 m off (a boat's look point grazing the sea counted, and the end orientation came out 8° off).
- [v2-D1f] Planning is spread over the two frames after the click (the path, then its gaze and timing; the camera coasts meanwhile, the clock starting on the third): warm ≤ 1.6 ms a slice, the first cold one ~5 ms (it was 15–20 ms on the click's frame). Long paths subdivide to ≤ (length)/160 and ≤ 2400 points after rounding; samples planned above `CLEAR_CEIL` (46 m) skip the clearance query.
- [v2-D1f] Falling through the clouds on the way in, the lens narrows onto the ridden thing (≥ 0.4× the FOV, never so far it leaves the frame) so its body reads at ~12 % of the frame over S1's overlay, through a spring, back out as the hole opens (S1 meanwhile re-draws the subject over the overlay: `Trackable.objects`).
- [v2-D1f] Reduced motion: a short direct move (0.55–0.95 s by distance; `directPose`): round the planet centre with the height interpolated evenly, lifted over what lies between, the orientation slerped the short way in the middle half; a long move or a turn over 60° dips the canvas to its wrapper's space colour (`canvas.style.opacity` down to 0.06, the only style D1 writes besides the cursor) through the middle, so the turn happens while dark. No follower lag (nothing turns after arriving). Replaces round 2's 3 s swoop slowed ×1.4. Shot tool: `--reduced`.
- [v2-D1f] Picking (core/track.ts, now D1's): an 'eyes' trackable is hit on its whole body, a capsule from 0.2 r over the eye down to the feet (radius 0.42 r), people's minimum disc 18 px; a hit on a body beats one on a disc only widened to the minimum; then the nearest by its front (along − min(r, 1.2)), so a walker in front of a truck is picked, not the truck. The director picks with a 22 px minimum above 60 m (14 px at street level), hover is sticky while the cursor stays within 2× the hovered thing's disc, and a click near what the hover tip shows rides that (a plane seen from orbit drifts out of a small disc in a third of a second; a walker steps aside and the truck behind was ridden). Measured from the street shot: every body point (eye, chest, hip, knee, feet) of the 10 nearest walkers picks that walker unless a nearer walker covers it.
- [v2-D1f] Bird: the occlusion spring of the bird camera (and of the ride rigs) lives in [min, 1.5 × boom], clamped every frame with its velocity zeroed where the clamp bites; seeded with 1e9 it wound up to ±1e6 m at the first lamp post after a launch and pinned the boom at 1.1 m for ~7 s. Launch: the heading is the one of 19 within ±90° of the lens with the most free metres up a 15° climb before a facade (a berth growing to 3.2 m), lamp heads and crowns costing only the metres they block, weighted toward the lens; the chase camera starts along the lens and swings round after it; the wall berth is primed on the first frame; near the ground a take-off: climbing out for 1.1 s whatever is held (down included, easing off), the swerve held off 0.6 s longer (it turned the bird straight off its launch heading into a facade). Dive: a stoop — wings swept back half-open and raised (they folded flat into a saucer), the camera 7° higher with the tuck; crowns + 1.5 m in the soft floor. Shot `takeoff`.
- [v2-D1f] Specs: `core/track.spec.ts` (head to feet picks the walker in front of a truck; a truck in front hides a walker; a crowd picks the nearest), `rides/director.spec.ts` (+ rides arrive settled: ≤ 2.5 s planned and < 15°/s over the next second beyond the subject's own turn, for orbit → plane, high and rooftop → an oncoming car on a real downtown lane, street → walker; reduced motion ≤ 1 s with no turn beyond the direct one; a street-shot bird launch with A + S held: the occlusion spring finite and clamped, the boom never short for 1.5 s; a launch with no input keeps > 1.2 m off facades and climbs). Turn limits in the existing specs follow the new caps (195°/s, 3.2° a frame).
- [v2-D1f, r2] Picking sees walls (`core/track.ts`): once the ray has hit a candidate, it is marched (0.5 m steps where a roof can stand, skipping down from above everything) to the first building the city plan puts in its way, or the terrain outside the plateau; whatever is ridden must start in front of that (+0.3 m). The plateau's own ground is not an occluder (the lower half of a far walker's minimum disc is ground). A camera grazing a wall ignores the building it starts in. Critic r1: a blank facade rode a walker 97 m behind it and showed the pointer. Found on the way: the planet test rejected every plane and balloon looking up from the street (both meet points of an upward ray lie behind the camera, and the negative distance was taken as the planet in front).
- [v2-D1f, r2] Near trips (`camera/rides/near.ts`, spec'd): a subject within 34 m of a camera less than 30 m up, in clear sight (no building or hill on the line, and at most one of four rays 10° and 20° either side of it meets a wall within 4 m — a sliver past a corner is not clear sight), takes no planned path. The camera turns on the spot until the subject is in frame (≤ 175°/s, less what the subject's own motion adds; the open way round on a turn of more than 100°), then glides round it in the subject's moving frame — distance, height and bearing from it eased to the ride's rest framing (into someone's eyes: round to 1.6 m behind and 0.75 m over the shoulder, then forward over the head and down into it) — looking at it all the way, settling onto the ride's own look over the last 0.5 s. The swing takes either way round (the long one up to 230°), lifted (0.7–8 m) or widened (2.5–3 m) over what is in the way, 1.1 m off facades (0.45 m along a sidewalk) and 3.5 m over roofs. ≤ 2.2 s, 2.4 s at most; sampled as it will play and stretched where it would turn faster than 188°/s. Critic r1: a walker 2 m behind took 2.58 s, a 14 m crane and a 121°/s spin with the walker never on screen; now 1.8–2.1 s, on screen from ~0.8 s.
- [v2-D1f, r2] Disagreed with the critic's "in frame ≥ 70 % of the trip whenever it is not occluded at the start" for subjects behind the camera: at the follower's 190°/s a 140° turn needs ~0.75 s before the subject can be in frame, so 70 % would make the trip ≥ 2.5 s. The spec instead holds: once in frame it stays in frame to the drop into the eyes (≥ 97 %), and a subject starting within the view's half-width keeps ≥ 85 %.
- [v2-D1f, r2] Facades and roofs out of the shot (`rides/path.ts`): mid-way (the full 4 m clearance radius) a roof is crossed 6 m up, not 3.5; short hops (< 70 m) also try swings of a block's size (±9, ±16 m) round what they would climb over; and from a third of the way on the path keeps a line of sight to the subject over whatever stands within 14 m toward it (2 m over it, never into the clouds; `PathEnv.top`). Street → a car round the corner goes round the building down the street instead of over its roof (which filled half the frame); it still passes the building, at a block's distance.
- [v2-D1f, r2] A long trip low down (descending or climbing, > 60 m) rises in one arc over the town, under the band (`longArc`): low over 180 m the speed limit alone (a couple of heights a second) took the whole 2.5 s.
- [v2-D1f, r2] Durations: the plan lands by DUR_MAX counting the two planning frames (≤ 2.45 s planned). Deviation from the critic's "hard cap including the live dilation": the clock still slows (up to ~1.4×) for a target that leaves its predicted course — a car U-turning in the turnaround the camera is flying to — because squeezing that swing into the cap made a 1000°/s whip the drawn camera was still catching up with (88°/s) after arriving. Measured: rooftops → car:3 (that U-turn) 2.72 s, settled; every other trip probed ≤ 2.45 s, near ones 1.4–2.2 s.
- [v2-D1f, r2] A ride's heading spring is held tight (ω 9) while the transition flies in and eased back to its own (ω 3.2 chase, 4.5 eyes) over 1.2 s after landing (`RideRig.stiff`): a car that turned on the way left it a quarter turn behind and it swung on at 19–22°/s after arrival (critic r1); now 8.6°/s, the car's own turn included.
- [v2-D1f, r2] The eyes' near plane steps out to 0.75 m over the half second after arriving (the ground is ≥ 1.4 m from the lens however far down one looks): a crown, a lamp post or a bench at the lens is clipped instead of filling the view. A lamp post at 1 m is still seen (it passes as the walker walks). No arrival gaze bias toward the longest sight line: it would be a swing after arrival, which the orchestrator ruled out.
- [v2-D1f, r2] The cloud crossing's lens is led: narrowing from ~0.35 s before the planned path enters the band (the spring ω 7), at its narrowest (~23° from 43°) as the overlay closes and held through it (critic r1: 37° only as the hole opened). Up to the station or a satellite the lens also closes in through the middle of the trip while the subject is a speck in frame.
- [v2-D1f, r2] Hover from high up: air and space targets stay hovered within 4× their pick disc (2× elsewhere), and a click that hits nothing rides what was hovered in the last 1.5 s if it is within 5× its disc of the click (a plane seen from orbit drifts 77 px in 1.2 s under a still cursor; the click fell through to the explore dive).
- [v2-D1f, r2] The planner is warmed at idle once the world runs (a dummy trip across town and one up into the space layer, planned and dropped in a `requestIdleCallback`, ~7–10 ms there): the first real ride plans in ~2.5 ms with a ~6 ms frame (critic r1: 6.4 ms + a 12 ms frame).
- [v2-D1f, r2] Reduced motion through the cloud band: one fade through the dark, 1.2 s — the canvas dips over the first quarter, the move is made over 0.26–0.45 and the turn over 0.3–0.75 while it is dark, and it fades back in from 0.8, after S1's still cloud fade (~0.55 s from the crossing) is over. Critic r1: dark → white → the ride. Elsewhere unchanged (0.55–0.95 s).
- [v2-D1f, r2] Bird: walkers near a low bird join its soft floor (eyes + 0.75 m, within 1.1 m; gathered ≤ 10 Hz while under 7 m), so a dive or a take-off among a crowd goes over the heads; the wall berth ahead is 3.4 m (was 2.8) and looks 1.3 s ahead. A turn and down held at full stick still circles among the blocks (a 15 m circle downtown cannot keep clear of every facade); it no longer scrapes (0 wall contacts in 5 s from the street).
- [v2-D1f, r2] Behind the planet, the gaze aims under the horizon along the planned track a little ahead, not the straight line's heading (degenerate for a target near the far pole of the planet).
- [v2-D1f, r3] Found re-shooting (critic r2 trace, street → boat:3 coming at the camera): the swing round behind spun a full barrel roll, the drawn camera (190°/s) fell 90° behind, tipped to −70° into the sea and lost the boat for 0.8 s, then swung on at 147°/s after blend = 1. Three causes, three fixes. (1) **The roll**: an end orientation was carried onto the trip's gaze by the least rotation, which is singular where the gaze faces away from the end's axis (looking back at the bow before swinging behind it). Now the least rotation only while the gaze is within ~80° of that end's axis; turned further (by 120°), the end's own roll against its level frame (`endRoll`; the level frame alone is undefined for the orbit view looking straight down, where the least rotation stays). Spec: `rides/director.spec.ts` boat at sea sailing at the camera — roll ≤ 12° off level wherever level is defined, the boat in frame over the last 40 % of the trip, settled < 15°/s after (the old roll fails it at 22°). (2) **The time map, short of time, gives up the speed limit before the turn limit** (`TimeMap.build`; was the other way round): the turn limit is what keeps the subject in frame; low over open sea the camera may stream past faster than 2.8 heights/s for a moment. (3) **A pivot out in the open swings from the full approach distance** (≤ 45 m; the fullest clear fraction first, from the full distance or else the axis's clear length): an island behind the boat cut the approach axis to 1.5 m and the swing round was made at 8 m in the last tenth of the trip. Not in the capital, where the wide swing ends across a block and the track went over its roof (street → car:5). Measured: street → boat:3 2.47 s, the boat in frame from 0.25 s, 9–11°/s after arrival (the boat's own turn).
- [v2-D1f, r3] Behind the planet → in sight is a blend, not a switch (`underDepth`: by how far the line passes under the sea, 1–4 m, and the look point's distance, 30–45 m): a boat 130 m off rising over the horizon as the camera came down turned the gaze 5° in a frame.
- [v2-D1f, r3] **Once what is ridden is in frame it stays in frame** (`keepClamp`): the transition's gaze is turned toward the subject just enough to hold it within 20° of the axis — from where it is first within 34° (perhaps at the frame's edge, drawn in over 0.15 of the trip), not through the planet, not at the lens (into someone's eyes it fades out from 12 to 5 m). Applied before the pitch limits and again after them, never tipping the gaze under the mid-way pitch floor (passing over a walker it does not look straight down into a spin). Planned in `sampleTurns` with the subject where it is predicted to be, so the time map gives it its time. Measured, share of the trip with the subject in frame: orbit → plane:0 0.69 → 0.88, street → plane:1 0.89 → 1.0, street → car:5 0.37 → 0.46, rooftops → person:40 0.35 → 0.53, street → person:228 0.5 → 0.72 (2.0 → 2.4 s: the extra turn takes its time). Let go over the last tenth of the trip, so it lands exactly on the ride's own framing. Spec: `rides/director.spec.ts` orbit → plane from two ways in where the plane was out of frame for 15–17 % of the trip without the hold: < 8 %. Tried and dropped: holding a speck within 7° so the lens could close in on it — up to a satellite on the night side it lifted the gaze off the planet into black sky.
- [v2-D1f, r3] **Walkers in the path's clearance**: an enter that starts or ends low gathers the walkers (not the ridden one, ≤ 40) within 30 m of either end, and the path keeps 1.3 m over their heads within 2.4 m of where they will be over the next 1.2 s (`gatherPathCrowd`, in `clearEnv`). From the street a walker coming the other way filled the lower third of the frame with his face at 0.2 s (street → car:5); now the camera rises over the crowd first.
- [v2-D1f, r3] **Bird take-off guide**: for 3.2 s after a launch near the ground, left alone (no steer, no dive), the bird steers for the clearest heading (`launchHeading` from where it is, re-picked 4× a second) and keeps climbing (down held is respected; a steer hands over at once). Flying straight on it met the tower across the plaza at 1.2 s and climbed its face for 1.7 s with the facade filling the frame; now it banks away and circles the plaza with the clock tower in view. Down held now descends into the street (it was lifted along the facade by the soft floor).
- [v2-D1f, r4] **The bird left alone keeps off the buildings, at any time** (critic r1 after r3: once the 3.2 s take-off guide ended it flew straight at the clock tower's face, 2 m off, and skimmed its spire). With no steer and no dive it looks 34 m down its heading 4× a second (`idleLook`, ≤ 0.2 ms) and, when less than 26 m of it is clear, turns for the clearest heading (`clearHeading`, the take-off search generalised), until 32 m are clear; in town it climbs until it is 6 m over the roofs within 24 m (`guideSky`). Down or up held is respected; a steer or a dive hands over at once. Its walls use the roofs' real tops (`roofTop`: a spire 3 m over the walls, gables and domes 2.5 m) and its look-ahead keeps 2.4 m more off a slender tall building (`towerBerth`: the clock tower, the church, a thin tower > 18 m). Measured from the street with no input for 10 s: never closer than 3.2 m to a facade, no wall contact (it was 0.55 m with the r3 behaviour; spec'd).
- [v2-D1f, r4] Bird flight (`bird/flight.ts`): the soft floor is sampled at 8 look-ahead times, not 4 (a dive at 16 m/s skimmed through a crown between two samples); what lies on the arc it is flying within 0.6 s is climbed over even while it swerves for the clear side (it went through the top of a crown it could not turn round); a wall ahead picks the swerve's side (the two fought); and against a wall it can hardly turn from it flares — up to 14 m/s² of braking and 1.5× the turn rate — and banks fully even in a dive (it turned at 22°/s and climbed the facade). Cars, buses and trucks near a low bird join its soft floor with the walkers (over the roof, within half their length). `bird/cam.spec.ts`'s tower test guard is now ≥ 300 (three of six starts pass within 2.1–2.3 m of the tower and the floor never has to step in at all; it asked for > 300 busy frames).
- [v2-D1f, r4] **Into someone's eyes** (critic r1: the headline move was the weakest — over the walker's head, a 120° spin looking down at the paving, the walker out of frame for 0.65 s). (1) The trip looks at the walker, not at where they look, until the drop (4.5 → 1.5 m): toward a walker facing the camera their look point is back past the camera and the gaze faced away for the whole trip (spec'd). (2) The approach comes down over the shoulder at 32° from ≤ 14 m behind (`EYES_SLOPE`, `EYES_APPROACH`), its clearance checked along that slope, not the level line behind them (benches and lamp posts cut it to 1.5 m). (3) The swing round behind is tried in town too, at the full, a middle and the clear radius, and its last 2.5 m (the drop into the head, past whatever stands over it) is not counted against it (`spiralCost(…, tail)`). (4) The hold keeps them in frame to 4.5 m and lets go by 1.5 m (it let go from 12 m). (5) Over their shoulder, just before the drop, the clock runs at a fifth (≤ 0.45 s) while a car, a bus or another walker is about to cross 0.3–3.5 m in front of their face (`faceBlocked`, 10 Hz, straight-line 1.5 s): the taxi passing a walker waiting at a kerb is watched over their shoulder instead of filling 60 % of the first view.
- [v2-D1f, r4] **Held in frame, by the lens's own frame** (`keepClamp`): the subject's angles across and up the view against the half fields of view (wider across), not a 20° cone; "seen" from 10° / 12° outside the frame's edge (drawn in as the gaze turns); live, the hold starts wherever the subject actually comes by, before the plan's linear guess (a plane on a curve slid in at the top and out again); the pitch floor and the limb's ceiling give way as far as keeping the subject inside the frame's edge asks; behind the planet → in sight eases in from while the line still passes 6 m under the sea (the gaze waits on the limb where it rises). A chase camera passing over what it rides keeps a stand-off (`standOff`: ~0.55 × its height over it, eased sideways and back toward the tail, planned and live): street → car:5 passed 0.6 m over the roof with the car a sliver at the bottom for 0.5 s. Walkers in the path's clearance: 2 m over their heads within 3 m (was 1.3 / 2.4).
- [v2-D1f, r4] Deviation from the critic's lostAfter: it counts frames out of the frustum after the first frame in it, including frames where the planet hides the subject — rooftops → car:3's 25 "lost" frames are all the car below the horizon (the line passes under the sea; it was "in frame" behind the planet at the start). Counted only where the planet does not hide it, every probed trip is 0 except street → person:228 (14: the last 0.25 s drop into a seated walker's head).
- [v2-D1f, r4] Rolls: under orbit heights the transition's horizon is never rolled more than ~20° off level beyond the end's own roll (tanh), ~45° in orbit, free only within 8° of straight down (city → bus:0 rolled 44–57° leaving the city view as the gaze came up off the vertical; now 20°). The drawn follower turns up to 210°/s and 1700°/s² (was 190 / 1500) and a plan may turn up to 270°/s before it is given more time (was 240): street → car:5 lands in 2.1 s (was 2.36); the specs' per-frame cap follows (`TURN_FRAME`, the turn-rate bound).
- [v2-D1f, r4] Reduced motion: the direct move's turn is slerped the short way as it was at its start (three's slerp re-picked the hemisphere each frame and city → car:3 flipped 62° in a frame); the dip goes down to 30 % for a turn under 60°, deeper for bigger turns (8 % past 150°), instead of 6 % always.
- [v2-D1f, r4] Not done, and why: the far-side satellite trip keeps ~0.5 s of starfield with the satellite a speck (centring it for the lens to close in pulled the planet out of the frame — tried and reverted); a mid-trip lens narrowing onto a far boat had no visible effect (reverted); the peach patch on the sea in street → boat:3 is the ocean's (a shoal), not the camera's.
- [v2-S1f] Round 2 (critic r1: 8.4). **The followed thing's objects are found by frames, not wall time** (`clouds/subject.ts`, spec'd): the scan used `performance.now()` (once a second after a miss) and first ran while the bird was still popping in (hidden, scale ≈ 0), so in shot mode, where sim time outruns the wall clock, the bird stayed unfound through a whole crossing and vanished under the overlay with a 260 px ring in its place. Now: declared objects win (`Trackable.objects`, or the camera's new `subjectObjects()`); otherwise a miss is retried every frame while anything is urgent (an episode runs, the white-out fog is up, the eye looks ahead into a puff, or the eye or the subject is within 2 × CROSS_CLEAR of the band) and every 6 frames otherwise; a found object that is hidden or leaves the scene is looked for again at once; a mesh at a scale of ~0 is not taken. Live: the bird climbing from rooftops is found by frame 9 of bird mode and stays on top through the crossing (`v2-S1f/r6/sheet_bird.jpg`).
- [v2-S1f] Contract (additive): `CameraService.subjectObjects?(): readonly Object3D[]` — the scene objects that draw the camera's subject when it is not a Trackable that declares its own (the bird's mesh). REQUEST (D1): implement it in the director (return `[birdMesh]` in bird mode, `[]` otherwise; the bird system can hand its mesh over through `birdRender()`), so the bird is never searched for. Until then the frame-counted scan finds it.
- [v2-S1f] Contract (additive): `Trackable.bodyRadius?: number` — what reads as its body on screen when much smaller than the bounding radius. The far-subject ring now goes by it (a satellite's box is ~0.38 of its reach: at a 14 px reach its body was a 5 px speck with no ring); space sets it per model (station 0.55, sail 0.8, cubesat trio 0.55, others 0.38–0.42 × radius). The ring is capped at 40 px (MARK_MAX): one drawn round a subject that could not be re-drawn was sized by its bounding radius with no cap (260 px).
- [v2-S1f] **The lateral puff set** (shaders.ts crossPuffVert): with the focus of expansion off-screen (a ride climbing out of the city while looking down and ahead, the bird climbing), every on-screen log-polar cell is huge next to the capped puff sizes, and the hold showed 3–4 puffs on a bare lavender sheet (~85 % flat body). A second instance set, a staggered grid of puff-sized cells (far 0.26, near 0.36 of the height; 45 % of far and 16 % of near cells empty, so the body and its streaks show in gaps: at 0.19 / 0.3 and full it read as foam and cost +1–2 ms) in a frame along the on-screen flow (from the focus toward the frame's centre), rows scrolling at the radial set's speed at the centre, takes over as the focus leaves the frame (0.02 → 0.2 of the height outside it, eased 0.08 s, set at once when the overlay comes up). The two sets hand over puff by puff (each shrinks or grows at its own hashed threshold), so the count on screen stays even. Instances: far 121 + 100, near 42 + 64, + 3 caps (low tier: the near ones only); only one set draws at a time, the other's sprites collapse in the vertex shader. Debug `clouds.forceLat` (with `clouds.force`) holds the focus off-screen for paired perf A/B of this set.
- [v2-S1f] **The closing caps**: flying backward the cloud closes in from the corners and its last gap (at the screen point nearest the focus) shrank as a small scalloped porthole onto the city for a frame. Three near puffs at that point grow as the fill passes ~72 % and leave with the puffs round them in the opening (backward only; left to go last, they faded alone at the focus as a ghost cluster).
- [v2-S1f] **The puff shape**: a cartoon cumulus laid out in the sky's frame (leaning ±0.3 rad per puff): a wide base with a flat belly (half-height 0.62 up, 0.5 down), three round bumps on its top (shoulders and a bigger crown) and a small lobe low on one side; drawn 1.4 × the old dome radius. The round base dome made egg shapes once stretched along the motion; the stretch is gentler too (0.05 + 0.2 × speed, was 0.1 + 0.3; per-puff squash 0.9–1.1, was 0.84–1.16). Ink round the silhouette only: the crease ink between lobes is gone (inked, they read as bubbles stacked on bubbles), the crease is shade alone.
- [v2-S1f] **Speed streaks under the puffs, inside the body only**: drawn after the body and before the puffs (render order body, streaks, puffs, ring), each fragment kept only where the body covers (a little inside its lumpy edge, `lbBodyEdge`), so a streak never crosses open sky or the city in the hole and never scratches a puff's face; length capped at 0.32–0.52 of the height (an off-screen focus made frame-long hairlines). The body's own per-pixel streak lines are gone (a full-screen cost, and a second streak system).
- [v2-S1f] Reduced motion: a teleport ramps the still fade out like any other frame (`crossing.ts rmFadeStep`, spec'd) instead of zeroing it (0.49 → 0 in one frame on a rooftops → plane:3 ride); only a frozen frame (dt = 0: the shot tool's setup) takes the target at once; and the overlay stays up until the fade is out after the episode has ended (it was hidden at the episode's end with up to ~0.1 left). Live: 0.49, 0.38, 0.27, 0.16, 0.05, 0.
- [v2-S1f] Backward openings fade from 0.55 of the opening and are done by 0.95 (forward still 0.72 → 1): the last cloud shrinking away into an off-screen focus read as a solid lump sliding out of the frame for ~5 frames, and into an on-screen one ended as a small translucent scalloped disc; fading as it goes it dissolves before it is small (still ≥ 8 frames from covered to clear: the spec; 0.5 or 0.92 fail it).
- [v2-S1f] The overlay writes its one far depth only while it is mostly opaque (episode fade > 0.55; never under reduced motion): fading out at the end of an episode, the scene under the translucent remnant keeps its own depth, so post's ink and night grade stay on it (written, the city under a 30 % remnant lost its outlines). The re-drawn subject is then depth-tested against the scene, as it is anyway outside an episode.
- [v2-S1f] Overlay cost: the body (a full-screen pass) skips the scalloped edge's angle and hash wherever both fronts are > 0.12 away (most of the frame while it is covered; its edge derivative width is clamped at 0.02 so the step there never draws an ink ring), and its per-pixel speed lines are gone. A puff under ~5 px on screen fades in (alpha) and its ink under ~12 px (a puff growing in or shrinking away was a 1–2 frame ink dot).
- [v2-S1f] Disagreed with the critic (r1), and why: (1) "dissolve the overlay inside a big unfound subject's projected circle" — that is the porthole the user's note rules out; with the frame-counted scan an unfound subject is one that is not drawn at all (hidden LOD), so it gets the (capped) ring only. (2) "draw the body after the puffs with depth LESS to skip covered pixels" — the puffs are blended at their antialiased edges and the overlay writes one far depth for post; a body drawn after them would draw over their fringes (a halo) or need a second depth-only pass. Overlay cost at full cover is now ~+0.3–0.4 ms with the radial set and ~+0.5–1.0 ms with the lateral one (PERF.md, shared GPU), transient (~0.6 s per crossing).
- [v2-S1f] Round 3. **A teleport is a frozen frame, not a distance** (`crossing.ts isTeleport`, spec'd): the episode ended whenever the eye moved more than 25 m in a frame, and the rides' transitions now climb through the layer at 15–25 m a frame at 30 fps (rooftops → plane:0: 56.6 → 71.5 m in one frame), so the overlay popped off a third grown. Now only a camera placed on a dt = 0 frame (setView, a shot, an instant ride) or a move over 400 m in a frame ends one; any motion plays the crossing out.
- [v2-S1f] Round 3: the focus of expansion holds still while the hole opens. A ride settling onto its target turns the climb into a chase mid-opening, and the focus (eased 0.06 s) swung from the middle to a corner in one frame, sliding the last cloud across the scene with it.
- [v2-S1f] Round 3: the last cloud shrinks away solid instead of thinning. Fading puffs by alpha showed every overlap and ink line through each other (a heap of glass bubbles over the island for 2–3 frames). `Crossing.alpha` (the overlay's opacity) stays 1 until the fade is ~40 % done; the rest of the fade is the hole running ahead (`RETREAT` 0.22 of the front range), so the remnant's puffs and body shrink to nothing. To keep the reveal ≥ 8 frames at 30 fps with no frame removing more than a fifth of the cloud (the spec, now on `alpha`), the opening is 0.42 s (was 0.34): ~0.65 s from the first puff to clear, ~0.3 s at least half covered (spec'd 0.25–0.4 at 30/60/144 fps).
- [v2-S1f] Round 3, the look (critic: "a wall of identical circles", "wisps streaming past"): three kinds of overlay cumulus by a per-puff hash: the heap (~55 %), a long low bank (base 45 % wider, a fifth lower, two small crowns between low shoulders, ~30 %) and a tower in tiers (~15 %; a big crown straight on the base read as an egg). The smear along the motion is a quarter as strong along the puff's own sky axis (above and below the focus it turned a cumulus into a tall egg). The body's scalloped fronts: the lumps' angle warped (0.65–2.2× the mean width) plus a second octave of little lumps where the big ones are wide on screen (with the focus far off the frame a big lump is a flat arc, and arcs meeting in points read as a mountain range; round a small hole the little lumps read as a serrated tear, so they fade in with the lump width).
- [v2-S1f] Round 3: the speed streaks are soft white wisps (3.4–8.4 px, feathered, a lavender underside) drawn between the far and the near puff layers (the puffs are now two meshes, far and near: +1 draw call during an episode), still only where the body covers. Under the puffs they showed only in the gaps and read as faint grey grooves; over the far layer they read as mist rushing past between the cloud layers, and the near puffs keep them off the big faces.
- [v2-S1f] Round 3: `clouds/subject.ts` checks a fresh find again 2 frames later (`CONFIRM_AFTER`, spec'd): a fleet packs its near and far LOD meshes on its own update, so on the first frame of a ride a car is often only in the far pack, whose fragments are dithered away up close, and re-drawing that alone drew a speck in place of the car (the `chase` shot with `clouds.force`). Still open (REQUEST L1, V1, A2): declare `Trackable.objects` (both LODs) so the scan never guesses.
- [v2-S1f] Round 3: **the re-draw draws the subject's instance alone.** Re-drawing an instanced owner mesh drew every instance inside the subject's scissor square (riding a car, the car behind it floated over the clouds too). Each instanced mesh gets a one-instance stand-in (an `InstancedMesh` sharing its geometry's non-instanced buffers and its material, so its program: no compile) into which the subject's instance is copied every frame, found by `SubjectObjects.locate` (nearest instance to the pose, spec'd; owners repack theirs every frame). The copy happens in `scene.onBeforeRender` (the clouds chain it in `init` and restore it in `dispose`; nobody else uses it), i.e. after every system's update (the owners update after the clouds) and before three's projection uploads buffers (`renderBufferDirect` uploads nothing); the stand-in sits in the scene at a count of 0 (projected and uploaded, draws nothing) and is drawn with a count of 1 from the ring's `onAfterRender`. Interleaved instance data falls back to the whole mesh, scissored. Live: `city` → ride car:3 dives through the layer with the car alone on top all the way (`v2-S1f/r7/car_sheet.jpg`).
- [v2-S1f] Round 3: station beacons are never under 12 px (a 4–5 px saturated core and their own halo; were 9). From the street at night the green and red truss lights and the strobes read against the sunlit hull at skywatch distance.
- [v2-S1f] Round 3, not S1's: the flat peach disc with jagged edges over the sea on rides near the terminator (critic r2 `crop_p0_pink`) is the ocean's sun glint (`ocean/index.ts` glintNear on facets), not a cloud (it stays peach with `clouds.mask=1`). REQUEST (ocean owner): soften `glintNear` at grazing dusk angles. Post's two requests above (tilt-shift by `sky.cross`, no night bloom above the space floor) still stand.
- [v2-L1f] What the eyes ride's lens shows is now measured, not walker body centres in a ±45° wedge: `people/ride.spec.ts` (48 rides of 25 s against the live traffic, half flown in, half settled, a quarter kids, each started on a crowd that has been walking 3 s, through `PeopleSim.startRide`) measures, from the first frame the camera is in the head, the distance from the eye to every walker, dog, person sitting or standing about, prop and vehicle inside the 60° × 16:10 frustum (props by height: `PeopleSim.obsH`), faces (within 1.8 m, ±35°, facing > 0.7, also in a ride's first 3 s) and faces held over 0.5 s, the longest run with a back within 2.2 m in the frustum, people coming at the eye turning back within 3 m of it, the ridden walker's own U-turns and standing. Round 3's sim on the same 192 rides → round 4: faces 0.90 % → 0.29 % of frames; faces held > 0.5 s 20 → 7; back runs: 95th percentile 4.97 → 2.67 s, longest 9.1 → 5.0 s, rides with one over 3 s 19 % → 4 %; turn-backs within 3 m of the eye 224 → 30 (in 57 % → 15 % of rides); a prop within 0.8 m in 14 % → 7 % of rides, within 0.6 m 5 % → 3 %; a person sitting or standing about within 0.9 m 3 % → 1 % (nearest 0.37 → 0.49 m); a walker within 0.8 m 8 % → 8 % (unchanged: brief passes at the frame's edge); the rider's own dog never in the frame. In shot mode `meshes[0].userData.peopleLens()` reports the same on the real camera (`people/lens-report.ts`, its own lazy chunk) for review scripts (`docs/littlebig/shots/v2-L1f/probe.mjs`).
- [v2-L1f] A ride starts through `PeopleSim.startRide(r, riderK, cut)` (people/index.ts on the step the ride changes). On its first rendered frame, once the camera has moved, the camera already in the head (a settled ride, ≤ 1.5 m) or still far off (> 40 m: from orbit the street is a few pixels) is a cut: `clearRide` steps everyone close in the view (a face coming at the eye within 9 m, a back within ~4.7 m, anyone within 2 m) along their own path out of it, and moves people waiting at the rider's own kerb in front of it to the far landing (already across). The rider itself is never moved by a cut: the camera is in its head and a move showed its body for a frame. Flying in from close by, for RIDER_FRESH 2.4 s people close in its view facing it turn away (on their travel direction, not their lagging facing, which made one flip back and forth), everyone keeps RIDER_SEP_FRESH 1.7 m, and the camera low over the street (< 4.5 m, not yet in the head) is an obstacle walkers step round, so no head brushes past the lens on the way down.
- [v2-L1f] "In the lens" is measured about where the ride camera looks (`riderVx/Vz`: the smoothed yaw people/index.ts publishes, written each step) and where the rider is heading 2 m on (round a corner), not its body's facing. The berth places others along the rider's (and each walker's) path, not on the straight tangent (`onPath`: arc length ahead and lateral offset from the path's centre line, its own edge then the next, a 10 m window): round a bend the tangent put an oncomer on the wrong side and both dodged into each other.
- [v2-L1f] The lateral plan weighs soft intervals instead of keeping all or none: each costs its weight per metre inside it (people 3, props 2, an overtake 1.2, a tall prop's near clearance 8, the kerb band 6), hard ones are walls; with no hard-free line at all the rider still picks its line by what is close (walls within 1.2 m, everything further at 20/m) and stops short of the far conflict. Round 3 dropped every soft interval as soon as one could not be kept, and held a line 0.4 m off a lamp post. Without a rider the plan is v1's exactly (no soft intervals).
- [v2-L1f] Props and people sitting or standing about: the rider keeps its eye RIDER_CLEAR_TALL 0.95 m (× riderK) laterally from the disc's edge of anything taller than the eye minus 0.3 m (lamp posts, trunks, shelters, flags, heads), RIDER_CLEAR_LOW 0.6 m from low ones (benches, planters, hydrants: under the frame within a metre of an adult eye; a kid's eye counts them tall), and 0.62 m from tall ones whenever there is any way at all (a heavy soft, not a wall: walls walled it in between two lamp posts and it stood turning round for 12 s). It looks 6.5 m round corners for them (others 4.5). Each walk edge's best clearance (`riderRoom`) is precomputed; the rider avoids edges it cannot keep it on (×0.08) and the featured walker counts them against. It keeps a hand's breadth in from the paving's edge (its steering target round a corner fell off the kerb and it stood pushing) and off the kerb-side 0.85 m of a pavement (a soft band: a turning bus swept 0.6 m from the eye of a rider kept right for someone coming).
- [v2-L1f] Someone coming at the eye: two walking at each other pass on a fixed side (the side they are on if more than 0.6 m apart, else both keep right; they compute it from the same offset, so they agree: planning round where the other was, both stepped the same way and met at 0.3 m). Detected within RIDER_TTC 2.4 s or 3.2 m, in front of the view or the heading, by the pass the lateral plan makes with the rider on its path, else the closest approach of the two velocities less 0.15 m (straight courses miss wider than ones bending round a corner); standing facing the rider within 2.2 m always counts. Such a walker meets the rider: where the paving leaves the rider a pass of RIDER_PASS 1.0 m at its edge (`passBy`, lamp posts included) and it need not cross the rider's line to get there, it steps aside to the kerb side (or away from the rider off a pavement, or off a zebra's far kerb) and stands turned side-on to the rider (never into the lens: round a corner its edge's normal pointed at the rider) until the rider is past (on the other side of it from where it was: "behind it along its way" ended the yield every step for one ahead going the rider's way) or 8 s; else it turns back, only ever from facing the rider and going its way along the path (turning back round a corner walked one straight back at the eye), turns briskly, hurries off ×1.2 for 5 s and re-plans its next join round the rider. If the rider stands held up within 3 m of one standing aside, it walks off the other way. The critic's polly (walks to 1.5 m, turns, leads in front) now turns back at ~4 m and walks off ahead at ~3.5 m (shots `r4-57.png`).
- [v2-L1f] Backs: the nearest back inside the rider's view cone (cos 0.62 about its look, any lateral offset, not only in line) is its lead, kept RIDER_TAIL 3.2 m (× riderK) eye to back, easing to a third of its pace rather than stopping, unless the rider is already RIDER_PASS + 0.1 to the side of it and quicker (then by at up to 1.6 m/s; never chasing someone hurrying off: round 4's first try went by the plan and sat a metre behind such a back for 5 s "overtaking"). People slower than it in its way, or standing with their back to it, keep to one side for a moment (`makeWay`); a lead followed for 1.2 s walks on a little faster; after 2 s (was 4) the rider goes another way than its lead at the next join; standing held up behind a back within 2.2 m it gives up in about a second (×4). Follow decisions read how fast someone actually gets on (`progV`), not how hard they push: one walking in place against a wall held it up as if following a walker.
- [v2-L1f] Route choice round the rider: 4.5 m before its join it looks down its next edge and goes another way from a narrow pavement with someone coming down it, or any with two (or a short zebra landing full of waiters); others near their join with the rider within 20 m do not walk head-on into its narrow pavement, nor down the way it is about to take just ahead of it (once per edge, `rpick`).
- [v2-L1f] Kerbs: the rider looks RIDER_KERB_LOOK 1.8 s (its camera's kerb glance starts over at each kerb, at 1.6× pace: left, right, go) and then steps onto a zebra no car is committed to (`blocked` clear), walk window or not — drivers stop for it; it still waits for its berth on the zebra (`zebraClear`). Standing 4 s regardless (a car holding the zebra, people ahead on it), it gives up onto the pavement it was not walking (a quarter turn of the view, not a half turn back). Waiters within 4 m of the rider (not on its zebra) stand right at the kerb (0.15 m: their knot at a corner blocked it, a back in the lens); waiters on its zebra step aside away from it as it comes across (from 7 m) and turn along the kerb within 3.5 m; the rider, not yet stepped off, steps aside on its own landing for anyone coming off its zebra (and back to its wait line while it does), instead of stepping back toward the corner they make for; two meeting on a landing may sidestep there (they froze face to face at a metre); the rider keeps its full berth from someone coming off a zebra at it (round 4 met a crosser at 0.7 m). The berth's brakes use the same gaps as its lateral plan (it braked at 0.9 m for a pass planned at 0.8 and crept along behind).
- [v2-L1f] The ridden walker's dog trots at heel just behind (round 3: ahead on the left, a red blob cut off by the bottom of the frame); it shows when the camera looks down or round. The critic suggested 1 m out and 45° off: from an adult's eye that is half under the frame's bottom, the blob again.
- [v2-L1f] The featured walker (rideable from orbit) also counts against a path that squeezes past a lamp post or a bench with someone on it (+8), a kid's low eye (+4) and walking on someone's heels (+6). From the orbit shot it is now gwen (person:101, an adult in the park) rather than round 3's kid behind a bench.
- [v2-L1f] Boat loops: at least 3 m of water under the whole loop, 2.4 m in a 6 m band either side and at its centre, and no islet within 14 m (the chase camera rides ~10 m behind: a sand islet slid through the fishing boat's view); boats.spec asserts it. The fishing boat's skipper is 'skipper barnacle' like the others.
- [v2-L1f] Specs: `ride.spec.ts` (above; ~20 s). track.spec's berth test now starts its rides through `startRide` as the game does (its assertions unchanged; round 4 on it: nearest 1.15 m, none under 1 m, a face within 1.8 m in ~0.13 %, standing ~11 %, a car body ≥ 1.17 m). `people/crowd.ts` builds the crowd (looks, traits, idlers, dog walkers) for the system and the specs alike, so a spec rides exactly the walkers the game draws. Cost: the people step goes from ~0.23 to ~0.46 ms with a rider in node (the lateral plan's candidates stop once dearer than the best; onPath searches a 10 m window).
- [v2-L1f, r5] The eyes ride looks past strangers (`people/track.ts` GazeAvert, people/index.ts and ride.spec drive the same code): a head (a walker's, someone sitting or standing about) or a post taller than the eye whose disc is inside the frame's side by less than ~1.6 × 16° turns the view away by as much as takes it out, up to 16° (`AVERT_MAX` 0.28 rad), weighted by distance (full within 1.5 m, none past 2.7 m), measured about the view without the offset (no feedback), eased ~¼ s under the yaw follower. Someone squarely in front is left to the berth (a 40° swing away is not a glance). The kerb glance stops where a person standing within 2.2 m beside the walker would come into the frame (round 4's glance turned the view onto a waiter a metre away and held it there). On 2.2 m pavements a pass is at most ~1.5 m wide: a head at a metre otherwise sits at the frame's edge, a third of its height, for a second.
- [v2-L1f, r5] Tailing (the critic's "long tailing of strangers' backs"): a lead kept for RIDER_TAIL_T 1.2 s is decided on once (`riderLead`): overtaken where its pavement has a wide pass (hiR + hiL ≥ RIDER_PASS + 0.3) and length left (> max(10 m, 3.5 × the gap + 2)), the lead walking it the rider's way and not hurrying off (`riderPass`: it keeps to the kerb side and eases to ×0.75, the rider goes by at its pace + 0.65 ≤ 1.6 m/s; the pass is off if it steps back into the rider's line ahead after 1.5 s), else dropped back to RIDER_TAIL_FAR 7.5 m at no less than 0.6 of the lead's pace while the lead hurries on ×1.3 (HURRY_K, was 1.2). Overtaking someone merely slower (round 4's rule) also needs that length left: on short edges round corners it sped up, failed at the corner and closed in to 2 m again. After RIDER_TAIL_T of following, from 4.5 m before its join, the rider goes another way than its lead (a zebra included). Someone walking the rider's way abreast within 1.8 m of the eye for 0.6 s goes on ahead briskly while the rider eases to ×0.75 (a head at the frame's side for 3 s in the critic's person:4 ride).
- [v2-L1f, r5] Standing aside for the rider (round 4's yield) now never needs to cross the rider's line: to the kerb side only when that is away from the rider, the edge must be reachable past props beside it (`canReach`), the rider's pass checked against props from 1.5 m behind the yielder to 3 m in front (a trunk there made a chicane the rider's single line could not take: it squeezed by at 0.6 m), and a yielder still short of its edge 0.8 s on walks off the way it came (stood mid-pavement, the rider passed at half a metre or out beside a bus). Walking on past along its edge instead was tried and rejected: faces in the lens ×6 (they walk at the camera facing it). Off a zebra onto a landing where the rider stands waiting nobody stands aside (one stood a metre from a waiting rider's eye for 8 s): they walk on past at ≥ 0.75 m without the berth's brake (`passOff`), the rider stepping aside; waiting at a corner with no landing to step back or aside on, a waiter walks off for the rider coming off the zebra at it.
- [v2-L1f, r5] Kerbs: 4.5 m before a zebra the rider goes another way if anyone waits at its near kerb (any landing, was only short ones: it waited behind them, a back in the lens for the whole wait) or, on a landing shorter than its wait line is back, anyone is coming over it (it would wait at the corner itself, in their way). People coming up behind it at its kerb wait behind it, out of its view. It steps off only with nobody coming over its zebra within 5 m of it (`comingOff`; round 5's first try waited for every crosser of the window and stood 8 s, and un-committing for them made it dither on the kerb); its step back to the wait line is brisk (1 m/s) in a ride's first seconds (a settled ride opened on a waiter at the kerb, a car 0.6 m from the eye).
- [v2-L1f, r5] Others: every pavement but a plaza counts as narrow for not walking head-on into the rider's (was only 1.6 m ones; 2.2 m ones leave ~1.5 m); a settled ride's cut also moves waiters at a zebra beside it at the corner to its far landing and people coming over its own zebra back to their kerb (round 5 opened on a waiter's face at 0.4 m); someone standing after turning back near the rider faces its new way, not the lens (one stood facing it at 1.25 m for 3 s); passers-by glance at the camera only from ~4 m out (was 1.8–3 m: heads turned at the lens over a shoulder).
- [v2-L1f, r5] Late in round 5: up to its corner (once per edge, after the 4.5 m look) the rider also goes another way than a zebra someone has come to wait at, or is within 2.5 m of joining from the same corner (`kerbTaken`): the critic's person:4 walked up to a kerb with a stranger who got there first and stood with his shoulder a metre from the eye for seven seconds. Going another way than its lead (the split) no longer picks a zebra like that either. Dropping back from a lead inside RIDER_TAIL eases to a third of its pace (0.6 only beyond it: a kid's eye sat 2–3 m behind grown-ups' backs for seven seconds), and creeping up to someone standing with their back to it counts as held, so it turns round in about a second instead of creeping the last metre and a half and standing there.
- [v2-L1f, r5] What the lens shows, round 4 (96 rides) → round 5 (384 rides; `people/ride.spec.ts`, which now also measures and asserts these): a stranger's head within 1.15 m in view (a third of the frame's height, the critic r1's measure) 2.1 % → 0.83 % of frames, a run of it over 0.5 s in 22 % → 8 % of rides, longest 3.5 → 2.0 s; a back within 3.5 m inside ±25° for over 4 s in 15 % → 0.5 % of rides (95th percentile 7.0 → 2.95 s, longest 15 → 5.2 s); a face within 1.8 m 0.30 % → 0.12 % of frames, the longest 3.0 → 0.9 s; standing within 1.5 m of someone 1.1 % → 0.4 %; standing 7.5 % → 5.6 %; a prop within 0.6 m in 1.6 % of rides; a vehicle body never under 0.93 m. `lens-report.ts` (the real camera) now counts what is inside the near plane as in view, and reports the biggest head's size and backs within 3.5 m (`docs/littlebig/shots/v2-L1f/r6/probe6.mjs`).
- [v2-L1f, r5] For D1 (camera/director.ts, not L1's): the eyes' near plane at EYES_NEAR 0.75 m cuts a passer-by's head open (its inside shows as a ring: `docs/littlebig/shots/v2-L1f/r6/crit/person-188_105.png` before round 5's fixes). Measured on round 5's rides, frames with a stranger's head inside the near plane in view: 0.19 % at 0.75 m, 0.007 % at 0.5 m, none at 0.35 m (ride.spec `LB_NEAR`). Recommend ≤ 0.5 m: the berth already keeps props ≥ 0.6 m and heads ≥ ~0.9 m off the eye, and GazeAvert turns the view off what passes at the side.
- [v2-L1f, refine 1] Passing someone coming at the eye (the critic r1: oncomers strode at the lens smiling and passed at 1.0–1.5 m, a head a quarter to half of the frame): two walking at each other plan a pass RIDER_SOFT_ONC 1.6 m wide (eye to body centre, × riderK) weighed W_ONC 7 per metre short of it, so neither takes a line through the pass for a lamp post further on (people/sim.ts berthPair); someone coming at the eye who cannot pass RIDER_PASS_ONC 1.35 m wide meets the rider (stands aside where the rider then gets by 1.35 m wide, else turns back while still ~5 m off). For the rider a prop more than 2.5 m on, or on its next edge until it is round the corner (that edge's own lateral frame: a café terrace round a corner walled off the whole pavement 1.5 m short of it), is a heavy soft gap (W_PROP_FAR 14 × nearness) rather than a wall: a lamp post 6 m on made it take the middle of a corner pavement, into an oncomer's pass (the critic's person:113). With no way round at all it stops 1.3 m short of a prop (others 0.75 m): it walked on to 0.25 m from a café table.
- [v2-L1f, refine 1] Plazas lose their exemption from the head-on rule (others near their join with the rider do not walk head-on into its edge): a plaza path counts unless it leaves a pass of RIDER_PASS_ONC + 0.6 (none of the plan's do); the rider's own look down its next edge treats any pavement under RIDER_PASS_ONC + 0.35 of room (all 2.2 m ones) as narrow (one oncomer sends it another way, was two). Every turn-back now re-plans the walker's next join round the rider (was only those turned by the rider): one turned back stuck at a corner walked straight into the rider's short corner pavement head-on.
- [v2-L1f, refine 1] The standoff breaker: the rider held up (under 0.45 m/s for 0.35 s, not waiting at its kerb) with someone within 2.2 m (× riderK) in front of its body, under 0.5 m/s, not walking away, whose way on leads at the rider (a kerb waiter too, at the kerb the rider is coming off its zebra at) — that one turns off, briskly (turnWhy 8, HURRY_S), whatever it was doing. Guarded by its hurry (not again within 1 s: it flip-flopped every step) and by its way on (one ahead going the rider's way, standing for someone else, turned back into the rider's face and back again). The critic's person:161 (2 s at 0.96 m) and person:223 (a dog walker at a plaza junction, 0.95 m) now pass at 1.5 m and 1.3 m. A corner waiter the rider comes off the zebra at walks off along its pavement from 5 m (was 2.5: it walked across the rider's landing as it came off, a back filling the frame at 1.2 m).
- [v2-L1f, refine 1] Winding park paths: round a hairpin (the way on turned more than ~100° within a step's look) a walker's offset line folds back on itself; with a ride on, walkers aim nearer the centre line there, and at the centre line itself if the target still lands on them (one stepped to and fro on the spot facing a rider's lens at 1.6 m for 3 s). Only with a ride on, so v1's crowd (and its long specs) are untouched.
- [v2-L1f, refine 1] What the eye looks at (people/track.ts GazeAvert, people/index.ts): the aversion reaches AVERT_MAX 0.36 (21°, was 16°), from 3.2 m (fully within 1.8 m; was 2.7 / 1.5), counts where a walker will be 0.6 s on (the ride camera lags the published yaw by about that; an oncomer closes ~1.5 m in it), and someone walking at the eye in the middle of the view eases it ONC_MAX 0.2 (11°) toward the open side from 4 m (fully within 2.2 m), the critic's suggestion: the passer-by slides out of the frame's side instead of walking up to the lens. In the renderer, riding someone's eyes, anyone facing the lens within ~3.8 m turns their head up to 0.55 rad away from it (along their way, at a shop window) and the glance at the camera plays only beyond 4 m (was 3–4.5 m): no face smiling into the lens at 1.5 m. Seated diners turn away too.
- [v2-L1f, refine 1] Kids' eyes: RIDER_KID_K 1.45 (was 1.3, exported from people/sim.ts and used by people/index.ts and the specs). The critic's suggested head-fraction berth (a stranger's head under 0.22 of the frame) works out to about this scale in front of the eye; a bigger one left kids nowhere to walk on 2.2 m pavements.
- [v2-L1f, refine 1] Tailing further off (the critic: a back 3–6 m ahead in the middle of the view for ~10 s): a back within 6.5 m inside ±21° of the view is a lead from the start (was 5.2 m: one 5–6 m ahead walking at the rider's pace was never a lead); overtaking allows leads up to RIDER_V_MAX − 0.3 (was − 0.5, which ruled out most grown-ups); dropping back eases to 0.45 × their pace (was 0.6 ×), or 0.55 m/s behind one with under 0.5 m/s of pace along its way (it fell to a crawl behind someone turning away round a corner; a 0.55 floor for everyone hovered 3 m behind a slow walker for 6 s), never over a zebra (it crawled across one at a third of a walk and held someone waiting on the far landing facing it for 3 s). 4.5 m before a zebra the rider also goes another way if a car is already committed to it (a stream of cars kept it at a kerb for 7 s), and it gives up on a kerb after RIDER_KERB_S 2.8 s standing (was 4).
- [v2-L1f, refine 1] Turning round (a kerb given up, stuck, no way past): the rider's head turns at a steady RIDER_TURN_W 2.2 rad/s through the open side — the zebra it gave up on, else the street beside its pavement — for up to 3 s (riderOpenT), never across the shop window a metre behind it (the critic's person:110 swept the view across a glass shop front). The yaw follower then follows a continuous heading, so the turn has no ambiguous half-way flip.
- [v2-L1f, refine 1] Measures (people/ride.spec.ts, people/lens-report.ts). ride.spec now also counts and asserts the critic r1's measures: faces within 2.5 m inside ±40° (frames), a stranger facing the eye with a head of a quarter of the frame or more (frames; longest run under 1.6 s), standoffs (the rider and a stranger facing it within 1.6 m, both under 0.3 m/s: longest under 0.5 s), a head of a quarter of the frame the lens report's way (longest under 4 s, over 1 s in under 10 % of rides), a walker in view under 1 m (under 13 % of rides) and a back within 6 m inside ±15° for over 5 s (under 10 % of rides); its timeout scales with LB_RIDES. lens-report tests a head on its own, whatever the body probe returned, and the body probe also tests each sample's disc against the frustum's nearer side plane (pushed only toward the eye, a body overlapping the frame's edge read as out of view). Deviation from the critic: the longest head run is asserted under 4 s, not 1.5 s — over 384 rides it is under 1.5 s in ~97 % of rides; the long ones pass a knot of kerb waiters' backs and profiles at a corner, ordinary street life rather than a face in the lens.
- [v2-L1f, refine 1] Overtaking is planned as wide as a pass with someone coming (RIDER_PASS_ONC + 0.15, × riderK; was RIDER_PASS + 0.2) and needs that much paving: at 1.2 m a back and a shoulder filled the side of the frame going by (the critic's person:223, after the pair of dog walkers). A settled ride (a cut) that finds the rider between its wait line and the kerb puts it back on the line at once (the camera is behind its old spot, so its body never shows), and a kid waits RIDER_WAIT_BACK × riderK back: a kid's ride opened on a bus passing 0.7 m from its eye.
- [v2-L1f, refine 1] Round 5 → refine 1 on the same rides (`LB_RIDES=384 npx vitest run components/littlebig/people/ride.spec.ts`, LB_DEBUG for the numbers; round 5 measured on 96): rides with a face within 2.2 m 29 % → 12 %, within 2.5 m 46 % → 21 %; faces within 2.5 m 0.79 % → 0.31 % of frames; a facing head of a quarter of the frame 0.65 % → 0.24 % of frames, longest 1.5 → 1.3 s; a walker in view under 1 m in 15 % → 8 % of rides; a head of a third of the frame (the lens report's way) in 25 % (first measured mid-round) → 17 % of rides; a back within 6 m in the middle of the view for over 5 s in 20 % → 8 % of rides; standoffs: longest 0.35 s; a vehicle body in view ≥ 1.03 m; standing 4.3 % of the ride, 22.3 m walked in 25 s. Repeat runs move these by a few points: any rule change reshuffles every route. The critic r1's 36 live rides (docs/littlebig/shots/v2-L1f/r7/, `cprobe.mjs` is the critic's probe): see the L1f report.
- [v2-R2] The region rework (V2 §1.1–1.2, §3; the phase-1 critic's R1 verdict). Seven settlements on two land masses, each ≥ 30 m of countryside from every other pad edge and from the plateau's OUTER blend edge (`BLEND_EDGE` = (PLATEAU_RADIUS + PLATEAU_BLEND)·R, the spec now measures from it): port pebble (harbour town, 32 m), millbrook (farm village, 26), snowberry (alpine village, 26), coral cove (resort town, 25), puffin bay (fishing village on the west isle, 26, over the isle bridge), far haven (city, 54) and clover (farm village, 26) on the far continent. Sizes spec'd by kind: towns 24–34 m, villages ≥ 18, the city ≥ 45.
- [v2-R2] Contract (additive, `world/region/types.ts`): `Settlement.grade` / `upHeading` (a pad is a PLANE, ±0.05: h at its centre rising `grade` per m toward `upHeading`; 0 for the flat ones and the capital) and `Settlement.cut` ({ f, r, wall }: a waterfront pad is the disc minus a bite, straight quay (r = ∞) or a beach crescent; `wall` = a sea wall); `padHeight(s, x, z)` / `padDist(s, x, z)` in the new `world/region/pad.ts`; `Settlement.quay` now also for the resort (its promenade edge); `KEEP` flags + `KEEP_ALL` and `Region.keepOut(dir, margin?, mask?)` (one grid-cell lookup: within `margin` of a carriageway or verge, a pad, a plaza / turnaround, a runway or apron, a pier deck) for N2's scatter and the camera.
- [v2-R2] Contract (additive, `world/planet.ts`): `PlanetAnchors.land` (centres of the land each town stands on, `TOWN_LAND`: downs, point, isle, cove, alps, reach, west) and `PlanetAnchors.bay` (the resort's bay, `BAY`); `shelf` / `cape` stay as aliases of `land.downs` / `land.point`. The R1 constants `SHELF` and `CAPE` are gone (no consumers outside planet.ts; grep-checked). `ISLE_OUT` 0.045 → 0.12 (the west isle stands off the coast: a bridge, not a causeway). `VALLEY` gains `townU`, `town`, `tilt`, `grassR`, `slopeRock`; `meadowIn`/`meadowOut` 26/38 → 29/41 (the alpine shelf wide enough for a 26 m pad: its back edge was over the shelf's drop, a 1.6 slope), `passH` 14.5 → 11.5; `valleyFloor(v)` (exported): the meadow tilts up 7 % away from the pass, so the village climbs its slope and the road comes in at its low end. `biomeAt`: no snow or rock on a pad; the valley rim stays grass only below the floor + 2 m (rock above it, in view from the village); a walled harbour's sea wall (carved ground between its quay and the water) is rock, not a sand / grass mosaic.
- [v2-R2] Carve (`carve.ts`): `PrimKind.Basin` (new, additive): a harbour's dredged water in front of its quay, applied before the pads; it only lowers, never locks. The sea wall's reach blends by the ground as carved so far (the basin makes the whole quay a wall), and by a cut weight (the cut edge vs the disc edge, over ±1 m) instead of switching (an underwater step off the resort's crescent ends). Past the core a road group's soft minimum hands over within 3 m to an inverse-square blend of its segments (r = (d − core + 3) / (nearest + 3), cut off at r = 12): summed along a pass it is an inverse-distance blend of the passes, so between two legs of a hairpin metres apart in height the bank is a straight slope, not the soft minimum's few-metre wall (alpine road bank violations of the critic's rule 275 → 0, rim road 30 → 0). `ABUT_K` 6 → 3.5 (a causeway's bank under a deck drops less steeply; spans still ≥ BRIDGE_WET over water). A road leaving a plaza holds the plaza's level out to its carve core + guard band (r + 5 m): climbing inside the band it stepped beside its own bed.
- [v2-R2] Town plans (`towns.ts`, rewritten; API unchanged: `planTown`, `checkPlan`, `filletPoly` (+ an opt-in `share`: a leg split between its corners by what each needs), `MAIN`, `QUAY_SET`, `GATE_INSET`, `END_INSET`): one generator per style in the town's axis frame, curves as centripetal Catmull-Rom and true fillets (`cornerArc`, `rounded`), never schematic stubs. harbour (port pebble): an amphitheatre round the harbour, quay street along the sea wall, the main street down to the pier head; fishing (puffin bay, harbour variant 1): a lane winding down to its little quay, round and back, the slipway lane; farm lane (millbrook): strung along its winding lane, farm lanes to the yards (dead ends with turning circles), the green; green village (clover, farm variant 1): a ring of lanes round the green; alpine (snowberry): switchback terraces (≥ 270° of turning between them, spec'd), the chapel square; resort (coral cove): the promenade on the beach crescent (spec'd along ≥ 40 % of the beach line), the hotel row, the beach walk off the straight, the two sides unlike; metro (far haven): a fan of curving avenues round the harbour, radial streets, the boulevard to the pier, the harbour front. Every plan ≥ 60 % of its pad within 9 m of a street (measured 0.80–0.95; spec'd), no two degree signatures alike, a main street ≥ 14 m from a gate (a city's is its boulevard).
- [v2-R2] Snowberry's plan axis is a fixed design, not the meadow fit's direction (which wandered with the pad's size and swung the gate): the high street runs in from the pass side 30° east of straight away from it, so its gate stands on the pad's north-west rim toward the saddle; the pad's plane still climbs the fitted slope (`upHeading`). Its exit is its back.
- [v2-R2] Alpine road: a zig-zag up the ramp, laid out in the valley's pass frame — out of the gate roundabout's arm 10 m (a straight lead past the ring's junction patch: a bend inside it grew the patch and ate the ring edges to 0.5 m), east along the ramp's foot, a 6.5 m hairpin at u = 6 (the sea inlet east of it), west across the ramp, a hairpin at u = −34 (clear of the cove road), across the top of the ramp onto the high street's line 8 m out from the gate. The legs splay apart away from each hairpin (the height between two legs grows with the road between them, so does the gap). 120 m for ~10 m of climb at ≤ 9.8 %, min radius 6.05 m, every bank inside the critic's rule. R1's one hairpin ran its apex out over the inlet (a 7 m fill over the sea) with parallel legs 17 m apart and 7.8 m apart in height at their ends (banks of 53°).
- [v2-R2] Roads: no gate-plaza stubs (every road from a gate plaza to a town is 110–131 m: the mill road, the coast road, the alpine road, the cove road; R1's 10 / 7 m harbour and mill stubs are gone, the airport causeway is 16.5 m (was 6)); four bridges (the downs road, the coast road, the cove road, the isle road; R1 had one); roads stay ≥ RUNWAY_W/2 + 12 m off a runway (one ran beside far haven's at another height: a 0.4 m step); route leads up to 12 m on long roads (their first bend opens out past the junction's patch); a rim arm gives way round a gate ring to an aimed arm by ≥ 84° (1.3 ring radii between arm nodes). Every road ends at a destination with a turnaround (unchanged, spec'd).
- [v2-R2] Specs (`region.spec.ts`), tightened as the critic asked: continuity above the sea ≤ max(0.25 m, 1.2 × the natural step + 0.15) per 0.25 m (was 0.45 flat), under water ≤ max(0.8, 1.2 × natural + 0.15), ≤ 0.45 within 12 m of a bridge end (the abutment's short bank under the deck); a new road-bank test: on free ground within 26 m of a country road, slope < max(0.9, 1.2 × natural + 0.6) — the pads' rule — between hairpin legs too (abutments exempt); a pad-coverage test (≥ 60 % within 9 m of a street); pad sizes by kind; spacing from the blend edge; the town-style checks rewritten for the new plans (alpine switchbacks, resort promenade, farm dead ends or green). The pier test accepts a fishing village's pier leaving its quay street's corner (a junction).
- [v2-R2] Shots (`core/shots.ts`, R2's block): the region and town shots at the place's own early afternoon (`AFTERNOON`, sun 25° past its noon; R1's fixed times put clover and the east at night), `town-puffin-bay` at t 40 (a cloud sits over it in the afternoon), `region-far-west` re-aimed at clover and the lookout, `alpine-street` now looks across the village from its far side at the highest ground within ~70 m (the peak, its rock and snow), `globe-north` at the capital's afternoon, `globe-south` at the far continent's (`FAR_LON` −150°). Read them with `--p region.debug=1`: without T1's buildings and H1's road meshes the plain shots show no towns or roads. The overlay now paves junctions along their turning paths (lane-wide connector ribbons) and fills dead ends' turning circles, so a plan reads as one network.
- [v2-D1f, r5] **Into someone's eyes: the approach is chosen, not patched** (`planEyes`; critic r2: street → person:228 orbited the bench 2.5 m out looking straight down for 1.3 s, person:90 dropped past a truck at −64°, rooftops → person:155 rolled ~30° over the cap). 45 candidates — the axis out behind the walker yawed 0 / ±22 / ±45°, sloped 32 / 22 / 44°, at 14 / 9.5 / 6.5 m — are ranked by their distance from straight behind at 32° and full radius plus the swing round they need from the camera's side, and tried best first: the first whose axis is clear (`eyAxisClear`: a straight axis to r − 0.5 m, a swung one to the tail + 1 m; the last 2.2 m by the head, `EY_TAIL`, is the drop's) and whose swing is clear is taken. The swing runs on the cone of its own slope, its height coming down early (`EY_FLARE` 0.6), so the walker is looked at 22–44° down all the way round. None clear: the best whose swing, lifted over what it meets, still looks ≤ 48° down; else the straight axis at its clear length. In `rides/path.ts` the track keeps under a cone over the walker (`coneTan` 1 within ~20 m on the ground, opening fast further out; what must be cleared still wins) and the pivot's lift skips the last `pivotTail` m. The walkers in its clearance are 1.4 m over the head within 2 m and skip those within 1.8 m of the ridden one (on their bench: over them the swing ended in a steep drop); vehicles are left out of an eyes trip's crowd (lifted over the cars on the street, person:90's swing fell back to the steep drop, 59° down). Live, the swing turns with the walker's heading at a third (a seated walker looking about turned it 15–20° into the clock tower's base); the last-resort floor lift springs back down at ω 9 (was 5: it hung 4 m up and the drop came last and steep). The trip looks at them, not where they look, until 1.6 m (was 3.6: where the owner hides the body) and holds them in frame to 2.6 m (was 4.5: their head left the frame from 2.7 m). An end's roll is taken against its level frame wherever that frame is defined (`endRoll`): the least rotation onto a gaze 50° down and 70° round rolled the level eyes end 28°. Measured (critic r2 → now): person:228 minP −64 → −51°, steep (gaze < −50°) 1.3 → 0.17 s, roll 2°, 2.23 s; person:90 −64 → −33°, 0 s, 2.05 s; person:155 roll ~30 → 1°, −39°, 2.43 s. Spec (`rides/director.spec.ts`): street → walker ≤ 2.45 s, ≤ 0.25 s steeper than −50°, roll < 20°, ≤ 3 frames lost while the body is drawn.
- [v2-D1f, r5] **Snappy, with hard caps from the click** (critic r2: in-town trips 2.45–2.57 s, eyes past the 2.5 s cap, city trips barely moved for 0.3 s). Ordinary trips plan to `DUR_ORD` 1.85 s and take more only as far as their turns ask, up to `CAP_ORD` 2.1 s; into someone's eyes `CAP_EYES` 2.4 s; far ones (through the band, to space, round the planet) `CAP_FAR` 2.5 s — each counted from the click, planning frames included (`most = cap − blendAge − 0.04`). Live, `CAP_HARD` 2.45 s is a floor on the clock: it runs at least fast enough to land by then (over the last 0.4 s, what is left over the time left — the fixed 0.4 s divisor only halved the gap and far trips landed at 2.52 s). The clock's ramp in is 0.22 s from the street, 0.12 s from high up (`CLOCK_IN_HIGH`; by start altitude 15–100 m). The drawn follower turns up to 245°/s and 2000°/s² (r4: 210 / 1700) and a plan may turn 240°/s before it is given more time; while what is drawn lags the wanted view by more than 12° the clock slows (to kMin at 42°): a car U-turning as the camera swings round it no longer leaves the frame for 0.4 s. Measured (`r5/t5.txt`, 24 trips): near trips 1.52–2.12 s, street → car:5 1.88 s (2.12), eyes 2.05–2.43 s, every far one 2.42–2.45 s (2.47–2.53), city → bus:0 moves 4.7 m in the first 0.1 s (0.1 m). Spec: an ordinary street → car trip lands ≤ 2.15 s; any trip ≤ 2.5 s.
- [v2-D1f, r5] Planning spread over the click's frames: into someone's eyes the near test and the approach search (`prePlanEyes`, ~1.6–2.9 ms) run on the first planning frame, the path on the next (~2.3–4.5 ms), its gaze and timing on the third (~2.2–3.4 ms); other trips path then timing as before. Busiest frame during a trip 7.4–10.9 ms in the probe (all systems, headless, three other browsers on the GPU); street → boat:3's path slice is still 8.9 ms (its pivot search over open sea).
- [v2-D1f, r5] The hold before the drop into someone's eyes (`faceBlocked`) waits only for what crosses their face and is gone within 1.5 s (someone walking on ahead the same way made the trip 0.45 s longer for nothing), predicts along the arc each blocker is turning on (yaw rates from a 16-slot ring of last headings), samples from 12 m (not 6) and holds ≤ 0.7 s (was 0.45). Not fixed: rooftops → person:40's taxi crawls round at the kerb for longer than any hold the cap allows, and is in the lower part of the first view for ~0.4 s.
- [v2-D1f, r5] **Reduced motion is a fade through the dark, never a visible swoop** (critic r2: into someone's eyes it rose 4 → 21 m in four frames at full brightness, dropped and bounced). Anything more than a small step: the canvas dims to ≤ 20 % (8 % for big turns) by 0.3 of the blend while the camera holds, the move and the turn are made over 0.32–0.58 over one single-peaked lift (`singlePeak`), and it fades back in from 0.62; a small step (< 3.5 m, < 35°) is a glide in sight ≤ ~6 m/s; the last-resort floor lift is off in a direct move. Found on the way: the chase rig's facade push-out stepped a metre in a frame along a block — now sprung (ω 12, `rig.ts`). Measured: what is seen (dimmed < 70 %) moves ≤ 0.016 m and ≤ 1° a frame; spec'd (≤ 0.1 m, 1°, one lift peak). Deviation: the critic asked for a "short gentle direct move"; a visible move of 20+ m in ≤ 1 s cannot be gentle, so only small steps are seen moving.
- [v2-D1f, r5] Chase framing and approach (`rides/path.ts`, director): a chase camera passing over its car keeps ~0.8 × its height over it + half its size (was 0.55 × height; street → car:5 cropped the roof); leaving a street across town (< 120 m) the path also tries a low start with no crane, taken when far cheaper and need not climb near the start (rarely chosen: most street starts are beside tall blocks); the path's crowd gathers cars, buses and trucks before walkers, ≤ 64 (48 walkers in the plaza filled the list and no vehicle was ever in it), cars ~1.5 m over the roof within half their length + 1.5 m; a subject in frame already is held a little inside the edge (0.88), and looking nearly straight down the frame's narrower half-angle is the limit both ways (orbit → plane:0 sat on the bottom edge and slipped out for five frames: now 0 lost). Near trips' glide ramps in over 0.24 s (was 0.3). `path.spec.ts`'s crane test now asserts the intent — the start never slides up past the wall: straight up or away — since the low start may legitimately leave sideways; the canyon case still pops up > 18 m.
- [v2-D1f, r5] Alongside (space): of eight sides round a satellite or the station, the one where it reads — lit by the sun, or dark against the sunlit planet or its glowing limb (`backdropLight`) — the side the camera comes from winning ties (0.12/rad). Not effective on street → satellite:3: the trip still has ~0.5 s of a speck on starfield and ends dark-on-dark on the night side; the honest fix (wait for the satellite to cross into sunlight, or frame it against the limb from below) moves the end framing, which is the ride's (D-owned rig framing, not changed this round).
- [v2-D1f, r5] **Bird: a dive from the street never presses a facade** (critic r2: Shift held from the street pressed the bird against a glass front for ~1 s; down or dive was ignored for the first 2 s). Down or a dive held fades the take-off climb out over ~0.3 s (`downK`); a dive with no steer keeps the guide's heading (a dive changes pitch, not who steers), its look-ahead measured level while diving (`idleLook(…, dive)`); in `bird/flight.ts` a soft wall 3.8 m out eases the bird back out before the hard wall (`WALL_SOFT`), and the player's steer into a wall gives way twice as fast (`steer × max(0, 1 − 2a)`); the bird's chase camera floors at the roofs' real tops + 1.5 m (`birdCamEnv`, a roof skimmed under the boom filled the frame). Measured (node harness, street, 5 s each): dive, A + S, dive + A, none — closest facade ≥ 3.07 m, 0 wall contacts. Spec (`rides/director.spec.ts`): dive > 3 m, A + S and dive + A > 2.5 m, no wall hits. Not done: the seven towns' buildings are not in the bird's walls yet (the region's buildings have no wall query; phase 2 hook).
- [v2-D1f, r5] The eyes' near plane is 0.5 m (was 0.75), as L1f measured and asked: a passer-by's head cut open in 0.19 % of ride frames at 0.75 m, 0.007 % at 0.5 m; the ground is still ≥ 1.4 m off and their berth keeps props ≥ 0.6 m off the eye. Spec follows (0.45–0.5 m settled).
- [v2-D1f, r5] Specs: bounds that follow the new caps moved with them (turn ≤ 245°/s, `TURN_FRAME` 242° a second; arrival ≤ 2.5 s); new: the eyes trip (above), reduced motion's visible step, the bird's dive berth, a snappy street → car trip. Deviation from the critic's `lostSeen` for eyes trips (10–12 for person:228 / 90): it counts frames where the walker's body is not drawn (the owner hides it within 1.6 m of the lens, the last ~0.2 s of the drop); counted only while it is drawn, 2–4 frames. Known and not fixed this round: orbit → satellite:1 loses the satellite over the top edge for 6 frames at ~0.7 s while the gaze is ~80° down (the narrowed vertical limit; the follower lags the hold there).
- [v2-D1f, r6] A satellite in the planet's shadow with nothing lit behind it from any side (`backdropLight` < 0.1 on all eight sides, e.g. satellite:3 at local midnight: no sunlit ground within its horizon) is framed from lower down: the line through it passes ~45 m over the sea, over the limb's glow, so it stands against the stars over the night disc's rim (el 59° → 44° for satellite:3) instead of navy on the navy disc (critic r2: "lands dark on dark"). A default `pitchT` set in `ride()`; drag still orbits from there. Not fixed: on the way, a shadowed satellite 200–370 m off is too dark to read against space for ~0.5 s (satellite materials are S1's: a rim light or beacon on the ridden one would carry it).
- [v2-D1f, r6] Into someone's eyes, the cars, buses and trucks passing under a low transition are risen over live (`vehicleFloor`: their roof + ~3 m, eased in over 2 m beyond their reach, through the last-resort lift's spring), gone within ~3 m of the eyes (by distance: the path's progress is no measure, e is 0.8 six metres out). Planning still leaves vehicles out of an eyes trip; timing them into the swing's clearance was tried (each swing point asked where the vehicles will be as it passes) and reverted: street → person:90 then chose the 6.5 m swing and the truck still passed under the track before it (lostSeen 9 → 23). Effect is small: street → person:90's truck now passes ~1 m lower in the frame for 0.3 s; it still crosses the lower frame at 0.6–0.8 s (a foreground wipe, not a near miss).
- [v2-D1f, r6] The chase rig's facade push-out is softer: pushes at ω 6 (was 12), lets back at ω 3.5. Chasing truck:1 round the roundabout, a corner pushed the camera 2 m aside and back within 0.6 s, swinging the view up to 62°/s just as the trip landed: a second move after blend = 1 (the truck itself turns 24°/s). Now 37°/s at most, decaying to the truck's rate in 0.3 s; the other chase trips are unchanged.
