- [v2-D1f, r6] Reduced motion: a move shorter than 40 m with no building on the straight line between its ends has no lift at all (`blendLift.hop(0)`): the clearance round the start (a facade a metre beside the street camera) lifted street → person:112's 8 m move to 19 m and back inside the dim (critic r2's altitude probe: 3.9 → 6.5 → 19.2 → 9.5 → 3.9), faintly seen at 20 %. Now 3.90 → 3.73 m, monotonic. Blocked moves keep the single-peaked lift.
- [v2-D1f, r6] Specs (`rides/director.spec.ts`, both fail without their fix): reduced motion's short clear move to a walker 8–10 m along the pavement never rises more than 0.3 m over its ends (was 20 m); a satellite at local midnight is framed with the line through it ≥ 25 m over the sea and the planet still in the lower frame (was −13 m: on the disc). Review getters: `director.dip` (how dimmed reduced motion's dip has the canvas, 0 … 1) and `PT.path` (the path's own share of the planning slice). Disagreement kept from r5: the critic's `lostSeen` for eyes trips counts the walker's feet leaving the frame in the last ~0.2 s, when the lens is within 1.6 m of the eye and the owner hides the body; counted while the body is drawn it is 2–4 frames (person:228 / 90).
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **What the refine round answered** (critic 7.5/10). Majors: organic plans, junction patches as street corners, far haven as a warped fan, waterfronts with a published sea wall, globe-135 holding a settlement. Minors: build time, the keep-out spec, blend-to-blend spacing, the orbit aim, snowberry's banks, the 96-ray pad spec, coral cove's beach end, bridge abutments, a second ferry, the region-far-west aim, the gate shots' timing. Evidence: `docs/littlebig/shots/v2-R2/final/` (plain), `final/debug/` (`--p region.debug=true`), `final/v1/` and `final/dive/`.
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **An eighth town: driftwood** (fishing village, harbour style variant 2, padR 26, on the far continent's west lobe; `TOWNS` in `build.ts`). It answers the critic's empty globe-135: that side held only a road to a lookout. Its plan is the bay village (`towns.ts` `bay`): a strand street along its sea wall, a boatyard corner, the pier, and a lane up to the west road. Spec'd (`region.spec.ts`, globe test): from 380 m over the equator every 45° and over both poles, a settlement pad's nearest point is face-on (> 0.5). On every equator bearing a town centre is face-on too, so the capital's rim alone never answers. Spacing: the critic's blend-to-blend rule is spec'd. Every town's blend ring is ≥ 20 m from every other ring and from the plateau's outer blend edge; measured minimum 22.6 m (port pebble / millbrook). `TOWN_LAND` changes: `point` at 173 m, `isle` at 282 m, and coral cove's island filled out behind its pad by a `cove` blob (69.5°, 201 m, r 0.085). The pad no longer draws the island's coastline.
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **Three ferries** (port pebble ⇄ far haven, driftwood ⇄ coral cove, puffin bay ⇄ far haven). Each runs over ≥ FERRY_DEPTH water away from its berths. Every pier has a boat, spec'd. Shot: `ferry-cove`.
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **Plan machinery** (`towns.ts`, `geo.ts`). Streets are biarc Hermite chains between nodes, held by straight guides: a node's arms stay straight for their guide length `g`, so its junction patch stays small. A dead end's lead is searched from min(6.6, 0.75·d) m down until the arc beyond it is ≥ 6.4 m radius. `fairLine` replaces R2's box filter. It runs Laplacian sweeps over only the tight stretches: the circumradius is tested root-free, and after the first sweep only the dirty range is re-swept. It then runs 240 fourth-difference passes (μ 0.1) over the free samples within ~2 windows of anything moved, which keeps a faired curve tangent-continuous where it meets a pinned straight; R2's sweep left kinks there. planTown for all eight towns costs ~2 ms in node (was 8 ms with the first fairing).
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **Junctions are street corners.** Every non-roundabout junction patch is ≤ 0.6 × its widest carriageway + 2 m, and ≥ 6 m of street lies between two junctions (spec'd for every town; patches measured 0.96–1.00 of the cap, shortest street between junctions 6.5 m). The binding case was a right turn of more than 90° whose arms curve inside the patch. Fixes, per town: guides (`g`) on the arms; tangents square to the radials in far haven's market street (`arcT`); the farm lane's second junction turned (`tJ2` (−0.65, 0.76)) with its dead end at (18, −4.5) and the orchard lane 3.5 m off the main tangent; puffin bay's cliff lane guided 11·k m; driftwood's corner node `auto` with a 3.5 m guide, the quay node squared (`t` [1, 0], g 6), the boatyard radius 7. A global junction guide was tried and made more towns fail: reverted.
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **Far haven is a warped fan.** The avenues arc round the harbour (two ≤ 70 m radius, spec'd), the park row and the avenues are guided clear of each other (`GA` 6.5, `GK` 6), the east avenue ends at 38.8°, and the airport road's guide and lead no longer overlap (`runK`). Blocks taper toward the docks: 8 blocks, area coefficient of variation 0.26 (spec ≥ 0.25, as the critic asked).
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **Sea walls are published for H1** (contract, additive: `Settlement.wall?: QuayWall`, constants `WALL_FOOT` −1.6, `WALL_COPING` 0.6, `WALL_LIP` 0.22, `QUAY_APRON` 3.0, `WALL_BAND` 4.5; `world/region/types.ts`). Each wall has its line (plan and unit dirs), deck height per vertex, foot, coping and apron, and the face's seaward normal. The apron behind the face is classified `'plaza'` by `surface()` and carries the plaza bit in `keepOut`, so nothing grows on the quay. `pad.ts` gains `cutDist(s, x, z)` (signed distance to the cut line, positive seaward). `biomeAt` dresses the ground stone ±4.5 m either side of every wall from WALL_FOOT − 0.6 to 2.6 m (`seaWallBand` in `planet.ts`). One rock band on both sides, because grass on the land side rendered as green teeth against the facet colouring (each facet takes its corners' majority biome); R2 had sand only on the sea side. The debug overlay draws the face, the coping and the apron. Walled towns: port pebble, far haven, puffin bay, driftwood. Shots: `quay-port-pebble`, `quay-far-haven` (re-aimed: 22 m off, 24 m up, pitch −30°, so the wall and the quay street read instead of sky), `quay-puffin-bay`, `quay-driftwood`.
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **Bridge abutments** (contract, additive: `Bridge.abutments: [BridgeAbutment, BridgeAbutment]`, constants `ABUT_WING` 10, `ABUT_DEPTH` 3.5, `ABUT_BACK` 9). Each box is the face across the road at the span's end, wings `width/2 + 1.8 + 10` m either side, 3.5 m into the span, 9 m back along the approach, from the lowest final ground in the box (`foot`, filled on the network's first read from the carve) up to the deck. H1 clads it as stone. The specs' blanket 12 m exemption at bridge ends is gone. The bank, continuity and pad tests now exempt only the declared boxes (`inAbutment`), and a new test requires every bank steeper than the rule within reach of a bridge to lie inside one. The overlay outlines them.
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **keepOut**: `margin` is clamped to `KEEP_MARGIN_MAX` (8 m, contract constant). The grid's keep-out buckets are built lazily on first call, each primitive's reach being its own reach or core + 8.5 m. `Carve.keepOutAll` / `Region.keepOutAll` (non-enumerable) is a brute-force pass over every primitive, for the spec. Spec'd on 20 000 points: agreement with `surface()` at margin 0, agreement with brute force for margins 0…8 (larger ones clamped), and ≤ 1 µs per call.
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **Deviation from the critic: snowberry's banks get a narrower fill, not a wider blend.** The critic suggested widening snowberry's blend. With blend 8.5 m, the 96-ray pad test found 40–80 bank violations where the pad's fill over the top of the pass ramp was carried out past the slope's foot. A cap on the fill's widening, `ALPINE_FILL_CAP` 1.5 m (`DiscOpts.fillCap`; the cap used is min(own, global)), makes the fill follow the ground down closely: 0 violations at the original blend 5. Lifting the cap over all land (`FILL_LAND`) was tried and reverted: it stepped at the bucket reach edge. The pad test now casts 96 rays (was 48); declared abutment boxes are exempt (the 96 rays found puffin bay's abutment face).
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **The default orbit shot is re-aimed**, as the critic asked: 380 m over (CITY_LAT − 16°, CITY_LON − 21°) at t = 468. The capital is face-on in its afternoon (facing > 0.75, sun > 30°), with port pebble and millbrook on the western lands under no cloud and the dusk on the right limb. Spec'd: two towns face-on > 0.5, sun > 12°, cloud-clear. This is the v1 `orbit` named shot, and the only v1 shot that changes; the other nine diff ≤ 0.21 % of pixels from the round's baseline. Other shots: `region-far-west` mixes clover (0.6) with driftwood and is timed by clover's own longitude; the gate shots are at the capital's afternoon (`CAPITAL_AFTERNOON`), since at t = 0 their plazas were in late light; `town-clover` is at its own noon (it lies far south, and an afternoon sun raked it dark); new shots `town-driftwood`, `quay-driftwood`, `quay-puffin-bay` (t 40), `ferry-cove`. Coral cove's beach end: the resort's stray promenade point at (13.2, −11.6) is gone, and its approach and promenade-back guides are 6 m.
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **Build time: 15.2–16.0 ms cold in the browser** (budget 20, critic's aim ≤ 15). Town streets are lazy. The first build adds each town's nodes from a baked plan skeleton (`planSkeleton` / `skeletonOf`: nodes, exits, square, quay, pier, exit backs, links; cm16). Its streets are planned and registered on the network's first read (`townStreets()`, called from `network()` or a settlement's `streets` getter); each street's world path is built on first use. In development a skeleton that disagrees with its plan warns: a stale bake. Interior town nodes are kept by `townLinks` in the pruning and the union-find. Road finishing is split: `routeSmooth` (strip-frame smoothing and fairing, baked: every other sample, rebuilt by the 4-point scheme, which is interpolating and keeps a bend's radius; linear midpoints broke the 3.5 m lane-radius spec by 5 mm) and `routeLift` (to the sphere, live). The grade profile is baked the same way (mm16). Every-fourth decimation was tried and reverted: rim road radius 2.4 m. Road carve segments are 1.5 m over the first and last 9 m (where the profile leaves a pad's level), 3 m between.
- [v2-R2, refine 1] **Bake format 2** (`bake.ts`). Base64 of raw int16s hardly compressed (18.8 KB → 11.5 KB gzip). The fixed-point entries now go to a text stream after a '.': per entry the shortest of difference orders 1–3 at strides 1–3, as zigzag varints of 5 bits per character. baked.ts went from 16.3 KB / 11.3 KB gzip to 10.0 KB / 6.4 KB gzip, with the finish still baked. Fixed on the way: a fixed-point value rounding to −0 read back as +0 and broke the chain hash on replay (`through()` adds `+ 0`). Regenerate with `LB_REGION_BAKE=1 pnpm vitest run components/littlebig/world/region/bake.spec.ts`.
- [v2-L1f, refine 2] **Tailing knots of backs** (critic r2, blocker: three of the landing shot's nearest walkers tailed groups 3–5 m ahead for 7–14 s). Three rules in people/sim.ts. (1) `riderPick`: the rider's way on at each join is chosen by what it would put in front of the eye, not v1's weighted dice: strangers' backs on that edge's first 12 m or about to turn into it just ahead (×3), people coming down it (×2, ×4 on a narrow pavement), a zebra whose landing is shorter than the wait line (+6: it would wait at the corner, cars under a metre from the eye), waiters at its kerb (+5), a car committed (+4), a short landing with someone coming over (+3), a path that cannot keep the tall-prop clearance (+3), plus a seeded die up to 1.5 so a quiet town still wanders. It replaces the 4.5 m look's separate checks, the `kerbTaken` dodge and the lead split; those pass the edge to avoid, which costs +5 rather than being ruled out (ruled out, the only way left was sometimes a blocked short zebra). (2) The backs rule (`RIDER_BACK_*`): a stranger's back within 5.2 m (× kid scale) inside ±30° of where the camera looks, for over 0.6 s, unless the rider's planned line already passes the nearest one wide (RIDER_PASS_ONC − 0.35 standing, − 0.25 walking): everyone walking away in the middle of the view within 9 m hurries on (a group as one) and the rider eases to half their pace (≥ 0.45 m/s; ≥ 0.3 inside 3 m); someone standing or barely getting on (< 0.7 m/s) within 4 m: the rider stops 3 m short, asks them to make way, and after 0.8 s standing turns round (turnWhy 9); after 3 s of the same back not dropping away (the gap opening < 0.25 m/s) it turns round: the critic's "cap following at about 3 s". (3) `startDir`: a ride starts walking whichever way along its pavement shows the calmer view (backs ahead weigh 3, standing 2.5, faces 1.2), and turns round from a kerb where it would wait behind someone. The walker turns as the camera flies in (on a cut, at once; `clearRide` snaps the heading). It never turns round with anyone within 2.5 m behind it.
- [v2-L1f, refine 2] **Arm's length** (critic r2, major: oncomers passing at angles at ~1 m, a kerb waiter dead centre at 0.7 m, person:90's waiter crossing the rider's path face first at 0.7 m). Crossing paths now take turns (berthPair, `RIDER_CROSS` 1.5 m × kid scale): when two walkers' courses cross within 2.5 s (velocities, or a nominal 0.3 m/s along the way for one standing), whoever reaches the crossing point first goes and the other stops 1.5 m short of it. The rider yields only to someone already moving, and for at most 3 s, then has a 3 s cooldown (in the first try two stood waiting for each other for 16 s). "First" also needs the rider to be 0.8 × that clear of the point by the time the other has passed: otherwise a slowing rider handed the turn to the waiter, who walked across its face. Someone coming over the rider's zebra at the rider waiting at its kerb passes on the far side of the strip, as wide as two walking at each other (soft RIDER_SOFT_ONC × kid scale). Waiters within 6 m (× kid scale) of the rider step onto their zebra as soon as no car holds it, walk window or not, as the rider does (they stood in a knot at the corner it walked round, their backs in the middle of its view for 5–6 s), except onto the rider's own zebra coming at it or ahead of it. Someone coming at the eye with the rider on another edge (round a corner, where the path frame is a guess) meets it by the closer of the plan's pass and the courses' closest approach: one coming round a corner at a rider standing by it planned a pass the courses never made and turned back at a metre, its face filling the frame (live person:12: 19 frames of a head over a third of the frame → 11, faces within 2.2 m 4 → 0). The berth's path coordinates (`onPath`) put someone walking away beyond the end of the rider's edge off its path by their distance from the end: the end's lateral of nought had the rider stop dead behind someone walking away down the other street (live person:213). This applies only to someone walking away. Applied to everyone, people standing or coming round that corner were no longer steered round and passed at 1.2 m (rides with a walker in view under 1 m: 4.4 % → 6.8 % over 768).
- [v2-L1f, refine 2] **A ride's cut, fixed where it put people in the lens** (found on new ride sets, LB_OFF=3 and 4: a walker 0.02–0.3 m from the eye for up to 0.8 s at the start). The cut's `stepOut` used to leave someone it could find no clear spot for wherever its search ended; once that was 0.3 m in front of the lens of a ride that had just turned round. Now: the best spot on its own edge (out of view first, then furthest from the eye), and if its next edge is a zebra, its far landing (already across). v1's cuts keep their old behaviour. Everyone in the cut's zone on the rider's zebra goes across while the rider waits (one setting off behind the waiting rider passed it at half a metre). Someone on a zebra beside the rider's corner goes to whichever end is out of view: its far landing can be the rider's own corner (one put there walked off it 0.4 m from the eye). Nearest walker in view over 1536 rides: 0.02 m → 0.46 m.
- [v2-L1f, refine 2] **Building front-corner columns** (critic r2, major: the rider passed the 0.4 m posts under the overhang at 0.7–1.0 m, a grey pillar a third of the frame, person:133/186/116/213). `columnObstacles` (exported, people/sim.ts) re-derives the posts that city/buildings.ts `block()` puts at (±hw, −hd + 0.2) of a building's first tier (towers, offices, midrises; not houses, shops, the café or landmarks) and adds them to the obstacles after the street furniture (`nFeat` ≤ column < `nProps`). Only the rider avoids them (its tall-prop clearance, soft). Everyone else ignores them as before: they stand inside the footprint, which no walker's body enters, and checking 2× the props per step for 230 walkers would buy nothing. Re-derived rather than read from city's meshes because the posts are not published (city/ is not L1's); if block()'s layout changes, this function must follow, and its comment says so. Measured: no column within 1.34 m of the eye in 1536 offline rides. The critic's person:133 column was 1.9 m away live, not the estimated 0.7–1.0 m (`docs/littlebig/shots/v2-L1f/r8/colprobe.mjs`).
- [v2-L1f, refine 2] Minors. Kerb stand-stills (standing over 2.5 s: 15.6 % → 8 % of rides) come down mostly through `riderPick` (short landings, waiters) and the crossing yield's cap and cooldown. Kids' crowding uses the same rules at kid scale (backs out to 5.2 × (1 + 0.45 × 0.35) m). The renderer's head turn is now `headLook` in people/track.ts (moved out of index.ts, behaviour identical), so the specs and the lens report test the head as drawn.
- [v2-L1f, refine 2] **Measures brought up to date** (critic r2). ride.spec now has: the near plane at 0.5 m (D1's EYES_NEAR); a stranger's back within 5 m inside ±30° (`tail5Runs`, over 5 s), two or more of them (`group5Runs`, over 3 s), and within 3 m (`back3Runs`); a face the lens can see, its head turned by headLook within ±70° of the eye, within 2.5 m (`faceVis`) and 1.5 m (`faceVis15`); standing still (`stillRuns`); columns as their own category; and `LB_OFF`, which shifts the ride set (other walkers and hours) so a change is judged on more than one set. Bars tightened: a walker under 1 m in < 7 % of the 48 rides (was 13 %; the critic's 5 % holds over 768 rides: 4.3 %), the longest head of a quarter of the frame < 3 s (was 4), over 1 s in < 6 % of rides (was 10 %). lens-report.ts: `face` is the visible-face test, `tail` the ±30°/5 m window and `tailN` its count (so the critic's probe's `faces` now counts visible faces within 2.5 m, more than before). **Deviation**: the critic asked for the longest head run at about 2 s; it is asserted < 3 s (48 rides: 1.7 s; over 1536: longest 3.9 s, over 1 s in 3.3 % of rides; the long ones pass someone standing at a kerb). A rate bar on 48 rides allows a ride or two (2 % steps), so the bars are guards; the many-ride numbers are in the spec's comment.
- [v2-L1f, refine 2] **Deviation: no consolidation into one "visibility cost"** (critic r2, maintainability). The berth's rules (lateral intervals, brakes, crossing turns, backs, starts, route pick) answer different questions over different horizons (the next 0.5 s, the next 3 s, the next join). A single cost function would have to be tuned against all of them at once, and the sim is chaotic: any rule change reshuffles every route, so the 384-ride sets differ by ±1 % between offsets on the same code. Instead, each rule is one block with its own constant group and a comment naming the case that motivated it, and the measures that guard it are in ride.spec. riderPick did replace three separate route checks.
- [v2-L1f, refine 2] **Numbers.** Refine 1 → refine 2 over 768 offline rides (`LB_RIDES=384 LB_OFF=1|2 LB_DEBUG=1 npx vitest run components/littlebig/people/ride.spec.ts`): a back within 5 m inside ±30° for over 5 s 7.7 % → 1.4 % of rides (over 1536 rides, sets 1–4: 2.1 %, longest 7.0 s, was 13.2); two or more for over 3 s 3.8 % → 0.65 %; a walker in view under 1 m 9.5 % → 4.3 %; a head a third of the frame 19.5 % → 13.5 %; visible faces 0.66 % → 0.54 % of frames, within 1.5 m 0.11 % → 0.06 %; a face within 2.2 m in 7.2 % → 5.1 % of rides; standing over 2.5 s 15.6 % → 7.9 %; the rider's U-turns 140 → 131; a car body in view ≥ 0.90 m. The critic's 56 live rides (its street/landing/eyes sets and probe, `docs/littlebig/shots/v2-L1f/final/t4-*.log` against `critic-r2/t-*.log`): tailing over 5 s 4 → 1 ride (longest 8.7 → 6.2 s), groups over 3 s 2 → 1 (longest 6.5 → 3.8 s), a walker in view under 1 m 5 → 3 rides (but under 1.2 m 5 → 8), rides with a head over a third of the frame 9 → 8 (frames 60 → 40), faces within 2.2 m 2 → 0 rides, a tall prop within 0.8 m 4 → 3, standing over 2 s 5 → 1; camera U-turns 5 → 9 (the backs rule's turn-rounds). Frame times unchanged (1280×800 high, best net: street 2.7, city 4.0, rooftops 3.2, orbit 3.6, riding person:2 2.8, person:110 2.7, bus:0 2.6 ms). People step in node: median 0.11 ms either way; 95th percentile 1.0 → 1.1–1.4 ms (the berth's extra checks on the rider's busy steps).
- [v2-L1f, refine 2] Late in the round, from re-shooting the critic's sets. A ride that turns round as the camera flies in (`startDir`, live) turns through the street side of its pavement (`riderOpenT`, as a turn-round later does): the shortest way swept the view across a glass front and landed on it a few centimetres off (street person:186). On a pavement beside a carriageway the rider's preferred line is halfway between the kerb band and the shop fronts, up to 0.6 m off them (was 0.15 m: a glass front at the side of the frame for the length of a block); offline neutral (1536 rides), columns further off (nearest 1.18 → 1.34 m). Still seen: round a building's corner the view passes within half a metre of the facade (person:186's second block, frames 120–180), where the pavement's edges hug the building and no line keeps off it.
- [v2-R2, refine 2] **What refine round 2 answered** (critic 8/10). Majors: the sea walls against the rendered terrain mesh, plans that were thin and alike, the bundle. Minors: lane turning circles, spec margins, the orbit shot, the coral cove promenade, clouds in named shots, the wall band's ends. Deviations are listed at the end of this round's entries.
- [v2-R2, refine 2] **A second order of lanes in every plan** (`towns.ts`). Port pebble's terraces: church lane runs straight from the high street through fore street's end, square to both (no S between two junctions 17 m apart); fore street and the steps are lanes; market hill drops straight from the pier head to the market cross; harbour hill sweeps down the east side round an 8 m bend. Puffin bay: the village lane through its crossing, with three alleys (chapel row round onto the quay, cliff lane, rope walk). Driftwood: back lane between the two gate streets, the strand, net loft lane. Coral cove: the promenade, the hotel row, the beach walk (now a lane), villa lane. Clover's green: its oval is no longer pinned, so the fairing keeps 1 m curvature smooth for the traffic lanes. Snowberry's chalet lane runs straight ≥ 6.5 m out of the crossing and ≥ 5 m into its turning circle, since a lane curving into its dead end swells the circle (it had measured 0.9 m between patches). Every junction's arms run straight and square for `GJ` = 6 m. Counts: lanes 4 / 3 / 2 / 2 / 3 / 2 / 2 (port pebble, puffin bay, driftwood, coral cove, clover, snowberry, millbrook); nodes 5–7 (far haven 20).
- [v2-R2, refine 2] **Plan specs** (`region.spec.ts`): ≥ 6 nodes in a town, ≥ 5 in a village; street length between patches ≥ 1.6 × padR × π/2 (measured 69–337 m against 65–136 m); ≥ 2 lanes (≥ 4 at port pebble); every dead-end lane ≥ 6 m between its patches (shortest 6.75); a lane centreline radius ≥ 4.8 m (its traffic lanes then clear 3.5 m); coral cove's promenade within 4.5 m of ≥ 70 % of the crescent (0.80). No two towns alike, a new test on top of the 6 m plan distance: each plan's signature is its street headings (12 bins over 180°, by length) plus its turning (heading change per 2 m, 5 bins). L1 distance between signatures, taking the best turn (15° steps) and flip, must be ≥ 0.4; the closest pair measures 0.48. `checkPlan` measures a lane's curvature 1 m apart (region.spec's lane sampling), so it caught the pinned-oval and S-curve kinks the 2 m test missed.
- [v2-R2, refine 2] **Yards** (contract, additive: `Settlement.yards?: Yard[]`, `Yard { node, kind: YardKind, x, z, r }`, `YardKind` 'farm' | 'boat' | 'chalet' | 'villa' | 'market' | 'school' | 'lookout'; `world/region/types.ts`). Each yard sits at a dead end, and its circle covers the turning circle there, for T1 to build on. Spec'd: every yard at an end node of its town, r ≥ the node's turnR; a farm village has ≥ 1 farm yard (clover's second gate replaced its second dead end). A lane's dead end turns in `LANE_TURN_R` 4.6 m (network.ts; was a street's 5.6). Plan constants: `PROM_SET` 4.2 (the promenade's set-back from the beach), `END_INSET_LANE` 6.2.
- [v2-R2, refine 2] **Sea walls stand in front of the terrain mesh** (contract, additive: `QuayWall.depth`, constants `WALL_DEPTH` 3.5 and `WALL_LOW` −0.8; `WALL_FOOT` is now −2.3, was −1.6). The wall is a solid block from its face to `depth` inland. Under it the carve ramps the pad down from its plane at the block's back to `WALL_LOW` at the face (`DiscOpts.wallRamp` in carve.ts; classification still answers to the face). The harbour basin's core starts at the face, at −2.2 m (was −1.7 from 0.4 m out), so a coarse facet from the deck behind the block to the seabed crosses the face under water. Spec'd by rasterising every terrain facet within 15 m of each wall at detail 6 and detail 5, exactly (a facet is planar in height, so its highest point past the face is a corner of the facet clipped to the strip 0.1–8 m out, along the line short of its last 1.5 m): no point above −0.2 m. Worst measured: −0.81 m at detail 6, −0.42 m at detail 5. Also spec'd: under the block the ground is never above the pad's plane, and it is below the sea at the face. The debug overlay draws the block's deck to its depth and its end faces. Not done as the critic sketched (the terrain sunk to −0.5 over the whole 3.5 m band): mesh interpolation would then leave a trench up to ~2 m deep behind the block, up to a facet wide. The ramp keeps that dip ≤ ~0.4 m at detail 6.
- [v2-R2, refine 2] **The wall's stone band** (planet.ts `seaWallBand`) now runs from `WALL_BAND` seaward to the block's back inland, from the line's first point to its last. It narrows to nothing over `WALL_TAPER` 3 m past either end; it used to end square on a circle round the pad, a serrated stub in the grass. A long edge of the band still steps facet by facet (a vertex takes one biome). H1's block covers it.
- [v2-R2, refine 2] **Bundle: the searches are compiled out of production.** Every costly memo compute in build.ts is written `process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production' ? null : () => …`. That covers site, airport, summit, lookout and pier searches, the plan skeletons, A* routes, route smoothing, profiles, grades and carve dcaps. `Memo.get` with a null compute replays the baked entry by its key alone, and throws if there is none. Production only ever builds `SEED`, and its bake is checked against a fresh build by bake.spec. The ferries' A* is memoised on first read: `ferry:a-b`, outside the build's chain; bake.spec reads them before dumping and checks they replay with 0 misses. Also compiled out: the bake writer (`dump`), `keepOutAll` and the plan debug path (`checkPlan`). Measured with rolldown (minified, three external, NODE_ENV production): engine 335.4 → 330.0 KB gzip; buildRegion and its world dependencies alone 46.9 KB gzip, against 33.3 at HEAD. route.ts 15.9 → 8.7 KB and build.ts 85 → 71 KB, both before minifying.
- [v2-R2, refine 2] **Roads into three gates got longer leads**, because their corner's rounding reached the pinned end and left a hook. The coast road is 16 m into port pebble's west gate (it had a 1.8 m radius 6 m out). The cove road is 15 m into coral cove's gate (2.8 m). The lookout lane is 8 m off driftwood's west gate (5.2 m). A bridge span stays `max(10.5, 7 + (DECK_MIN + 0.1 − h_end) / MAX_GRADE)` m off a low end: the cove road's deck landed 10.5 m short of the resort's 1.85 m gate and could only climb to 2.3 m; it now measures 2.51.
- [v2-R2, refine 2] **A causeway over the sea is rock** (planet.ts `biomeAt`: verge or free ground within 16 m of a road, under 2.2 m, over natural seabed below −0.4 m). The lighthouse had moved 170 m onto the cove road's causeway spit (nature/landmarks.ts picks the grassy or sandy spot with the most sea round it), and now stands back on its headland.
- [v2-R2, refine 2] **Shots.** The orbit is 380 m over (CITY_LAT − 18°, CITY_LON − 25°), 4° west and 2° south of refine 1, with the western towns nearer the middle of the disc. Spec'd: capital facing > 0.7, two towns facing > 0.6 (was 0.5), sunlit and cloud-clear. A new spec, 'clear of cloud', resolves every town-, quay-, beach-, region- and globe- shot through `SHOTS`. Each shot's line of sight to its own town (or, from 240–380 m up, to every town it holds face-on) clears the cloud layer at the shot's time. To pass it, globe-270 moved to hour angle 38 and globe-315 to 40, globe-180 to its morning (−20) 8° east, and region-west to hour angle 40. The town shots were clear. No `orbit-west`: globe-315 is that view. Far haven's blocks: market street r2 = r1 + 18.6 and park row r3 = r2 + 25 (block-area CoV 0.32; its boulevard and east avenue had 5.8–5.9 m between junction patches).
- [v2-R2, refine 2] **Deviations from the critic.** (1) Patches ≤ 0.9 of the cap: not met. A right-angle T of 5 m streets needs √((RHO_TOWN + 1.25)² + 1.25²) ≈ 4.96 m against the cap's 5.0, so 0.99 is the floor without slackening RHO_TOWN (measured max 0.986). A crossing of lanes alone needs ≈ 4.78 m, over 0.6 × 4.4 + 2 = 4.64, so its cap is 4.85. (2) 4–6 lanes a town: only port pebble has 4. Coral cove has 2 (plus the promenade): on a 28 m pad with ≥ 6 m between junction patches there is no room for more junctions, and a dead-end lane needs ~23 m to keep 6 m between its patches. (3) Dead-end lanes ≥ 12 m: specced at ≥ 6 m (shortest 6.75). 12 m needs ~29 m of lane on a 26 m pad. (4) Bake the town polylines: not a size win. The generators gzip to 11 KB; the 4,890 street samples would cost about as much at 1 cm, and 1 cm quantisation puts curvature noise on a 4.8 m lane bend that breaks the 3.5 m traffic-lane spec. (5) Cold build ≤ 12 ms: not reached, 14.6–17.8 ms on the shared machine (budget 20). The carve's CSR bucketing is ~4.5 ms of it, mostly cold JIT (1.1 ms warm). Baking its ~20 k pairs would cost more bytes than the time is worth. (6) The dusk walker: world/city/views.ts belongs to another builder, so it is untouched. (7) The north islet: not built.
- [v2-R2, refine 2] **Coral cove** is a 28 m pad (was 25). Its promenade hugs the crescent: pinned points on the arc `PROM_SET` 4.2 m behind the beach, its ends turning onto the arc at ±16.5 m round 6.8 m fillets tangent to it, and the lanes up the town's edges are tangents from the hotel row's ends to those fillets. Its quay (the crescent) is clipped where the bite meets the pad's rim; the old chord estimate ran it off the pad. `REGION_VERSION` 5.

## Review follow-up (2026-10-09): lifecycle and responsibility boundaries

- `core/frame-events.ts` owns presentation subscriptions; the engine presents before notifying
  HUD/stick listeners. Overlays no longer keep separate animation loops alive during suspension.
- `core/session.ts` defines an engine-free, versioned handoff/recovery payload. A ride carries its
  target ID and framing; a bird carries flight pose, speed, steering state and camera distance.
  Restoration waits for stage-two trackables; an unavailable target keeps the captured explore
  viewpoint. This is an in-memory session transfer, not a persistent save format.
- `camera/clearance.ts` owns static-world spatial clearance and its scratch/grid storage;
  `camera/picking.ts` owns hover/pick hysteresis. The director retains mode transitions and
  camera orchestration. `people/path.ts` owns path sampling; `people/lens.ts` owns camera avoidance
  state. Their public compatibility exports preserve existing callers and deterministic tests.
- Touch controls retain the POP styling, show the active mode, allow details to collapse, and
  use 44px action targets. Below 380px height the dock uses two columns. Page chrome accounts
  for safe-area insets without changing the projected world-label coordinate system.
